                    CHIRAL KOSZUL DUALITY
  NONCOMMUTATIVE FACTORISATION D-MODULES,
ORDERED QUANTUM FIELDS, AND THE GEOMETRY OF
                 DEFECTS
  E1 -algebras in factorisable D-modules on ordered chiral configurations

                                        RAEEZ LORGAT

                                             July 2026


                                              Abstract
    Let X be a smooth complex curve. The primitive object of this paper is an augmented associative
 algebra
                                 A ∈ Algaug
                                        E1 FactD(X), ⊗ , 1
                                                          pt
                                                               

 in factorisable right D-modules. The factorisation coefficient records the complete chiral singularity
 on X: every principal part, every regular product, and their covariance under coordinate change.
 The internal E1 multiplication records ordered fusion along a transverse oriented line. Its analytic
 realization is a holomorphic–topological factorisation algebra on X an × R.
     The Ran category carries pointwise tensor product and disjoint-union chiral convolution. Beck–
 Chevalley base change supplies their interchange, so the relevant factorisation category is duoidal.
 An ordered chiral algebra is a bimonoid: its pointwise associative multiplication is a morphism
 of chiral factorisation coalgebras. The ordered bar therefore carries two compatible coproducts,
 deconcatenation in the interval direction and factorisation in the curve direction.
     The geometric carrier is the Grothendieck construction of the associative operad with the
 chiral configuration category. Its objects are ordered sequences of finite chiral configurations. A
 two-coloured Boardman–Vogt resolution is indexed by planar levelled forests. Chiral edges resolve
 collisions in X; transverse edges resolve associative composition. Codimension-two faces give the
 chiral relation, the associative relation, and their interchange. Orientation of the two determinant
 lines makes the corresponding edge differentials anticommute.
     The interval bar is the simplicial derived tensor product
                                                                     Z
                     BarX (A) = [r] 7−→ 1 ⊗ Ā ⊗ 1 ≃ 1 ⊗A 1 ≃
                         ord                     ⊗r            L
                                                                                  A.
                                                                      ([0,1],∂[0,1])

 Its faces compose adjacent transverse operations. The full chiral OPE remains a morphism of
 factorisation D-modules and accompanies every bar term. Connected positive-weight algebras
 and their complete Mittag–Leffler limits satisfy internal associative bar–cobar reconstruction.
 The independent chiral convolution on D(Ran X) satisfies the Francis–Gaitsgory pro-nilpotent
 Chevalley–primitive equivalence. A strong monoidal realization compares the two constructions
 whenever its essential image is closed under bar and cobar.
     The augmentation boundary condition has endomorphism algebra

                                         A! = RHomA (1, 1).
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


     For positive finite-type objects, the double centralizer is the derived augmentation completion.
     The universal twisting morphism identifies augmentation-complete A-modules with conilpotent
     comodules over C = Barord X (A). Its two-sided form sends the diagonal bimodule to the diagonal
     bicomodule, transports derived tensor product to derived cotensor product, and identifies Hochschild
     theory with coHochschild theory. The interval, annulus, boundary dual, and derived centre are four
     faces of one Koszul–Morita square. Quadratic duality, PBW deformation, curved nonhomogeneous
     duality, and Verdier duality enter through explicit comparison morphisms to this universal resolution.
         Planar transport supplies exchange. An E2 enhancement, a flat meromorphic connection, or a
     braided line category produces braid monodromy. Internal Hochschild cochains

                                       Zord,ch (A) = RHomA⊗Aop (A, A)

     form the universal E2 recipient of central actions. A rigid meromorphic line category with a faithful
     tensor fibre functor reconstructs a coquasitriangular coordinate bialgebra; its continuous dual gives
     the corresponding completed quantum algebra. RTT relations identify the rational case with the
     Yangian. Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov transport and four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory realize
     the same exchange geometry.
         A square-zero BRST derivation extends factorwise to the ordered bar and forms a bicomplex
     with its bar differential. The resulting spectral sequence compares the bar of BRST cohomology with
     BRST cohomology of the bar. Cohomological concentration recovers quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov
     reduction and its principal W -algebra.
         The theory has two genus variables. A Calabi–Yau trace on the transverse algebra closes
     intervals into circles and produces Hochschild chains, ribbon graphs, and open topological surfaces.
     A modular sewing structure on the chiral coefficient glues pointed algebraic curves and produces
     conformal blocks over Deligne–Mumford moduli. Their simultaneous algebra is the two-edge-
     coloured master equation
                               1         1
                           dΘ + [Θ, Θ]X + [Θ, Θ]⊥ + ℏX ∆X Θ + ℏ⊥ ∆⊥ Θ = 0.
                               2         2
     The two loop operators arise from independent normal directions and supercommute. Curved
     Koszul elements, determinant-line curvature, BRST anomaly, framing anomaly, and conformal
     anomaly occupy their native deformation complexes; family index maps and open–closed comparison
     maps relate them in concrete theories.
         The paper proves the structural results above and calculates them on free and square-zero
     algebras, quantum and Jordan planes, Rees Weyl algebras, quiver and preprojective algebras,
     finite-group crossed products, enveloping algebras, Heisenberg and affine currents, free ghosts,
     lattice theories, Virasoro and principal W -algebras, RTT Yangians, KZ transport, prime-form
     sewing, Siegel theta series, and holomorphic–topological gauge theory. Every scalar appears as
     the value of a specified trace, index, period, or monodromy operation on a previously constructed
     object.

Overture: five geometries of one quantum operation
The diagonal carries collision. The interval carries order. The plane carries exchange. The circle
carries trace. The stable curve carries sewing. These five geometric carriers generate five algebraic
operations.
   On a smooth complex curve X, the singular product is supported on the diagonal:

                                       µ : j∗ j ∗ (g ⊠ g) −→ ∆! g.                                    (1)

On an oriented line, fusion is an associative operation

                                          m : A ⊗ A −→ A.                                             (2)

On a transverse plane, parallel transport in configuration space produces braid-group representations.
On a circle, closure is factorisation homology
                                           Z
                                                 A ≃ HH∗ (A).                                         (3)
                                            S1

On the boundary of the moduli space of curves, contraction along stable-graph edges produces sewing.
  The common source is
                                  A ∈ Algaug
                                           E1 FactD(X), ⊗
                                                            pt 
                                                                 .                               (4)
The factorisable D-module carries the chiral singularity. The E1 structure carries transverse order.
Planar transport adds exchange. A cyclic pairing adds trace. Clutching-compatible state sums add
modular sewing.

Theorem 0.1 (Five-carrier theorem). Let A be as in (4). The following constructions are functorial.
   The chiral coefficient is the system of D-modules on powers of X with factorisation maps and
diagonal singularities. The transverse bar is

                                         Barord
                                            X (A) ≃ 1 ⊗A 1.
                                                       L


Under analytic excision, it is factorisation homology of an interval with augmentation labels at the
endpoints. An E2 lift or flat meromorphic connection supplies braid transport. A cyclic trace supplies
the circle object and ribbon contractions. A modular action supplies stable-curve sewing. Each
construction is determined by its displayed geometric datum.

Proof. The coefficient system is the definition of a factorisable D-module. The simplicial two-sided bar
computes the derived tensor product, and factorisation excision identifies it with interval homology.
The little two-disks operad contains exchange paths and therefore braid operations. A cyclic pairing
evaluates the two ends of an interval and creates a circle. A modular action assigns contraction maps
to separating and nonseparating clutching correspondences. Functoriality follows from functoriality of
realization, transport, trace, and clutching.




                                                    17
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   The order of dependence is
                                            E -fusion
                         chiral coefficient −−−
                                              1
                                               −−−−→ ordered chiral algebra,
                                            E2 -lift or transport
                   ordered chiral algebra −−−−−−−−−−−−−→ exchange category,
                                                                                                           (5)
                                            cyclic trace
                   ordered chiral algebra −−−−−−−−→ ribbon amplitudes,
                                            modular sewing
                         chiral coefficient −−−−−−−−−−−→ stable-curve amplitudes.

The last two arrows coexist in the bicoloured theory and retain independent genus gradings.

The common law
Codimension-one boundary strata carry elementary compositions. Codimension-two corners carry
their relations. The oriented boundary of an oriented boundary is zero. Coefficient maps assigned to
the faces transform this topological identity into an algebraic master equation.
   For the interval, the two ends of a one-dimensional associahedral boundary give

                                          (ab)c − a(bc) = 0.

For three chiral points, the partial diagonals give the chiral Jacobi identity. For compactified Feynman
configurations, renormalised graph weights give the classical or quantum master equation. For stable
curves, separating and nonseparating nodes give the bracket and loop terms of the modular equation.
Topology supplies the signed incidence relation; the quantum theory supplies the coefficient maps.

Theorem 0.2 (Stokes-to-master theorem). Let C be an oriented stratified space whose codimension-one
faces are labelled by degree-one contractions ρF of a coefficient complex (E, dE ). Assume compatibility
with restriction to corners and
                                           dE ρF + ρF dE = 0.
For
                                           D = dE +
                                                           X
                                                               ρF ,
                                                           F

the component of D2 supported on a codimension-two stratum equals the signed sum of the composites
attached to its incident faces. Hence D2 = 0 exactly when every corner sum is zero.

Proof. Expand D2 . The internal square is zero and the mixed terms cancel by the chain-map identity.
Every remaining term is an ordered pair of incident faces. Grouping these pairs by their common
codimension-two stratum gives the oriented cellular boundary relation.

   This theorem organizes the bar differential, chiral Jacobi identity, Yang–Baxter equation, Batalin–
Vilkovisky equation, and modular sewing equation. In each case the compactified geometry provides
the incidence signs and the field theory provides the operations.

Six realizations
Dirac’s language is distributional: singular support belongs to the observable. The diagonal-supported
D-module operation in (1) is its algebraic incarnation.



                                                    18
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Feynman’s language is compositional: an amplitude is a sum over intermediate states. The ordered
bar is the derived complex of those intermediate boundary operations, and its differential fuses adjacent
states.
   Polyakov’s language is anomalous: quantisation produces a determinant line with curvature. A
number is obtained by evaluating its characteristic class on a chosen family.
   Witten’s language is topological: isotopy of line defects produces braid transport, and closure of a
line produces a ribbon trace.
   Gaiotto’s language is categorical: boundary conditions are objects, boundary-changing fields are
morphisms, interfaces are bimodules, and junctions are higher morphisms. The algebra (4) is the
endomorphism algebra of a boundary condition inside this category.
   Costello’s language is factorisational: renormalised observables extend across compactified collision
strata and satisfy the BV master equation. A theory holomorphic along X and topological along a
line realizes (4) analytically.
   These six realizations share one geometric syntax and preserve their native object types.

The invariant sentence
            A noncommutative chiral quantum field theory on a curve with one ordered
            topological direction is an augmented E1 -algebra in factorisable D-modules.

Every subsequent object is obtained from this datum by a named functor, geometric extension, or
trace. The geometry specifies the available composition; the coefficient specifies its value; the oriented
boundary specifies its equation.




                                                   19
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Part I
The noncommutative chiral object
1     The phenomenon and its invariant language
1.1   Two limits of the same experiment
Place local operators on a complex curve X and line defects on an oriented real line. There are two
independent short-distance limits. In the first, two points of X approach one another while their
transverse positions remain fixed. The product develops a finite principal part along the diagonal. In
the second, two transverse intervals become adjacent while their positions on X remain separated.
The product is associative and ordered. A theory in which both limits exist therefore carries two
operations of different geometric origin:

                            chiral collision on X,        ordered fusion on R.                         (7)

The first is encoded by a factorisation D-module. The second is encoded by an E1 -multiplication
internal to the category of such modules.
   This distinction is forced by topology. A punctured complex disc has a nontrivial fundamental
group, so analytic continuation of insertions can produce monodromy. An ordered chamber

                                      Conf <
                                           n (R) = {t1 < · · · < tn }

is contractible, so parallel transport inside one order chamber is trivial. The chiral singularity and the
associative order are independent data. Thickening the transverse line to a plane supplies exchange
paths and their braid classes.
Definition 1.1 (Ordered chiral algebra). Let X be a smooth complex algebraic curve and let FactD(X)
be the symmetric monoidal stable category of factorisable right D-modules defined in Section 2. An
ordered chiral algebra on X is an augmented algebra

                                        A ∈ Algaug
                                               E1 (FactD(X)).

Its multiplication is denoted m : A ⊗ A → A, and its augmentation is denoted ε : A → 1.
  The word chiral belongs to the coefficient category. The word ordered belongs to the E1 -operation.
The two adjectives name independent structures.

1.2   The diagonal and the interval
The diagonal is the carrier of the operator product expansion. Let

                               j : X 2 \ ∆(X) ,→ X 2 ,       ∆ : X ,→ X 2 .

A chiral singular product is a morphism from an extension off the diagonal to a distribution supported
on the diagonal. In the Lie form of Beilinson and Drinfeld it is

                                        µ : j∗ j ∗ (g ⊠ g) −→ ∆! g.                                    (8)

                                                     20
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


A coordinate expansion of (8) produces all OPE modes. The morphism itself is coordinate-free.
   The interval is the carrier of the ordered product. If I1 < I2 are disjoint subintervals of an oriented
interval I, factorisation gives
                                         A(I1 ) ⊗ A(I2 ) −→ A(I).
Shrinking I1 and I2 inside their order chamber produces the binary multiplication. Three ordered
subintervals produce the two composites (ab)c and a(bc); the contractibility of the space of such
shrinkings gives their coherent homotopy. After fixing the endpoints and quotienting by affine
reparametrisation, the standard real compactification of ordered points is an associahedron.
   The two geometries meet on X × R. A small rectangle U × I carries a complex of observables.
Chiral factorisation varies U in X; ordered factorisation varies I in R. Their interchange is the
geometric origin of an E1 -algebra in FactD(X).

1.3   The unique language
A useful language must satisfy four requirements.
   First, it must retain the entire singular product. A logarithmic residue detects a simple normal
coefficient. A fourth-order Virasoro pole and a double affine pole require higher normal jets. A
factorisation D-module contains all of them at once.
   Second, it must retain noncommutative fusion before any symmetrisation. Tensor order, line order,
and braid exchange are three different structures. The E1 -multiplication retains the first two; an E2
enhancement supplies the third.
   Third, it must separate algebraic and analytic completion. The bar construction is defined in
the stable category. Trace-class sewing and convergence of conformal blocks are additional analytic
theorems.
   Fourth, it must type every anomaly. Determinant-line curvature, the BRST anomaly class, the
BV quantum anomaly, and curved Koszul data live in different complexes. A comparison morphism
identifies selected classes in a concrete theory.
   These requirements lead to the following square:

                                                  Barord
                           AlgE1 (FactD(X))          X
                                                               Coalgconil (FactD(X))

                                 an                                        an                              (9)

                          Factrect
                              hol,lc (X
                                        an × R)    R               Facthol (X an ).
                                                       ([0,1],∂)



The upper row is the internal associative bar construction. For positive complete objects, cobar
reconstructs it. The lower row is interval factorisation of a rectangular holomorphic–topological theory.
On regular holonomic objects, Riemann–Hilbert identifies the vertical arrows. The square expresses
one phenomenon in algebraic and analytic language.

1.4   What is genuinely noncommutative
A conventional factorisation algebra on a manifold has symmetric multiplication for disjoint opens
because disjoint union is unordered. A codimension-one direction orders the opens and creates
noncommutative composition. Boundary conditions, interfaces, and parallel line defects supply such


                                                       21
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


directions. The resulting multiplication is composition in the defect category, while the chiral OPE
remains the diagonal singularity in the coefficient.
   Let C be a factorisation category on X and let b be an object representing a boundary condition.
The internal endomorphism object
                                          Ab = RHomC (b, b)                                       (10)
is an E1 -algebra in the factorisation coefficient category. Composition of endomorphisms gives the
E1 product. Collision of their insertion points gives the chiral singularity. Formula (10) is the native
source of noncommutative chiral algebras.
    A simple algebraic model is obtained from a factorisation object V and an associative algebra B.
If V is commutative as a factorisation object, then

                                                A=B⊗V

is an ordered chiral algebra with multiplication mB ⊗ mV . Matrix-valued free fields and Chan–Paton
factors have this form locally. The bar construction sees the noncommutative resolution of B while
every term remains a chiral coefficient system on X.

1.5   The bar is transverse
The reduced bar construction of an augmented algebra is generated by ordered words

                                                [a1 | · · · |ar ],

and its differential multiplies adjacent letters. The word order is the order of intervals on R. Each
coefficient ai and every factorisation map of A⊗r already carries the chiral singularities; the bar
differential composes the transverse word.
    This observation removes the original ambiguity between collision residues and the associative bar
differential. The de Rham differential of a configuration space, the residue differential along a collision
divisor, and the simplicial bar differential are three distinct operators. They can coexist in a total
complex, but their source and target degrees must be written separately.
Proposition 1.2 (Low-arity bar identity). Let A be a dg E1 -algebra in a stable symmetric monoidal
category. On homogeneous elements of the augmentation ideal,

                                b2 [a|b|c] = (−1)α [(ab)|c] + (−1)β [a|(bc)],

with the standard suspension signs, and

                                       b22 [a|b|c] = ±[(ab)c − a(bc)].

Hence the multiplication part of the bar differential squares to zero exactly by associativity.

Proof. There are two length-two contractions of a word of length three. The suspension orientation
gives opposite signs. The two coefficient operations agree by associativity.

   The complete chiral identities are tested elsewhere: by the chiral operad on the formal disc, by
factorisation on the Ran space, or by the partition collision complex. Associativity of the transverse
product and the Borcherds identity of the chiral coefficient are compatible, distinct equations.

                                                       22
R. Lorgat                                                              Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


1.6   From order to exchange
The distinction between order and exchange is the first physical test of the language. Parallel line
defects in a topological line multiply according to their order. A second topological direction supplies
an exchange path. The homotopy class of that path is a braid. Thus
                                      one more topological direction
                        E1 fusion    −−−−−−−−−−−−−−−−−−→                 E2 exchange.

Dunn additivity identifies the second structure with an E1 -algebra object in E1 -algebras. The universal
E2 object acting on an E1 algebra is its Hochschild centre.
  This yields a precise physical dictionary:

                  ordered intervals                fusion of parallel defects,
                  chiral diagonals                 singular OPE on the spectral curve,
                  second transverse direction      braid exchange,
                  Hochschild centre                universal bulk or exchange algebra,
                  Tannaka coend                    reconstructed quantum symmetry.

Each arrow in this table is a geometry-determined functor between typed objects.

1.7   The two notions of modularity
A trace on an E1 algebra closes an interval into a circle. Iterated gluing produces ribbon surfaces.
This is the modularity of open topological field theory and Hochschild homology.
   A chiral algebra on a varying algebraic curve has a different modularity. Plumbing a node changes
the complex curve. The relevant spaces are Mg,n , and the amplitudes are conformal blocks. The first
genus is transverse and ribbon; the second is complex and Deligne–Mumford.
   When an ordered chiral algebra carries both sewing structures and their mixed interchange maps,
the two contractions supercommute with the product orientation. The resulting master equation has
two loop operators. The separation of the two loop parameters is essential in gauge theory: one counts
topological colour loops, the other counts handles of the chiral curve.

1.8   Mathematics and physics
The mathematical object is an E1 algebra in factorisable D-modules. The physical object is a
holomorphic–topological factorisation algebra with a boundary or line defect. The comparison is the
morphism of factorisation algebras obtained by restricting quantum observables to the defect.
   Let ObsT be the quantum observables of a renormalised BV theory T on X × R≥0 . A boundary
condition gives a factorisation algebra Obs∂ on X × R. When holomorphic dependence admits a
factorisable D-module realization and the boundary theory is locally constant in the ordered direction,
Theorem 10.5 gives
                                     Obs∂ ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X)).                                     (11)
The renormalized quantum master equation makes the observable differential square to zero and makes
the factorisation maps compatible with ultraviolet contractions. A nonzero BV anomaly obstructs
this quantum factorisation object.
   The perturbative protected sectors of Chern–Simons theory, four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory,
suitable holomorphic twists of supersymmetric gauge theories, and open string field theory furnish

                                                      23
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


instances of this pattern after their boundary or defect observable comparisons are constructed. Their
quantum symmetries are determined by their exchange geometries and spectral curves; the interval
bar retains the common ordered composition.

1.9     The theorem spine
The paper proves the following sequence of statements.

    (i) Ordered block-Ran factorisation objects are equivalent to E1 -algebras in FactD(X).

 (ii) Their ordered bar is interval factorisation homology and has an associahedral cofibrant model.

 (iii) On connected positive-weight objects and their complete Mittag–Leffler limits, the internal
       associative bar and cobar reconstruct one another. Separately, Ran cardinality is pro-nilpotent
       for chiral convolution.

 (iv) The augmentation boundary has endomorphism algebra A! , and the double dual is the augmen-
      tation completion.

 (v) The Hochschild centre is the universal E2 algebra acting on an E1 algebra; a planar enhancement
     supplies the associated braid operations.

 (vi) Rigid meromorphic line categories reconstruct quantum symmetries by Tannaka coends.

(vii) BRST derivations extend to the bar and yield a convergent comparison spectral sequence under
      boundedness and completeness.

(viii) Cyclic transverse sewing and chiral curve sewing form a bi-modular master equation with two
       independent loop operators.

 (ix) Under the algebraisation, locality, gauge-fixing, and anomaly-cancellation hypotheses stated in
      Part V, factorisation observables realize the preceding constructions in perturbative holomorphic–
      topological field theory.

  The remainder of the paper develops the geometry and the exact examples behind these statements.
The remainder proves that these subjects are functorial realizations of a single ordered chiral object.


2      Factorisable D-modules and the complete chiral coefficient
2.1     The finite-set presentation
Let X be a smooth complex algebraic curve. Write Finsurj for the category of nonempty finite sets
and surjections. A surjection α : I ↠ J determines a diagonal

                              ∆α : X J −→ X I ,     (xj )j∈J 7−→ (xα(i) )i∈I .

The Ran prestack is the colimit
                                       Ran(X) =      colim         XI .
                                                  I∈(Finsurj )op




                                                  24
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


A right D-module on Ran(X) is represented by a compatible family FI ∈ D-mod(X I ) with transition
morphisms along the diagonals. All functors below are derived.
   For a decomposition I = I1 ⊔ · · · ⊔ Ir , let

                                         U (I1 , . . . , Ir ) ⊂ X I

be the open locus on which points belonging to different blocks are disjoint; points in one block may
collide. Denote the inclusion by jI• .
Definition 2.1 (Factorisable D-module). A factorisable right D-module F on X is a compatible Ran
family {FI } together with equivalences

                                    jI! • FI ≃ jI! • FI1 ⊠ · · · ⊠ FIr                                    (12)
                                                                         


for every decomposition of I, compatible with refinement, permutation, and diagonal transition maps.
   The equivalence (12) expresses independence on separated supports. The extension of FI across
partial diagonals carries the singular terms of the operator product. The definition is intrinsic and
coordinate-free; propagators enter in analytic realizations.
   Let FactD(X) denote a stable presentable enhancement of this category. Its coefficient tensor
product is the componentwise !-tensor product

                                       (F ⊗pt G)I := FI ⊗! GI .                                           (13)

The unit is the vacuum factorisation module 1.
Proposition 2.2. Assume that the chosen Ran enhancement is closed under componentwise !-tensor
product and componentwise colimits. Then (13) makes FactD(X) a presentable stable symmetric
monoidal category, and tensor product preserves colimits separately.

Proof. The assertion holds on each X I . The diagonal compatibilities and the restrictions in (12) are
preserved by !-tensor product. The stated closure hypotheses therefore descend the componentwise
structure to the factorisation subcategory.

  This is the symmetric monoidal category in which the transverse E1 multiplication is formed. It
must be distinguished from the chiral tensor product on D(Ran X), introduced below.

2.2   The complete singular coefficient
Consider the binary component F{1,2} on X 2 . Off the diagonal, factorisation identifies it with
F{1} ⊠ F{2} . The extension across the diagonal has local cohomology supported on ∆(X). In a formal
coordinate it is expressed by principal parts
                                           X      cm (w)
                                                          .
                                           m≥0
                                               (z − w)m+1

Coordinate changes act triangularly on the coefficients and preserve the filtered distribution.
  For D = ∆(X) ⊂ X 2 , the pole filtration is

                                     Fm OX 2 (∗D) = OX 2 (mD),

                                                    25
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


and
                                                          ⊗m
                                        grFm OX 2 (∗D) ≃ ND/X 2.                                     (14)
Thus the associated graded of a pole is a tensorial normal symbol; the filtered object retains the
coordinate-dependent mixing of modes.

Principle 2.3 (Complete coefficient principle). The chiral coefficient of an ordered chiral algebra is
the factorisable D-module itself. Residues, normal jets, formal modes, and distributional kernels are
realizations of this coefficient.

Proof. The factorisable D-module is defined on the whole configuration space together with its
extensions across every partial diagonal. Restriction to the complement gives the regular product;
local cohomology along a diagonal gives supported singular distributions; the pole filtration gives
normal symbols; a formal coordinate expands those symbols into modes; de Rham and distributional
realization give residues and kernels. Each construction is a functor applied to the same coefficient
object.

   For an ordered chiral algebra, the transverse multiplication

                                           m : A ⊗pt A −→ A

is a morphism of factorisable D-modules. It therefore respects the whole diagonal filtration and every
disjoint-factorisation map. In coordinates it acts on the complete singular expansion, including every
pole order and the regular term.

2.3   Two Lie structures and their comparison
Restriction along the operad map Lie → E1 gives the graded commutator

                                  [a, b]⊥ = m(a, b) − (−1)|a||b| m(b, a).                            (15)

Proposition 2.4 (Internal commutator). Restriction of operads defines

                          AlgE1 FactD(X), ⊗pt −→ AlgLie FactD(X), ⊗pt .
                                                                            


The bracket (15) satisfies the graded Jacobi identity.

Proof. It is the commutator bracket in an associative algebra object of a symmetric monoidal category.
The usual cancellation proof of Jacobi is operadic and therefore applies internally.

   The bracket [−, −]⊥ is a Lie operation on the transverse multiplication. A Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral
Lie bracket is instead a morphism
                                         j∗ j ∗ (g ⊠ g) −→ ∆! g
in the chiral pseudo-tensor category. The factorisation coefficient of A may carry such a chiral
operation. A morphism of operadic deformation problems compares it with the internal commutator
(15). This distinction is essential in boundary field theory: the chiral bracket measures collision on X,
while [−, −]⊥ measures the discontinuity between two transverse orders.




                                                     26
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


2.4   Augmentations and boundary conditions
An augmentation ε : A → 1 is a morphism of E1 algebras in FactD(X). It makes 1 a left and right
A-module, and the augmentation ideal is
                                                        ε
                                           Ā = Fib(A −
                                                      → 1).

For a right module R and a left module L, the two-sided bar Bar(R, A, L) computes interval factorisation
homology with endpoint labels R and L. The reduced bar is the case R = L = 1.
   The module formulation is physically primary. A boundary condition is an object whose local
endomorphisms form an E1 algebra. The unit represents the distinguished augmentation boundary
state.

2.5   Two monoidal products on the Ran space
There are two different tensor products in the subject.
  The coefficient tensor ⊗pt of (13) is componentwise. Its support satisfies

                                 supp(F ⊗pt G) ⊂ supp(F) ∩ supp(G).

It preserves each cardinality profile. Pro-nilpotence for this tensor product is therefore controlled by
augmentation or weight filtrations.
   The chiral tensor product on D(Ran X) is defined by the disjoint-union correspondence

                          Ran(X) × Ran(X) ⊃ (Ran × Ran)disj −→ Ran(X).

For reduced objects supported on subsets of cardinality at least r and s, respectively, chiral convolution
is supported in cardinality at least r + s:

                           D(Ran X)≥r ⊗ch D(Ran X)≥s ⊂ D(Ran X)≥r+s .                                (16)

Theorem 2.5 (Ran chiral pro-nilpotence). For the chiral tensor product ⊗ch , the quotient by objects
supported in cardinality > N is nilpotent on reduced objects, and the cardinality completion is pro-
nilpotent. This statement concerns D(Ran X) with chiral convolution. The pointwise tensor product
of FactD(X) uses its augmentation or weight filtration instead.

Proof. Equation (16) implies that an (N + 1)-fold chiral product of reduced objects is supported in
cardinality at least N + 1 and hence vanishes in the N th quotient. Taking the inverse limit gives the
pro-nilpotent category. This is the support argument used in chiral Koszul duality on the Ran space
[38].

   The two products answer different questions. The pointwise tensor permits an E1 algebra of defects
with a fixed chiral coefficient. The chiral tensor assembles disjoint finite subsets and is the ambient
product for the Francis–Gaitsgory Lie–commutative-coalgebra equivalence. Any comparison between
their bar constructions must name the monoidal functor that relates them.




                                                   27
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


2.6   Regular holonomic realization
Assume that every FI is regular holonomic and that the chosen subcategory is closed under the
tensor operations used above. After the standard de Rham shifts, Riemann–Hilbert identifies FI
with a constructible complex on X I,an . Factorisation equivalences become constructible factorisation
equivalences on the disjoint loci.

Proposition 2.6 (Analytic realization). Under these regular-holonomic closure hypotheses, Riemann–
Hilbert gives a symmetric monoidal realization from (FactD(X), ⊗pt ) to constructible factorisation
sheaves on X an . It carries internal E1 algebras, modules, bar constructions, and Hochschild cochains
to their constructible realizations whenever it commutes with the required geometric realizations and
internal Homs.

Proof. Riemann–Hilbert identifies regular holonomic D-modules with constructible complexes and
carries external tensor products and inverse images along the open configuration embeddings to
their analytic counterparts, with the standard de Rham shifts. Hence it transports the factorisation
equivalences and the pointwise symmetric monoidal product. Algebra and module structures are
operadic diagrams built from these products, so they are preserved. Geometric realization of the
simplicial bar and internal Hom are preserved under the additional commutation hypotheses stated in
the proposition, which gives the bar and Hochschild comparisons.

   The analytic realization reveals the monodromy of local systems on Conf n (X); planar exchange
data supplies the transverse braid action.

2.7   Factorisation categories and Morita origin
Let C be a D-linear factorisation category on X, and let b be a compatible object. Its internal
endomorphisms are
                                  Ab (U ) = RHomC(U ) (b|U , b|U ).
Composition is ordered, while factorisation in U supplies the chiral coefficient.

Theorem 2.7 (Morita source). Let C be a presentable D-linear factorisation category and let b be a
dualisable factorisation object. Then Ab = RHomC (b, b) is an E1 algebra in FactD(X). If b is compact
and generates each local category, and these local Morita equivalences commute with factorisation
descent, then
                                 RHomC (b, −) : C −→ ModAb (FactD(X))
is a factorisation Morita equivalence.

Proof. Composition gives the E1 multiplication. Factorisation of b on disjoint opens gives the
coefficient factorisation maps. The compact-generator Morita theorem applies locally; the final
hypothesis identifies the local equivalences on overlaps and descends them.

   This is the source encountered in gauge theory: a boundary condition or line defect is b; local
endomorphisms form Ab ; transverse composition is noncommutative; singular dependence on the
holomorphic insertion point is carried by the factorisable D-module.




                                                  28
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


2.8   Historical position
Dirac’s distributional formalism made the singular support of products of quantum fields unavoidable.
Wilson organised short-distance singularities by the operator product expansion. Borcherds and Kac
encoded their algebraic identities. Beilinson and Drinfeld replaced coordinate series by D-module
morphisms on diagonals. Factorisation algebras expressed the same local-to-global law for quantum
observables. The present object adds an oriented topological direction and retains its associative order
before any exchange or symmetrisation is imposed.


3     The duoidal law: locality as a bialgebra identity
The primitive object admits a second, equivalent description which makes its geometry visible at
once. The Ran category carries a pointwise tensor product and a convolution product along disjoint
union. One product combines coefficients supported at the same finite subset; the other combines
observables supported at disjoint finite subsets. Their compatibility is duoidal. An ordered chiral
algebra is a monoid for the first product and a factorisation coalgebra for the second. The assertion
that multiplication is a morphism of factorisation objects is therefore a bialgebra identity, but the
multiplication and comultiplication belong to different monoidal structures.

3.1   The two products and their interchange
Let
                                          DX = D(Ran X).
Write ⊗pt for the pointwise !-tensor product. Write ⊗ch for chiral convolution along the correspondence
                           j                            u
         Ran X × Ran X ←−− (Ran X × Ran X)disj −−→ Ran X,             F ⊗ch G := u! j ! (F ⊠ G).

The diagonal of the Ran space is compatible with disjoint union. The projection formula and
Beck–Chevalley base change therefore give a natural interchange morphism

                    (F1 ⊗ch F2 ) ⊗pt (G1 ⊗ch G2 ) −→ (F1 ⊗pt G1 ) ⊗ch (F2 ⊗pt G2 ),                 (17)

with the middle factors permuted by the symmetry of ⊗pt . In a six-functor realization for which the
relevant base-change and projection-formula maps are equivalences, (17) is an equivalence on the
disjoint locus. In the general six-functor setting it is the corresponding lax interchange map.

Theorem 3.1 (Duoidal Ran theorem). Fix a sheaf theory on Ran X for which pointwise !-tensor,
chiral convolution, the projection formula, and disjoint-union base change are defined. Then

                                            (DX , ⊗pt , ⊗ch )

is a lax duoidal stable category. On the full subcategory on which the base-change morphisms in (17)
are equivalences, it is a strong duoidal category.

Proof. Associativity and symmetry of ⊗pt are inherited componentwise from D(X I ). Associativity of
⊗ch is the associativity of union of pairwise disjoint finite subsets. Apply the two chiral coproduct
correspondences, tensor their outputs pointwise, and then use the diagonal base-change square to


                                                   29
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


regroup the first and third, and the second and fourth, factors. The resulting natural transformation
is (17). The coherence diagrams reduce to the associativity, symmetry, and base-change diagrams of
the six-functor formalism. This is precisely the definition of a lax duoidal category; invertibility of the
interchange maps gives the strong form.

   The theorem is an instance of the general theory of duoidal ∞-categories [79]. Its particular content
here is geometric: the two products are the diagonal product and disjoint-union convolution on the
Ran space.

3.2    Factorisation coalgebras
A chiral commutative coalgebra is a commutative coalgebra object for ⊗ch . Its coproduct restricts, for
disjoint finite subsets S and T , to a map

                                        ∆S,T : FS∪T −→ FS ⊠ FT .

It is a factorisation coalgebra when this map is an equivalence on every disjointness locus. Francis and
Gaitsgory identify factorisation algebras with the corresponding full subcategory of chiral commutative
coalgebras [38].
    Let
                                    ι : FactD(X) ,→ CoCAlgch (DX )
denote this realization. The pointwise tensor of two factorisation coalgebras is again a factorisation
coalgebra: its coproduct is obtained by applying (17) to their two coproducts.

Theorem 3.2 (Ordered chirality as a duoidal bimonoid). Assume the strong duoidal hypotheses of
Theorem 3.1 on the chosen factorisation subcategory. For an object A ∈ FactD(X), the following data
are equivalent:

  (i) an E1 multiplication on A internal to FactD(X);

 (ii) an E1 monoid structure for ⊗pt on ιA such that its multiplication and unit are morphisms of
      chiral commutative coalgebras;

 (iii) a bimonoid in the duoidal category (DX , ⊗pt , ⊗ch ) whose chiral coproduct is factorising on disjoint
       loci.

The compatibility is the duoidal bialgebra identity

                               ∆ch ◦ m = (m ⊗ch m) ◦ χ ◦ (∆ch ⊗pt ∆ch ),                                (18)

where χ is the interchange morphism.

Proof. An E1 algebra in the full subcategory of factorisation coalgebras is, by definition, an E1
multiplication whose structure maps remain in that subcategory. Saying that multiplication is a
morphism of coalgebras is exactly (18); saying that the unit is a coalgebra map is the corresponding
unit identity. These are the axioms of a bimonoid in a duoidal category. The factorisation condition
is the requirement that the chiral coproduct become an equivalence on disjoint loci.



                                                     30
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


    Equation (18) is the exact algebraic form of locality for the noncommutative theory. Separate two
collections of insertions in the chiral curve and fuse them in the ordered direction. One obtains the
same operation by fusing inside each separated region and then applying factorisation. The equation
is the bimonoid interchange law; cocommutativity and R-matrix relations are additional structures.

3.3   The bar retains factorisation
The simplicial ordered bar is levelwise a chiral commutative coalgebra:
                                                          pt
                                        Br (A) = 1 ⊗pt Ā⊗ r ⊗pt 1.

Every face is built from m or the augmentation. By Theorem 3.2, these are coalgebra maps. Every
degeneracy is built from the unit and is likewise a coalgebra map.
Theorem 3.3 (Double-coalgebra bar theorem). Suppose geometric realization in DX preserves chiral
commutative coalgebras and commutes with the pointwise tensor products appearing in the simplicial
bar. Then
                                        C = Barord
                                                X (A)

has two compatible coalgebra structures:

  (i) the coassociative deconcatenation coproduct of the E1 bar;

 (ii) the commutative chiral factorisation coproduct inherited levelwise from A.

Deconcatenation is a morphism of chiral factorisation coalgebras. Equivalently, C is a coassociative
coalgebra object in chiral commutative coalgebras.

Proof. The simplicial bar is a simplicial object in chiral commutative coalgebras because every simplicial
operator is a coalgebra morphism. Geometric realization gives the second coproduct. Deconcatenation
is defined by cutting an ordered word. The chiral coproduct acts independently on each letter, so
cutting and factorising commute by the interchange law. The resulting square is (18) applied to the
iterated pointwise tensor powers of A.

   This theorem explains why the ordered bar is simultaneously an interval object and a Ran-space
coefficient. The coassociative coproduct remembers where the interval is cut. The chiral coproduct
remembers where the curve is separated. Their compatibility is inherited from locality of the original
ordered chiral algebra.

3.4   A Fubini theorem
Let p : X an × R → X an and q : X an × R → R be the projections. Factorisation pushforward along p
integrates the locally constant real direction; pushforward along q integrates the chiral direction when
the corresponding chiral homology is defined. Product excision gives a Fubini comparison.
Theorem 3.4 (Ordered chiral Fubini). Let A be an ordered chiral algebra whose analytic realization is
rectangularly generated. Assume the relevant factorisation homologies exist and that the pushforwards
preserve the geometric realizations of the bar. Then there are natural equivalences
                                    Z                          Z     
                                         Barord
                                            X (A) ≃ Bar
                                                        ord
                                                                    A ,                                 (19)
                                     X                          X


                                                    31
R. Lorgat                                                          Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields

                                       Z                    Z     
                                            HH∗ (A) ≃ HH∗         A .                                  (20)
                                        X                     X

More generally, factorisation homology over a product rectangle may be computed in either order.
                                                                                  pt
Proof. The bar is a geometric realization of the simplicial object [r] 7→ Ā⊗ r . Chiral factorisation
homology is a colimit over chiral disks. Under the stated preservation hypotheses, the two colimits
commute, giving (19). Hochschild chains are factorisation homology over the transverse circle, or
equivalently the cyclic realization of the two-sided bar. Commuting that realization with chiral
factorisation homology gives (20). On the analytic product, both assertions are the Fubini theorem
for factorisation homology and follow from excision on product-disk covers.

    The first equivalence says that one may integrate the chiral curve before or after resolving ordered
fusion. The second says the same after the interval has been closed into a circle. In conformal
field theory this is the equality between first forming the open-string trace and then taking chiral
coinvariants, or first taking chiral coinvariants and then closing the ordered state space. Analytic
convergence of either trace remains a separate theorem.

3.5    Primitives and quantum symmetry
The chiral factorisation coproduct in Theorem 3.3 is a separation map. A quantum-group coproduct
acts on tensor products of line representations and is supplied by a tensor-compatible exchange theory.
Gauge-theoretic examples compare it with the chiral separation coproduct through the line-operator
realization.
   The primitive part of the chiral coalgebra records connected support. The primitive part of the
coassociative bar coalgebra records indecomposable interval states. Their intersection consists of states
that are connected in the curve and indecomposable along the interval. This bidegree is the natural
home of a binary collision kernel or a first line-defect interaction. An R-matrix arises only after an
exchange functor transports these primitives around a two-dimensional collision.

3.6    Physical meaning
The two products of the duoidal category are two experimental operations. Pointwise tensor places
two coefficient systems on the same chiral support and then fuses them in transverse order. Chiral
convolution places them on separated supports and then forgets the separation by union. Equation
(18) says that a local experiment performed in two separated laboratories is independent of whether
fusion is described before or after the laboratories are separated.
   This is the geometric content of locality in the ordered theory. It refines associativity by constraining
support dependence. Planar exchange paths refine it further to braiding. It is the point at which
Beilinson’s factorisation language and the noncommutative language of defects become one sentence.


4     The block-ordered chiral configuration category
4.1    What is ordered
Let ChX denote a fixed operadic enhancement of the Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral pseudo-tensor structure
whose algebras in the chosen derived system of right D-modules are the factorisable D-modules of


                                                    32
R. Lorgat                                                                  Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Theorem 2.1. The noncommutative chiral operad is

                                          OChX := E1 ⊗ ChX ,                                                   (21)

where ⊗ is the Boardman–Vogt tensor product of ∞-operads. An algebra over (21) has a chiral
factorisation structure and an associative transverse multiplication, with the interchange relation built
into the operad.
    The word ordered refers to the E1 inputs. It orders entire chiral input blocks. A typical operation
is represented by an ordered sequence
                                              (I1 , . . . , Ir ),                                    (22)
where the finite set Ia labels the chiral insertions carried by the ath transverse interval. Points
belonging to different blocks may have the same coordinate on X because their transverse coordinates
are distinct. Collisions inside the chiral direction are carried by the D-module extension across
diagonals; composition in the transverse direction is carried by E1 .

4.2   The operadic Grothendieck construction
The block-ordered chiral configuration category BRanX is the operadic Grothendieck construction of
the associative operad acting on the chiral configuration category. Its objects are pairs

                                                ([r]; I1 , . . . , Ir ),

with [r] a finite ordinal and each Ia a finite chiral label set. Its generating morphisms are of three
kinds:

  (i) chiral refinements, collisions, and permutations inside one Ia ;

 (ii) adjacent composition of two E1 slots;

(iii) insertion or removal of the unit slot.

The relations are the chiral relations, the simplicial identities of the associative nerve, and the
interchange squares between the two kinds of operation.
   The object space of a fixed block profile (22) is

                                               X I1 × · · · × X Ir .                                           (23)

This product is the geometric carrier for operations; the monoidal structure of FactD(X) is supplied
by the chosen operadic pull–tensor–push correspondence. The coefficient morphism attached to
an adjacent block composition is obtained from the E1 multiplication after the chiral inputs have
been placed in the common output profile. In a six-functor model, the required pull–tensor–push
correspondence is part of the chosen operadic enhancement. The theorem applies to D-modules
equipped with the selected operadic enhancement and its base-change maps.

Definition 4.1 (Block-ordered factorisation D-module). A block-ordered factorisation D-module
is an algebra over OChX = E1 ⊗ ChX in the chosen derived D-module system. Equivalently, it is a
coCartesian operadic section over BRanX whose restriction to each block is chiral factorisable, whose
restriction to the ordinal direction is Segal, and whose mixed squares satisfy interchange.

                                                          33
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Write OrdFactD(X) for this category. The definition includes the coefficient correspondences
needed to compare the different spaces (23); it is therefore stronger than the datum of a simplicial
family of sheaves on their underlying products.

Theorem 4.2 (Block-ordered recognition). There are natural equivalences

                       AlgE1 FactD(X) ≃ AlgE1 ⊗ChX (VX ) ≃ OrdFactD(X),                                   (24)
                                           


where VX is the chosen finite-set D-module system presenting FactD(X).

Proof. By definition, FactD(X) ≃ AlgChX (VX ). The exponential law for ∞-operads gives
                                                      
                               AlgE1 AlgChX (VX ) ≃ AlgE1 ⊗ChX (VX ).

Unstraightening an algebra over the tensor-product operad gives a coCartesian section over its operadic
Grothendieck construction. The restrictions to the two operadic factors give the chiral and Segal
conditions; the tensor-product relation gives interchange. Straightening reverses the construction.

  The theorem is the invariant meaning of factorisation over an ordered chiral configuration space.
The block picture is a geometric presentation of the tensor-product operad.

4.3   The strict and homotopy-coherent forms
In a strict dg model, an ordered chiral algebra is a factorisable D-module A with maps

                                 m : A ⊗pt A −→ A,              η : 1 −→ A,

satisfying strict associativity and unitality. In the invariant ∞-category it is an E1 algebra. A
cofibrant A∞ model replaces the single multiplication by operations indexed by planar trees. The
chiral coefficient is unchanged.

Proposition 4.3 (Interchange). Let γ be a chiral operation and let m be the transverse multiplication.
Whenever the two composites are defined by one product-disk embedding, their images satisfy

                                      γ ◦ (m ⊗ m) = m ◦ (γ ⊗ γ),                                          (25)

with the symmetry isomorphism that places equal-coloured inputs together.

Proof. Equation (25) is the defining interchange relation of E1 ⊗ ChX . In the analytic product-disk
model, both sides are the factorisation map associated with the same embedding, evaluated by
composing first in opposite coordinate directions.

   The proposition is stronger than the coexistence of two operations and weaker than braiding. It
says that holomorphic collision and transverse composition are compatible. A planar enhancement
adds paths that exchange transverse factors.

4.4   Analytic chamber model
For r ≥ 1, let
                               Cr< = {(t1 , . . . , tr ) ∈ Rr | t1 < · · · < tr }.

                                                      34
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


This chamber is contractible. A profile (22) has analytic carrier

                                      X I1 ,an × · · · × X Ir ,an × Cr< .                                (26)

Local constancy in Cr< erases the distances while retaining the order. The X-coordinates remain
holomorphic and may approach any partial diagonal allowed by the coefficient D-module.
   Compactifying the configurations of subintervals in one interval modulo translation and positive
dilation gives associahedra. Their strata are indexed by planar rooted trees. This compactification
resolves the homotopy coherence of the E1 multiplication.

Proposition 4.4 (Associahedral resolution). For a locally constant factorisation algebra on an oriented
line, cellular chains on compactified ordered interval configurations act by the A∞ bar operations.
Contracting every associahedral cell to its corolla gives the ordinary simplicial bar construction through
a quasi-isomorphism of operadic resolutions.

Proof. Factorisation assigns an operation to every ordered embedding of disjoint intervals. The
corresponding embedding spaces are contractible, and their compactified boundary strata are planar
nested collisions. Stokes’ formula is the sum of edge contractions, hence the Stasheff identity. The
cellular chains form a cofibrant resolution of the associative operad, while the simplicial bar is the
standard resolution of an algebra over that operad. The comparison is the augmentation of the
cofibrant resolution.

   The simplex and the associahedron therefore play different roles. The simplex is the indexing object
of the simplicial bar. The associahedron is a geometric cofibrant resolution of associative coherence.

4.5    Mixed collision geometry
A chain model for OChX may be built from two-coloured forests. Chiral edges describe nested partial
diagonals in X; transverse edges describe nested intervals in the ordered line. A face contracts one
edge of one colour. On a total complex, the two boundary operators anticommute by the product
orientation.

Lemma 4.5 (Mixed square-zero identity). Let dX be the differential supplied by a chain model of
the chiral coefficient and let b⊥ be the E1 bar differential. If both are morphisms for the interchange
structure, then
                                           dX b⊥ + b⊥ dX = 0
after the standard totalization sign. Hence

                                         (dX + b⊥ )2 = d2X + b2⊥ .

Proof. The two operators are boundaries in independent factors of the mixed operadic resolution.
Exchanging their conormal orientation lines contributes the totalization sign. Compatibility with
interchange identifies the two coefficient composites.

   Associativity proves b2⊥ = 0. The chiral identities prove d2X = 0 in the chosen chiral model. The
total square is zero when both identities and their mixed interchange hold.




                                                     35
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


4.6   Modules, interfaces, and marked blocks
A right module is an algebra over the two-coloured operad with ordered algebra inputs followed by
one module input. A left module uses the opposite order, and a bimodule has both boundary colours.
For a right module R and a left module L, the simplicial two-sided bar is

                                     Br (R, A, L) = R ⊗ A⊗r ⊗ L.                                     (27)

Its realization is interval factorisation homology with endpoint labels R and L.

Proposition 4.6 (Marked-block recognition). The category of right, left, and bi-modules over A is
equivalent to the corresponding category of marked coCartesian sections over the coloured block-ordered
configuration category. Relative tensor product of bimodules is gluing of marked intervals.

Proof. Apply the straightening argument of Theorem 4.2 to the standard coloured associative operads
for modules and bimodules. Excision for the glued interval computes the relative tensor product.

   This is the native geometry of boundary-changing fields and defect junctions.

4.7   Opposite order
Reflection of the transverse line reverses the ordinal factor while leaving X unchanged. It induces

                                         rev : OChX −→ OChX

and sends multiplication to its opposite.

Proposition 4.7 (Geometric opposite). Under Theorem 4.2, transverse reflection sends A to Aop .
Its chiral coefficient is unchanged.

Proof. Reflection reverses the order of the two little intervals in every binary E1 operation, so the
resulting multiplication is m ◦ τ . It acts trivially on the curve X and therefore leaves the chiral
operadic factor and its coefficient correspondences fixed. Straightening identifies the reflected section
with Aop .

   This is the canonical inversion available at the E1 level. An E2 structure equips this reflection with
inverse braiding.

4.8   Three distinct forgetful operations
Three distinct operations occur under the heading of symmetry.
   First, forgetting the E1 multiplication leaves the underlying factorisable D-module. This functor
loses all transverse products.
   Second, derived commutativisation is the left adjoint

                               AlgE1 (FactD(X)) −→ AlgE∞ (FactD(X))

when it exists in the selected presentable category. It imposes coherent commutativity on the transverse
product. It is the universal coherent-commutativisation of the transverse algebra.


                                                   36
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Third, descent from labelled to unlabelled configurations concerns monodromy of a coefficient
local system on Conf n (X). It asks for extension from the pure braid group to the full braid group;
symmetric exchange further asks that the action factor through Σn . This is independent of the
existence of a transverse E1 multiplication.
   The separation of these three operations is the first safeguard of noncommutative chiral Koszul
duality.


5     The mixed operad of chiral collision and ordered fusion
5.1   The invariant operad
Let ChX be an ∞-operad whose algebras in the chosen coefficient category are factorisable D-modules
on X. The ordered chiral operad is defined by the Boardman–Vogt tensor product

                                       OrdChX := E1 ⊗ ChX .                                           (28)

The tensor product is characterized by interchange: an operation in the E1 direction commutes with
an operation in the chiral direction after the canonical permutation and base-change maps.

Theorem 5.1 (Operadic additivity). Let V be a presentable symmetric monoidal ∞-category for
which the indicated algebra categories exist. Then

                                AlgOrdChX (V) ≃ AlgE1 AlgChX (V) .                                    (29)
                                                                       


For the D-module realization of ChX , the right-hand side is AlgE1 (FactD(X)).

Proof. Equation (29) is the universal property of the tensor product of ∞-operads. It identifies an
algebra carrying two compatible operadic actions with an algebra over their tensor product.

   For locally constant operations, Dunn additivity identifies E1 ⊗ En with En+1 [28, 54]. The chiral
operation on X varies singularly across collision diagonals, so (28) retains a holomorphic singular
direction together with an ordered topological direction.

5.2   Three geometric models
Three spaces occur for three different reasons.
   The simplex indexes the monadic simplicial bar. The associahedron resolves the coherent multiplica-
tion of an E1 algebra. The Fulton–MacPherson compactification resolves relative chiral configurations
near collision diagonals. Together they provide the simplicial, associative-coherent, and collision-
geometric resolutions.
   Fix an ordered partition I = I1 ⊔ · · · ⊔ Ir . On that stratum, a local product chart has the form
                                                r
                                                      FMIa (X),                                       (30)
                                                Y
                                         Kr ×
                                                a=1

up to the usual base and normal-bundle factors. When a transverse face merges two blocks, the
partition changes and the corresponding Fulton–MacPherson factors are replaced by the factor for



                                                  37
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


their union. Thus the global resolution is assembled from the charts (30) over the category of ordered
partitions; the global resolution is the resulting colimit of the partition-indexed charts.

5.3     Two-coloured levelled forests
Choose a cofibrant dg-operadic presentation

                                              QX −→ ChX

whose chiral cells are represented locally by Fulton–MacPherson incidence cells with their screen-chain
coefficients. Apply a functorial Boardman–Vogt construction to the tensor-product operad:

                                                                                                        (31)
                                                     
                                        W E1 ⊗ QX −→ E1 ⊗ ChX .

    A cellular basis element of this resolution is represented by a rooted levelled forest with two kinds
of internal edge:

  • a transverse edge, recording composition of consecutive E1 blocks;

  • a chiral edge, recording a nested collision inside a chiral coefficient.

A vertex carries the screen-chain coefficient prescribed by QX . The determinant line is

                                 or(F ) = det(E⊥ (F )) ⊗ det(Ech (F )).                                 (32)

Definition 5.2 (Mixed forest complex). For a finite label set I, let

                             W OrdChX (I) =            C∗ (Screen(F )) ⊗ or(F ),                        (33)

where F ranges over the two-coloured levelled forests in the chosen cellular presentation. The
differential is the sum of the internal screen differential and the signed contractions of one transverse
or one chiral edge.

   This definition is a chain model of the operadic resolution. A smooth compactification carrying
these strata, when constructed, realizes the same incidence complex geometrically.

5.4     The boundary identity
Write
                                           d = dscr + d⊥ + dch .

Theorem 5.3 (Mixed boundary theorem). With the orientation convention (32),

                             d2⊥ = 0,      d2ch = 0,        d⊥ dch + dch d⊥ = 0,

and the screen differential supercommutes with both edge-contraction differentials. Hence d2 = 0.

Proof. A codimension-two forest is obtained either by contracting two transverse edges, two chiral
edges, or one edge of each colour. In the first two cases, the two orders of contraction induce opposite
boundary orientations in the corresponding one-colour Boardman–Vogt complex. In the mixed case,

                                                       38
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


the final forest is the same in either order, while exchanging the two one-dimensional determinant
factors in (32) changes the sign. The screen differential is compatible with every operadic face map
and contributes the standard total-complex signs.

   When endomorphism operations are attached to vertices, the transverse part of this identity
becomes the Stasheff relations, the chiral part becomes the identities of the chosen chiral coefficient
model, and the mixed part says that transverse multiplication is a morphism of chiral objects.

5.5   Cofibrant comparison
Theorem 5.4 (Mixed Boardman–Vogt resolution). Assume that chain operads are taken in an
admissible monoidal model category, that QX is cofibrant, and that the tensor-product operad and
functorial W -construction preserve weak equivalences between the relevant cofibrant objects. Then the
augmentation
                                                                                                  (34)
                                                 
                                     W E1 ⊗ QX −→ E1 ⊗ ChX
is a cofibrant resolution. Its algebras are homotopy-coherent ordered chiral algebras. When rectification
holds in the coefficient model category, their localized category is equivalent to AlgE1 (FactD(X)).

Proof. The W -construction replaces each internal edge by a length parameter and has contractible
length fibres. Under the stated model-categorical hypotheses it is cofibrant and its edge-contraction
augmentation is an aritywise weak equivalence. The tensor-product universal property identifies its
strict algebras with compatible E1 and chiral structures. Rectification is the standard change-of-operad
equivalence along a weak equivalence between admissible cofibrant operads.

   The hypotheses in this theorem are part of the mathematical statement. The displayed hypotheses
supply the cofibrant tensor-product resolution and its interchange cells.

5.6   Incidence and screen geometry
A collision forest has an incidence type and a screen space. The incidence category records which
clusters collide and in what order. Its fibre over a fixed root partition has a terminal corolla and hence
a contractible nerve. The screen space records relative positions inside each cluster and generally has
nontrivial cohomology.
   For three points on an affine complex line modulo translation and dilation, the open screen is
P1 \ {0, 1, ∞}. Its first cohomology has rank two. Therefore a proper pushforward of the geometric
stratum retains more information than the pushforward of its incidence nerve. Arnold classes,
configuration-space periods, and propagator forms belong to the screen coefficient in (33); the screen
coefficient system carries them, while the incidence differential carries forest contractions.

5.7   Homotopy transfer and Feynman trees
Let a deformation retract of ordered chiral complexes be given. Homological perturbation transfers
the structure by summing over the two-coloured forests: vertices carry original operations, internal
edges carry the contracting homotopy, and screen chains carry the chiral coefficient. The resulting
operations satisfy the complete forest boundary identity because Theorem 5.3 is the differential of the
cofibrant resolution.



                                                   39
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


  This is the precise coincidence between Feynman trees and homotopy transfer. The equality is
combinatorial once the propagator and vertex maps have been supplied; the propagator and vertex
maps select the configuration-space forms that represent the transferred operations.

5.8   Renormalised Feynman weights
In perturbative field theory, an edge of a graph carries a propagator and a vertex carries an interaction.
Renormalisation extends the resulting distribution to a compactified configuration space. Boundary
restriction factors through contracted subgraphs.

Proposition 5.5 (Feynman weight morphism). Let a holomorphic–topological BV theory on X × R
have renormalised graph weights extending to a geometric realization of the mixed forest model. Assume
that restriction to every boundary stratum factorizes by graph contraction and that the renormalised
quantum master equation holds. Then the graph weights define a morphism from the mixed cofibrant
operad to the endomorphism operad of the boundary observables.

Proof. Factorisation on a boundary stratum identifies the weight with the composite of the weights
of the contracted graph and the quotient graph. Stokes’ formula, including the BV boundary and
counterterm contributions, is exactly compatibility with the differential of the mixed forest complex.
These two identities are the operadic composition and chain-map axioms.

5.9   Three reversals
Reflection of the transverse line sends an E1 algebra to its opposite. Reversal of an exchange path,
when an E2 enhancement exists, inverts the braid. Verdier duality reverses !- and *-extensions of the
chiral coefficient. These are three different operations. The two-colour grading keeps their domains
visible and prevents one from being inferred from another.

Degeneracies insert the unit in the unreduced model. The ordered chiral bar is the geometric realization

                                        Barord

6.2   The chiral coefficient remains intact
Formula (37) uses the internal differential and the transverse multiplication. Every OPE coefficient
belongs to the factorisation D-module A ⊗ A and is transported through the multiplication morphism
m. The bar face composes adjacent ordered factors, while the chiral coefficient retains the complete
Laurent expansion.
   A residue complex can be paired with the bar as a separate geometric realization. Such a pairing
has the form
                                    X (A) −→ Γ FMI (X), KI ⊗ AI ,

where KI is a chosen complex of forms, currents, or local cohomology classes. The map must specify
