The binomial coefficients arise from translating one formal variable relative to another. Equation
(118) is therefore invisible to a complex that retains only a simple residue and discards jets. The
normal-symbol filtration retains precisely the powers needed to recover the translated expansions.
   At the level of distributions, the same identity is the equality of three expansions of a rational
distribution on the complement of the three diagonals. The chiral operad records the expansions and
their common meromorphic origin.

16.5     Vacuum and conformal structure
A vacuum vector 1 and the creation axiom extend the nonunital structure to a vertex algebra. A
conformal vector ω supplies operators Ln = ω(n+1) with Virasoro commutation relations and L−1 = ∂.
The central charge is the coefficient of the fourth-order pole in

                                                      c/2     2Y (ω, w) ∂w Y (ω, w)
                              Y (ω, z)Y (ω, w) ∼            +          +            .
                                                   (z − w)4   (z − w)2    z−w

The vacuum, translation, and conformal vector are distinct structures. Chiral Koszul duality applies to
the underlying chiral Lie object; conformal blocks and modular anomalies use the conformal structure.

16.6     The classical limit
Let F• V be a filtration compatible with all vertex products, for example the Li filtration. The
associated graded is commutative with a derivation and a lambda bracket. The regular normally
ordered product becomes the commutative product; the first nontrivial singular symbols become the
Poisson vertex bracket.
Theorem 16.3 (Classical symbol operad). There is a canonical morphism from the pole-associated
graded of the chiral operad to the classical operad P cl . An odd Maurer–Cartan element in W (PΠV   cl ) is

a Poisson vertex algebra structure. For a vertex algebra equipped with a compatible filtration, the image
of the principal chiral symbol is the Maurer–Cartan element of the associated Poisson vertex algebra.

Proof. The symbol morphism is Theorem 15.2. The universal Lie superalgebra construction sends
operad morphisms to Lie-superalgebra morphisms and therefore preserves Maurer–Cartan equations.
The classical operad governs Poisson vertex algebras, so its odd square-zero binary operations are
precisely commutative differential products with compatible Lie conformal brackets. Applying the
symbol map to a filtered vertex Maurer–Cartan element gives the associated Poisson vertex operation
[6, 7].

    The theorem makes quantization a lifting problem in the chiral deformation complex. Obstructions
lie in the second cohomology of the classical differential; choices of lifts lie in the first. This is ordinary
Maurer–Cartan obstruction theory applied to the filtered operad.

                                                          83
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


16.7    Deformations
Fix a vertex operation X. The complex
                                                                 
                                           W ch (ΠV ), dX = [X, −]                                  (119)

controls formal deformations. If

                                    Xℏ = X + ℏX1 + ℏ2 X2 + · · · ,

then the coefficient of ℏ in [Xℏ , Xℏ ] = 0 says dX X1 = 0. Gauge-equivalent first-order deformations
differ by a coboundary. The second-order obstruction is the class of 21 [X1 , X1 ] in H 2 .
    This cohomology is the appropriate home of local deformation classes. Hochschild cohomology of a
mode algebra and cohomology of the moduli space of curves arise from separate complexes; explicit
comparison maps relate them to chiral deformation cohomology in the settings where those maps are
constructed.

16.8    Homotopy chiral structures
Let P∞ → P ch be a cofibrant resolution and let C∞ be a conilpotent cooperadic model carrying its
canonical twisting morphism to P∞ . A homotopy chiral algebra is an algebra over P∞ , equivalently a
Maurer–Cartan element in the completed convolution L∞ -algebra

                                             HomΣ (C∞ , EndV ).

The higher operations are indexed by cells in a resolution of collision composition. Their identities are
the components of the Maurer–Cartan equation. This is the correct setting for transferred structures
and for BRST models before cohomology concentration.
   A strict vertex algebra is a homotopy chiral algebra whose higher generators vanish in a chosen
model. Homotopy transfer preserves the quasi-isomorphism type and generally produces a homotopy-
coherent model. Therefore a transferred higher operation is a property of the contraction as well as of
the quasi-isomorphism class; its cohomology class is the invariant obstruction.

16.9    The meaning of “chiral Koszul”
The adjective “chiral” refers to the monoidal category and the diagonal operation. The phrase “chiral
Koszul duality” is reserved in this paper for the Chevalley–primitives equivalence on the Ran space. A
vertex algebra is a formal-coordinate manifestation of a chiral algebra. A quadratic presentation of
a vertex algebra may admit a smaller quadratic dual, but that is an additional Koszul property, an
additional presentation-level Koszul property.




                                                     84
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


17     The chiral Chevalley coalgebra on the Ran space
17.1    The Ran space
The Ran space of X is the prestack of nonempty finite subsets of X:

                                       Ran(X) =      colim        XI .
                                                   I∈Finsurj,op

A point of X I maps to the subset of its coordinate values; coincident coordinates are allowed in
the colimit. The transition maps are partial diagonals. Thus the Ran space remembers every finite
collision simultaneously.
   The category D(Ran X) carries two monoidal structures. The star tensor product is induced by
union of finite subsets along arbitrary unions of finite subsets. The chiral tensor product is induced by
union restricted to disjoint subsets. Chiral Lie algebras are Lie algebra objects for the chiral tensor
product. Factorization objects are those whose restriction to disjoint configurations is the tensor
product of their one-point pieces.
   The Ran prestack is the colimit of Cartesian powers along diagonal maps, and its D-module
category is controlled by the Cartesian powers X I and their diagonal transition maps. Every formula
in the preceding sections is therefore a labelled chart of a Ran object.

17.2    Chevalley chains
Let g be a chiral Lie algebra. Its chiral Chevalley chains are the cofree commutative coalgebra on g[1]
with the coderivation induced by the chiral bracket:
                                                                          
                               coChevch (g) = coSymch (g[1]), dg + dµ .                               (120)

Pullback to X I gives the partition decomposition of Theorem 13.4. The factorization coproduct is
induced by the decomposition of a finite set into disjoint subsets.
   The primitive filtration is the filtration by the number of factors. Its associated graded is the
cofree commutative coalgebra with only the internal differential. This filtration is complete in the
conilpotent direction: every element has finite primitive length in a labelled finite component.

Proposition 17.1 (Geometric realization of Chevalley chains). The forest incidence objects WIFM (g)
assemble, under disjoint union of labels and diagonal pullback, into a simplicial resolution of
coChevch (g) on Ran(X).

Proof. Let ιI : CIcoll (g) → WIFM (g) insert the terminal corolla in every block. Then ϵI ιI = id. For a
surjection of label sets ϕ : I ↠ J, let Tϕ be the canonical Ran transition on the partition collision
complexes and define the transition on the resolution by

                                             Teϕ = ιJ Tϕ ϵI .

Because ϵι = id, the maps Teϕ are strictly functorial under composition, and ϵI is a natural transfor-
mation. Disjoint union tensors the tree complexes and preserves terminal corollas, so these transitions
are compatible with the factorization coproduct. They therefore assemble the labelled resolutions over
the colimit presentation of Ran(X).


                                                   85
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


17.3    Primitives
Let C be a coaugmented conilpotent commutative coalgebra in the chiral monoidal category. The
primitive functor is defined invariantly as the right adjoint to the chiral Chevalley functor. With the
Chevalley convention of (120), its value is shifted by [−1] to produce the corresponding chiral Lie
object.
   In a strict conilpotent dg-coalgebra model, let

                                          C = Cofib(1 −→ C)

be the coaugmentation coideal and let ∆ : C → C ⊗ch C be the reduced coproduct. When strict
primitives compute the derived right adjoint—in particular for cofree conilpotent models and their
fibrant replacements—one may represent the primitive complex by
                                                                  
                                                        ∆
                                  Prim(C) ≃ Fib C −
                                                  → C ⊗ch C .                                     (121)

For a cofree conilpotent coalgebra this complex is the cogenerator complex.
   The Chevalley and primitive constructions form an adjunction
                                                   ch,conil
                       coChevch : Liech (X) ⇄ CoalgCom      (Ran X) : Prim[−1].                   (122)

The shift depends on the Chevalley convention. The substantive point is that the bracket and the
reduced coproduct are Koszul-dual operations.

17.4    Francis–Gaitsgory duality
The chiral monoidal category on the Ran space is pro-nilpotent with respect to cardinality. This
property removes the convergence obstruction that ordinary Lie–commutative Koszul duality encounters
in an arbitrary monoidal category.

Theorem 17.2 (Francis–Gaitsgory chiral Koszul duality). In the factorization and conilpotent
subcategories of the pro-nilpotent chiral monoidal category of D-modules on Ran(X) considered by
Francis and Gaitsgory, the chiral Chevalley functor and the primitive functor are inverse equivalences
between chiral Lie algebras and conilpotent factorization coalgebras:

                                     Liech (X) ≃ CoFactconil (Ran X).                             (123)

The unit and counit of the adjunction are equivalences.

   This theorem is the invariant core of chiral Koszul duality [38]. It is stronger than the statement
that a quadratic algebra is Koszul. The equivalence is intrinsic and presentation-free.

17.5    Chiral homology
The chiral homology of g on a complete curve X is the de Rham cohomology of its factorization
coalgebra:                   Z    ch
                                       g := RΓdR (Ran X, coChevch (g)).                           (124)
                                 X



                                                   86
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The cardinality filtration produces a spectral sequence whose first page is a sum of cohomologies
of configuration spaces with coefficients in external tensor powers of g. The collision differential is
induced by the chiral bracket.
   For an ordinary Lie algebra g0 placed constantly on a point, this reduces to the usual Chevalley
complex. For a current chiral algebra on a curve, it becomes a global version of Lie algebra homology
with local singularities. For modules inserted at marked points, the pointed collision complex computes
derived coinvariants.

17.6    The factorization envelope
Beilinson and Drinfeld associate to a chiral Lie algebra its chiral enveloping algebra U ch (g). Its
factorization structure is generated by the same local operation µ, but the output is associative rather
than coalgebraic. The Chevalley coalgebra and the enveloping factorization algebra play different
roles:
                           coChevch (g) resolves primitives and homology,
                               U ch (g) acts on modules and defines centers.
The Poincare–Birkhoff–Witt theorem identifies the associated graded of U ch (g) with the symmetric
chiral algebra under the standard filtration. This is a theorem about the enveloping construction, a
statement about the chiral enveloping functor and its PBW filtration.

17.7    Analytic realization
Over C, regular holonomic D-modules correspond under Riemann–Hilbert to constructible sheaves.
The chiral operation becomes a morphism of constructible objects with singular support along diagonals.
On the open configuration space, horizontal sections form a local system. The difference between the
algebraic and analytic descriptions is then the difference between a regular singular connection and its
monodromy representation.
   This passage explains the ordered theory. The Ran coalgebra retains symmetric factorization.
Pulling its analytic realization to labelled configurations and taking horizontal sections retains paths
and braid monodromy. The two constructions are related and preserve distinct information: de Rham
cohomology forgets a chosen basis of horizontal solutions, while monodromy acts on it.

17.8    Completion and cardinality
The Ran category is pro-nilpotent by cardinality, but additional completions may enter from conformal
weight, pole order, or infinite-dimensional state spaces. These are independent filtrations. If CN is an
inverse system of finite-window complexes with surjective transition maps on cohomology, then the
Milnor sequence

                   0 −→ lim 1 H m−1 (CN ) −→ H m (lim CN ) −→ lim H m (CN ) −→ 0                  (125)
                        ←−                        ←−          ←−
shows that completion is exact when the derived limit vanishes. This is the precise content of a
Mittag–Leffler argument.
   The abstract equivalence of Theorem 17.2 is formulated directly in the pro-nilpotent Ran category.
Completion enters when one replaces abstract Ran objects by explicit infinite products of modes or
conformal weights.


                                                  87
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


17.9    The Beilinson hierarchy
The objects generated by one chiral operation form a hierarchy:
                                           Z ch
                  g 7−→ coChevch (g) 7−→          g,        g 7−→ U ch (g) 7−→ Mod(U ch (g)).           (126)
                                            X

The first branch is coalgebraic and homological. The second is algebraic and representation-theoretic.
Centers, traces, and conformal blocks are built from the second branch; primitives and factorization
homology are built from the first. Chiral Koszul duality connects the primitive object to its factorization
coalgebra while preserving the algebraic and coalgebraic branches.


18     Chiral Koszul duality, bar–cobar, and presentations
18.1    Three meanings of Koszul duality
The phrase “Koszul duality” is used for three related constructions.
   The first is the Lie–commutative equivalence of Theorem 17.2. This is chiral Koszul duality in the
strict sense of this paper.
   The second is bar–cobar reconstruction for augmented associative or operadic algebras. An
augmented algebra A has a conilpotent bar coalgebra B(A), and a conilpotent coalgebra C has a
cobar algebra Ω(C). Under the usual conilpotence and completeness conditions, the unit and counit
are equivalences.
   The third is quadratic Koszul duality. A quadratic presentation A = T (V )/(R) determines a
smaller coalgebra A¡ generated by sV with corelations s2 R. The algebra is Koszul when the canonical
map A¡ → B(A) is a quasi-isomorphism.
   The first construction is presentation-free. The second is a universal resolution. The third is a
property of a presentation. They coincide on the locus where the canonical comparison maps are
equivalences.

18.2    Twisting morphisms
Let C be a conilpotent dg coalgebra and A an augmented dg algebra in a complete symmetric monoidal
category. A twisting morphism is a degree-one map τ : C → A satisfying

                                        dτ + µA (τ ⊗ τ )∆C = 0.                                         (127)

This is a Maurer–Cartan equation in the convolution algebra Hom(C, A). It determines a coalgebra
map C → B(A) and an algebra map Ω(C) → A.

Theorem 18.1 (Fundamental theorem of twisting morphisms). Assume that the bar and cobar
constructions are defined and that the coradical filtrations converge. For a twisting morphism τ : C → A,
the following are equivalent:

  (i) the map C → B(A) is a quasi-isomorphism;

 (ii) the map Ω(C) → A is a quasi-isomorphism;

 (iii) the left twisted tensor product A ⊗τ C is acyclic;

                                                       88
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


(iv) the right twisted tensor product C ⊗τ A is acyclic.

   The theorem is classical over a field and extends to filtered chiral settings when the monoidal
products preserve the relevant limits and the comparison spectral sequences converge [55]. The
formal equation is universal; the chiral hypotheses specify the ambient limits, tensor products, and
convergence required to interpret it (127).

18.3    Quadratic recognition
Let A = T (V )/(R) be a connected weight-graded quadratic algebra. Its quadratic coalgebra A¡ comes
with a canonical twisting morphism. The usual Koszul criterion is diagonal concentration of bar
homology.

Theorem 18.2 (Quadratic Koszul criterion). For a connected weight-graded quadratic algebra A, the
following are equivalent:

  (i) A¡ → B(A) is a quasi-isomorphism;

 (ii) Ω(A¡ ) → A is a quasi-isomorphism;

(iii) TorA
         p,q (C, C) = 0 for p ̸= q;

(iv) the quadratic twisted tensor complexes are acyclic.

Proof. The canonical quadratic twisting morphism A¡ → A gives comparison maps from the quadratic
coalgebra to the bar and from its cobar to A. The fundamental theorem of twisting morphisms
identifies either comparison with acyclicity of the left and right twisted tensor products. Filtering
the bar by homological and internal weight identifies the homology of those complexes with the
off-diagonal groups TorA
                       p,q (C, C). Thus the four conditions are equivalent [55].

    For a chiral algebra presented by generators and relations, the same criterion applies in each
finite window once the free chiral algebra, the quadratic relation object, and the filtered comparison
have been defined. Each universal affine, Virasoro, lattice, or W -algebra becomes Koszul after a
presentation and spectral-sequence argument verifies the criterion in its finite windows and completion.

18.4    PBW deformations
A PBW theorem identifies the associated graded of a filtered algebra with a prescribed graded algebra.
The bar comparison additionally incorporates the lower-order linear and constant terms: lower-order
linear and constant terms alter the Koszul differential and may introduce curvature. The invariant
comparison statement is the following filtered lemma.

Criterion 18.3 (Filtered Koszul comparison). Let q : C → B(A) be a morphism from a filtered
conilpotent coalgebra to the bar coalgebra of a filtered augmented algebra. Assume that

 (a) the filtrations on C and B(A) are exhaustive, separated, complete, and preserved by q;

 (b) the induced map gr q : gr C → B(gr A) is a quasi-isomorphism;

 (c) the two comparison spectral sequences converge strongly.


                                                  89
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Then q is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof. The cone of q inherits a complete exhaustive filtration. Its associated graded is the cone of gr q
and is acyclic by hypothesis. Strong convergence therefore gives zero cohomology for the filtered cone,
so q is a quasi-isomorphism.

    For a quadratic-linear-constant PBW deformation of a quadratic Koszul algebra, the appropriate
C is the nonhomogeneous or curved Koszul coalgebra. The PBW compatibility identities ensure that
its coderivation squares to the prescribed curvature, and the associated-graded comparison is the
ordinary quadratic Koszul resolution. Thus PBW, classical Koszulness, and the curved compatibility
identities together imply the filtered comparison. The three ingredients together give the filtered
quasi-isomorphism.
    In a vertex algebra, an application must additionally control derivatives, infinitely many modes,
and the completion in conformal weight or pole order. A family is declared Koszul after its PBW
spanning set has been combined with the filtered coalgebra, curved compatibility identities, and strong
convergence.

18.5    Verdier duality and a dual algebra
The chiral Chevalley coalgebra is a factorization coalgebra. When its labelled components are Verdier
dualizable, duality exchanges coalgebra maps with algebra maps. Let DRan denote Verdier duality on
an appropriate constructible or holonomic subcategory.

Proposition 18.4 (Verdier-dual algebra). Let C be a factorization coalgebra whose finite labelled
components are Verdier dualizable, and suppose Verdier duality commutes with the factorization
restriction maps. Then
                                         C ∨ := DRan C
is a factorization algebra. If biduality holds componentwise and is compatible with factorization, then
C → DRan DRan C is an equivalence of factorization coalgebras.

   Applied to C = coChevch (g), this produces a Verdier-dual factorization algebra. Identifying it with
a familiar vertex algebra, a reflected level, or a quantum group is a separate recognition theorem.

18.6    Completion
Let F 1 A be the augmentation ideal. The completed bar and cobar constructions use products over
tensor length rather than direct sums. Their differentials converge when they raise the filtration. The
completed counit
                                           Ωb B(A)
                                              b     −→ A
is an equivalence for complete augmented algebras in the standard pronilpotent setting.
    Curved algebras require another distinction. If a curvature element m0 is present, the unary
operation satisfies m21 = [m0 , −] as the inner derivation [m0 , −]. A central scalar produces the
zero inner derivation, whereas a noncentral curvature element produces the inner derivation [m0 , −].
Curved bar–cobar duality belongs to the coderived and contraderived categories of Positselski [60].
The vertex-algebra chiral differential remains square-zero and the curved Koszul differential belongs
to the separate curved comparison object.


                                                   90
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


18.7     Koszul dual modules
A twisting morphism τ : C → A defines adjoint functors between A-modules and C-comodules.
Under the hypotheses of Theorem 18.1, these functors give an equivalence on the appropriate derived
categories. For a quadratic Koszul algebra, the resolution is linear and yields the familiar exchange of
Ext and Tor.
   In the chiral setting, modules are factorization modules at marked points. The pointed collision
complex is the bar resolution. A module-level Koszul duality therefore requires the algebra comparison
and the compatibility of marked-point factorization. Fusion products and conformal blocks are
preserved only when a braided tensor equivalence of module categories has been established.

18.8     Maurer–Cartan moduli
Twisting morphisms form a Maurer–Cartan space in a convolution dg Lie or L∞ -algebra. Gauge
equivalence is homotopy of twisting data. The tangent complex at τ is the twisted convolution complex

                                          (Hom(C, A), d + [τ, −]) .

Its first cohomology contains infinitesimal deformations and its second contains obstructions.
    This is the natural moduli object associated with Koszul presentations. Koszulness itself is the
open condition that the comparison cone be acyclic in a finite perfect family; globally it is a derived
condition on the vanishing of that cone. The bar coalgebra is classified by its structured comparison
cone and Maurer–Cartan datum; scalar invariants arise by applying specified traces.

18.9     Recognition data for the principal implications
The following structured data turn the corresponding coarse properties into valid recognition theorems:

     PBW presentation, curved comparison, convergence                     =⇒ Koszul,
                                                            

     C2 -cofiniteness, linear bar resolution                              =⇒ Koszul,
                                           

     Verdier dualizability, biduality datum                               =⇒ self-duality,
                                               

     braided monodromy, fibre functor, presentation recognition           =⇒ specified Yangian,
                                                                      

     central-charge equality, OPE-compatible tensor equivalence =⇒ equivalent chiral algebras.
                                                                      

                                                                                              (128)
Each line names the additional structure that upgrades a numerical or filtered shadow to a theorem
about the full object.


19      Duality, Hochschild theory, and the derived center
19.1     The associative envelope of the centre
A chiral Lie algebra g supplies a bracket. Its chiral enveloping factorisation algebra U ch (g) supplies
the associative multiplication and bimodule category used by Hochschild theory. The derived centre is
formed in this associative envelope.




                                                     91
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Definition 19.1 (Derived chiral center). For a chiral Lie algebra g for which the chiral enveloping
factorization algebra is defined, set
                                                                     
                                  der
                                 Zch  (g) := HH∗Fact U ch (g), U ch (g) .                          (129)

For an associative factorization algebra A, write Zch
                                                   der (A) = HH∗
                                                               Fact (A, A).

   Hochschild cochains are derived endomorphisms of the diagonal bimodule. The bar resolution
supplies a concrete model. In the chiral setting the resolution is supported on configurations with
an incoming and an outgoing boundary component, and insertion of configurations produces brace
operations.

19.2    The E2 structure
The higher Deligne theorem gives Hochschild cochains of an E1 algebra an E2 structure. The same
statement holds for associative factorization algebras in a suitable symmetric monoidal category. The
product is cup product; the shifted Lie bracket is the Gerstenhaber bracket; higher braces encode
homotopies.
Theorem 19.2 (Derived center structure). Let A be an associative factorization algebra whose
                                                            der (A) is an E algebra. Its action on A
bimodule category admits the diagonal bar resolution. Then Zch             2
is universal among homotopy-coherent central actions.

Proof. The endomorphism operad of the diagonal bar resolution carries brace operations obtained
by inserting one bar configuration into another. The brace identities are the boundary identities of
nested insertions, and the Hochschild differential is the brace commutator with the multiplication of
A. The resulting brace algebra is an algebra over a chain model of E2 , giving the first assertion. The
two-coloured Swiss-cheese operad acts on the pair (Zch   der (A), A). Its universal property identifies a

homotopy-coherent central action of an E2 algebra B on A with a morphism B → Zch         der (A), proving

universality.

   The universal property is algebraic. An object B acting on A through the Swiss-cheese operad
determines a morphism of E2 algebras

                                                  der
                                            B −→ Zch  (A).                                         (130)

The map sends a closed operation to the family of open operations obtained by inserting it next to
boundary inputs.

19.3    The center and a physical bulk
Let Obsbulk be the factorization algebra of local observables of a field theory with boundary algebra
A. The bulk-to-boundary operator product defines a map

                                        Obsbulk |∂ −→ Zch
                                                       der
                                                           (A).                                    (131)

A quasi-isomorphism in (131) reconstructs the bulk local observables from the boundary algebra. A
product bulk T ⊗ D with trivial boundary action from D has the same boundary algebra and centre,
while ObsD appears in the homotopy fibre of the comparison map.

                                                   92
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Separation theorem 19.3 (Open–closed reconstruction criterion). Tensoring a bulk theory with
a factor D whose boundary action is the unit preserves the boundary ordered chiral algebra and its
derived centre. The bulk observable algebra becomes

                                             Obsbulk ⊗ ObsD ,

and the additional factor maps to the unit central action. Hence open–closed reconstruction is equivalent
to the assertion that (131) is a quasi-isomorphism, or to an explicit generation theorem that implies
this assertion.

Proof. Factorization observables of a product theory are the tensor product of the two observable
algebras. When the second factor acts through the boundary unit, its bulk-to-boundary map is the
unit map followed by the central action of 1. Thus the boundary algebra and the universal centre
are those of the first factor, while the reduced observables of D survive in the homotopy fibre of the
product comparison. Therefore the boundary reconstructs all local bulk observables precisely when
the comparison map is a quasi-isomorphism; a generation theorem proves the same conclusion by
showing that its cone vanishes.

   This criterion states the exact hypothesis carried by holographic reconstruction from local boundary
operations.

19.4    The annulus and Hochschild homology
Factorization homology of the circle with coefficients in an E1 algebra is Hochschild homology:
                                         Z
                                               A ≃ A ⊗L
                                                      A⊗Aop A.                                     (132)
                                          S1

For a boundary theory, the annulus is obtained by thickening the circle. Its state space is therefore a
trace of the boundary algebra. The pairing between Hochschild cochains and Hochschild chains is the
algebraic form of inserting a bulk local operator into an annulus amplitude.
   A cyclic trace on A thickens planar trees to ribbon graphs and supplies the loop contraction. The
derived centre already carries its E2 structure; the trace adds the operation that forms ribbon genus.

19.5    Morita invariance
Hochschild cochains and chains are Morita invariant. If A and B are derived Morita equivalent
associative factorization algebras, then

                                     HH∗Fact (A, A) ≃ HH∗Fact (B, B)

as E2 algebras, and their Hochschild homologies agree.
   A Morita equivalence induced by Koszul duality therefore produces an equivalence of centres. The
proof passes through the equivalence of module categories and preserves the full E2 structure.




                                                   93
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


19.6    A small convolution model
Assume A is augmented, complete, and Koszul with a perfect bar coalgebra B(A). The diagonal bar
resolution identifies Hochschild cochains with a twisted convolution complex

                                       der
                                      Zch  (A) ≃ Homτ (B(A), A) ,                                    (133)

where the differential is
                                    df = dA f − (−1)|f | f dB + [τ, f ].
The braces insert one coalgebra input into another before applying f . This model is finite and explicit
when the bar coalgebra is locally finite.
   The formula (133) explains the relation between deformation theory and the center. A first-order
associative deformation is a degree-two Hochschild cocycle; its obstruction is the Gerstenhaber square.
The chiral deformation complex of Equation (119) and the factorization Hochschild complex may
be compared, but they govern different structures: one deforms the chiral operation, the other the
associative factorization envelope.

19.7    Verdier duality and centers
Verdier duality exchanges !- and *-extensions across diagonals. For a perfect factorization algebra A,
duality produces an opposite or dual factorization algebra. The induced map on bimodule categories
reverses left and right actions. Consequently the Hochschild complex of the dual is the dual of the
Hochschild chain complex of A under a Calabi–Yau pairing.
   A degree-d Calabi–Yau structure on A gives a nondegenerate pairing

                                     HHi (A, A) ⊗ HHd−i (A) −→ C.

The degree is determined by the cyclic structure, by the cyclic structure before any moduli-space
realization is chosen. This pairing is the algebraic origin of BV operators on Hochschild cohomology.

19.8    Topological enhancement
The derived centre is E2 in the transverse directions. A coherent null-homotopy of one further real
translation gives an E3 enhancement. A coherent topologization of the whole complex curve supplies
two additional real topological directions and gives an E4 algebra, as in Proposition 9.8. A holomorphic
centre becomes topological after the required translation homotopies have been supplied.
   In three-dimensional Chern–Simons theory, local bulk observables are E3 because the bulk has three
topological directions. Comparison with a boundary centre therefore uses the appropriate intermediate
topologization of the boundary object. In a four-real-dimensional holomorphic–topological theory
whose holomorphic direction is also topologized, the corresponding local structure is E4 . In either
dimension, proving that the bulk-to-centre map is an equivalence is a field-theoretic calculation.

19.9    Centers at critical level
For the universal affine vertex algebra at the critical level, the ordinary vertex-algebra center is large:
the Feigin–Frenkel theorem identifies it with functions on opers for the Langlands dual group on
the formal disc [34]. This centre is a commutative chiral algebra inside the affine algebra. Its fields

                                                    94
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


act on the factorisation Hochschild complex, which additionally contains higher diagonal-bimodule
self-extensions.
    The distinction is visible in degrees. The vertex center consists of fields commuting with all fields
under nonnegative modes. The derived center includes higher self-extensions of the diagonal bimodule.
At noncritical level the ordinary center may be scalar while the derived center remains nontrivial.


Part III
Exchange, reduction, and quantum symmetry
20     Labelled chiral configurations and monodromy
20.1    Exchange geometry and the ordered line
The order in an E1 algebra comes from chambers of configurations on a real line. The monodromy in
this section comes instead from moving labelled chiral insertion points in the complex curve, or from
an E2 transverse enhancement. The exchange structures below enter through labelled motion in the
curve or through an E2 transverse enhancement.

20.2    Labels retain paths
The labelled configuration space of n points is

                         Conf n (X) = {(x1 , . . . , xn ) ∈ X n | xi ̸= xj for i ̸= j}.

The symmetric group acts freely, and the quotient is the unordered configuration space

                                      UConf n (X) = Conf n (X)/Σn .

For X = C,
                            π1 (Conf n (C)) = Pn ,         π1 (UConf n (C)) = Bn ,
the pure braid and braid groups. A local system on the labelled space therefore retains pure braid
monodromy; an equivariant local system descends to the unordered space and retains full braid
monodromy.
   A symmetric factorization coalgebra carries coherent descent data under all relabelings and diagonals.
Labelled analytic continuation is extra information: it is a representation of the fundamental groupoid
of the configuration space.

20.3    Derived descent
Let M be a chain complex with an action of a finite group G. The homotopy coinvariants are

                                            MhG = M ⊗L
                                                     C[G] C.




                                                      95
R. Lorgat                                                          Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The augmentation ideal IG = ker(C[G] → C) fits into a distinguished triangle

                                     M ⊗L
                                        C[G] IG −→ M −→ MhG .                                        (134)

Thus the homotopy fiber of descent is canonical. In characteristic zero and for finite G, the group
algebra is semisimple, so derived coinvariants agree with ordinary coinvariants. The fiber still records
all nontrivial isotypic components.
    For a braid-group local system, the relevant descent uses the full braid-group algebra before
symmetric descent. One must retain the monodromy representation and its equivariance under the
extension
                                    1 −→ Pn −→ Bn −→ Σn −→ 1.
Discarding the pure braid action loses analytic continuation even when the underlying vector space is
unchanged.

20.4    The descent fiber and collision filtrations
For a coefficient system Mn carrying a Σn -action, define the descent fiber

                                     Cn (M ) = Fib(Mn → Mn,hΣn ).                                    (135)

By (134), it is canonically Mn ⊗L C[Σn ] IΣn . A universal chain model is obtained from the ordinary group
bar resolution of the trivial C[Σn ]-module. In characteristic zero the group algebra is semisimple, so
this derived fiber is represented by the direct sum of the nontrivial isotypic components.
   The proper partition complex is a different universal object. It is the incidence and Koszul complex
of collision partitions, and its top homology is the Lie representation. For an arbitrary Σn -module,
the group-bar construction resolves the descent fibre. When Mn carries a functorial filtration indexed
by collision partitions, an explicit comparison morphism from the partition-incidence complex to the
group-bar model may filter Cn (M ) by binary collisions, ternary collision coherence, and higher strata.
Such a filtration belongs to the coefficient system and must be constructed; it is additional filtered
coefficient data carried by the local system.
   The descent fibre contains the nontrivial isotypic and braid data carried by the coefficient system.
An r-matrix appears when the first-order monodromy of a flat connection has the form required by
the classical Yang–Baxter equation.

20.5    Dual local systems
Let M be an oriented manifold and L a finite-rank local system with monodromy ρ : π1 (M ) → GL(V ).
Its Verdier dual is
                                         DL ∼= L∨ [dim M ],
up to the orientation line. The monodromy of the dual local system is

                                            ρ∨ (γ) = ρ(γ −1 )t .                                     (136)

The inverse occurs because parallel transport on the dual is contragredient; the transpose is the linear
dual.



                                                    96
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Corollary 20.1. Verdier duality sends a braid operator R to the inverse transpose R−t . If the
representation is identified with its dual by a nondegenerate invariant pairing, this becomes inverse
braiding.

   The further notation q 7→ q −1 is justified only when a family of braid operators has been identified
with a quantum-group family parametrized by q.

20.6    Associators
The monodromy of a flat connection on the configuration space of three points depends on comparing
asymptotic zones. A Drinfeld associator is a group-like formal series satisfying the pentagon and
hexagon equations. Associators form a torsor under the Grothendieck–Teichmuller group GRT1 after
the usual normalization [30, 77].
   The pentagon is a nonlinear arity-four equation for a group-like formal series; the hexagon relates
that series to the braiding. Their normalized solutions form the associator torsor.
   In the de Rham realization, the Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov associator is the regularized parallel
transport between tangential base points at 0 and 1 on P1 \ {0, 1, ∞}. Its coefficients are multiple
zeta values. This arithmetic belongs to chiral configuration-space monodromy, to the regularized
monodromy of the chiral configuration-space connection.

20.7    Tannaka reconstruction
Let C be a rigid braided tensor category and let F : C → Vect be a faithful exact fibre functor. The
coend                                    Z   M ∈C
                                     H=             F (M )∨ ⊗ F (M )                               (137)

reconstructs a bialgebra object when F is strong monoidal; rigidity supplies an antipode when the coend
belongs to the finite or continuous-dual class. If F is only quasi-monoidal, the associativity constraint
produces a coassociator and H is a coquasi-bialgebra. The braiding produces a coquasitriangular
structure. Under the relevant finite-dimensional or continuous duality, one obtains a quasitriangular
Hopf or quasi-Hopf algebra, respectively.
Theorem 20.2 (Ordered Tannaka principle). A rigid tensor-compatible monodromy category with a
faithful strong monoidal fibre functor reconstructs a coquasitriangular bialgebra, and a Hopf algebra
whenever the antipode is defined in the chosen finite or continuous-dual class. With a faithful quasi-
monoidal fibre functor, it reconstructs a coquasitriangular coquasi-bialgebra. A quasi-Hopf presentation
becomes a strict Hopf presentation after a twist whose coboundary trivializes the coassociator.

Proof. Form the coend (137). Tensor compatibility defines multiplication of matrix coefficients, while
the monoidal constraint of F defines the coproduct and counit. Rigidity transports evaluation and
coevaluation to the antipode. The categorical braiding defines the universal R-form; the hexagon
identities are the coquasitriangular identities. For a quasi-monoidal functor, the pentagon transports
to the coassociator equation. A twist changes the coassociator by its standard coboundary, so strict
coassociativity is equivalent to a twist trivialization. This is the Tannaka reconstruction theorem for
braided tensor categories.

   The theorem is universal. Identifying the reconstructed object with Uq (g), a Yangian, an ellip-
tic quantum group, or a Hall algebra is a recognition theorem using the specific connection and

                                                     97
R. Lorgat                                                                  Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


representation category.

20.8     Surface braid groups
For a curve of genus g > 0, π1 (Conf n (X)) is a surface pure braid group. Its generators include
collisions and transport around the A- and B-cycles of the curve. Flat connections such as the KZB
connection represent these generators by operators satisfying elliptic braid relations.
   The elliptic theory consists of the genus-zero associator together with quasi-periodicity, the choice
of a dynamical parameter, and the mapping-class-group action supply additional data. At higher
genus, conformal-block monodromy is a representation of the mapping class group of the punctured
surface, and refines the surface braid-group representations.

20.9     What symmetrization preserves
Symmetrization preserves invariant traces, dimensions of invariant subspaces, and any operation that
factors through the quotient. It may preserve nontrivial periods when those periods lie in the invariant
part of the local system. Symmetric descent retains the invariant arithmetic and monodromy and
removes the non-invariant quotient measured by (135).
   For conformal blocks, passing from vectors to their dimension preserves the Verlinde number and
loses the mapping-class-group representation. Passing from a braided category to its Grothendieck
ring preserves fusion coefficients and loses the associator and twists. These are concrete instances of
the same descent.


21      KZ geometry, quantum groups, Gaudin systems, and lines
21.1     The logarithmic connection
Let g be a finite-dimensional complex simple Lie algebra with invariant tensor Ω ∈ g ⊗ g. On
V1 ⊗ · · · ⊗ Vn , define
                                                                                      1
                               ∇KZ = d − ℏ          Ωij d log(zi − zj ),     ℏ=                              (138)
                                              X
                                                                                          .
                                              i<j
                                                                                   k + h∨

The parameter depends on the normalization of the invariant form; the shifted level is invariantly
determined by the Sugawara construction.
   The infinitesimal braid relations are

                         [Ωij , Ωkl ] = 0 if {i, j} ∩ {k, l} = ∅,          [Ωij , Ωik + Ωjk ] = 0            (139)

for distinct i, j, k.

Theorem 21.1 (Flatness of the KZ connection). The connection (138) is flat.

Proof. The logarithmic forms are closed, so for the convention ∇ = d−A the curvature is A∧A. Terms
with disjoint index pairs vanish by the first relation in (139). For every triple i, j, k, the remaining
terms factor as the Arnold relation

                        d log zij ∧ d log zjk + d log zjk ∧ d log zki + d log zki ∧ d log zij = 0

                                                            98
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


times the second infinitesimal braid relation. Hence the curvature vanishes.

   This proof displays the exact division of labor. The differential forms supply the Arnold identity.
The coefficient tensor supplies the infinitesimal braid identity. Their tensor-product cancellation is
the flatness equation.

21.2    From affine currents to KZ
For the affine vertex algebra Vk (g), current fields satisfy

                                                      k (a, b)   J [a,b] (w)
                                  J a (z)J b (w) ∼             +             .                          (140)
                                                     (z − w)2     z−w

Ward identities for primary insertions convert the simple-pole action into the differential equation
(138); the double pole and the Sugawara stress tensor produce the shift by h∨ . The KZ connection
is therefore the representation-theoretic projection of the affine chiral operation to the bundle of
conformal blocks.

21.3    Drinfeld–Kohno
Let q = exp(πiℏ) in the standard convention. The Drinfeld–Kohno theorem identifies the braided
tensor category defined by KZ monodromy with the category of finite-dimensional modules over
the Drinfeld–Jimbo quantum group Uq (g), with an associator determined by KZ parallel transport
[51, 30, 50].

Theorem 21.2 (Drinfeld–Kohno). For generic ℏ, the braid-group representations obtained from the
monodromy of the KZ connection are naturally equivalent, as braided tensor data, to those obtained
from the universal R-matrix of Uq (g) with q = eπiℏ .

Source. Drinfeld constructs the quasi-Hopf algebra whose associator is the regularized KZ transport
and proves its twist equivalence with the Drinfeld–Jimbo quantization; Kohno identifies the resulting
braid representations with KZ monodromy. The Kazhdan–Lusztig tensor equivalence supplies the
corresponding affine representation-theoretic form. With the invariant-form normalization used in
(138), the parameter relation is q = eπiℏ [30, 51, 50].

    The theorem is the rigorous statement that a quantum group is present in the ordered affine theory.
It includes the associator. The Hopf or quasi-Hopf algebra is reconstructed from the braided category
and fibre functor.

21.4    The classical r-matrix
The one-form in (138) has coefficient

                                                                Ωij
                                         rij (zi − zj ) = ℏ           .                                 (141)
                                                              zi − zj

The flatness equation at first order in ℏ is the classical Yang–Baxter equation. The monodromy is
a path-ordered exponential of the connection; its semiclassical expansion begins with the classical
r-matrix.


                                                       99
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   The same rational kernel appears in several constructions once their normalizations are matched:
the KZ connection, the rational Gaudin model, and the line-operator OPE of four-dimensional Chern–
Simons theory. The equality is a theorem only after the coefficient spaces and coupling constants have
been identified.

21.5    Gaudin Hamiltonians
Fix distinct points z1 , . . . , zn . The Gaudin Hamiltonians are

                                                           Ωij

The infinitesimal braid relations imply
                                               [Hi , Hj ] = 0.
They are the residues of the KZ connection in the zi variable. Their commutativity is therefore
another coefficient of flatness.
   For highest-weight modules, the Bethe ansatz constructs simultaneous eigenvectors from critical
points of a master function. In the simplest sl2 case, Bethe roots ta satisfy
                                      n
                                            λi             2

The Schechtman–Varchenko hypergeometric solutions of the KZ equations relate these critical points

flat connection.

21.6    Four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory
Costello–Witten–Yamazaki construct a four-dimensional gauge theory that is topological in two
directions and holomorphic in one complex direction. Wilson lines at distinct holomorphic positions
have an operator product governed by a spectral-parameter R-matrix. In the rational case, the algebra
of line operators is a Yangian; trigonometric and elliptic choices produce quantum loop and elliptic
structures [19, 20].
    The spectral parameter is geometric: it is the difference of holomorphic positions of the lines.
The Yang–Baxter equation follows from isotopy of three lines. The one-loop computation fixes the
first correction to the R-matrix. This is a physical construction of ordered monodromy, the physical
realization whose comparison with chiral Koszul duality is supplied by the boundary functor.

21.7    Yangians and recognition
A Yangian Yℏ (g) is a filtered deformation of U (g[t]) with a rational R-matrix on suitable representa-
tions. Recognition of a reconstructed quasi-bialgebra as a Yangian uses a PBW theorem, evaluation
representations, the rational difference property, and a presentation theorem showing that the listed
relations generate the complete kernel.

Criterion 21.3 (Yangian recognition). Let H be a filtered quasi-bialgebra reconstructed from an
ordered tensor category. If


                                                    100
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


  (i) gr H ∼
           = U (g[t]) as a bialgebra;

 (ii) the fundamental evaluation modules are faithful on the completed algebra;

 (iii) their R-matrices agree with the rational Yangian R-matrices;

 (iv) the defining RTT or Drinfeld relations generate the full kernel of the representation map;

then H is isomorphic, up to the associator twist already present in the tensor functor, to the corre-
sponding completed Yangian.

   The criterion states exactly what an ordered chiral construction must prove before the word
“Yangian” is justified.

21.8    Elliptic transport
On an elliptic curve, the KZB connection replaces d log(zi − zj ) by elliptic kernels with prescribed
quasi-periodicity. Its flatness uses Fay identities and dynamical correction terms. The monodromy
represents the elliptic braid group and the modular group. Depending on the setting, reconstruction
leads to elliptic or dynamical quantum groups.
   Rational, trigonometric, and elliptic quantum groups arise from the corresponding rational, cylin-
drical, and elliptic connection geometries and are related by the degeneration maps of those curves
and coefficient categories. Their relation is controlled by limits of the curve and of the coefficient
category.

21.9    Knots and ribbon traces
A ribbon category assigns invariants to framed links by composing braidings, duality maps, and
twists. The Reshetikhin–Turaev construction applied to Uq (g) produces quantum link invariants [63].
Chern–Simons theory supplies the physical path-integral interpretation. The KZ connection supplies
a monodromy realization of the same braided tensor category.
   The closure of a braid uses a categorical trace. This is the precise point at which ordered
monodromy becomes a scalar knot invariant. The scalar is the categorical trace of the individual
braid representation, while the full representation retains every braid operator.

21.10    Duality of monodromy
By (136), dualizing the KZ local system sends the monodromy to its inverse transpose. Under the
invariant form on representations and the Drinfeld–Kohno equivalence, this agrees with the dual
quantum-group representation and inverse braiding. A level reflection such as k 7→ −k − 2h∨ has the
same effect on the parameter ℏ = (k + h∨ )−1 , namely ℏ 7→ −ℏ, hence q 7→ q −1 . This is a statement
about the KZ family. For a general chiral Koszul pair, the analogous inversion is established by
constructing the dual local system and comparing its fibre functor.




                                                 101
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


22     Quantum line defects and meromorphic tensor categories
22.1   Fusion and planar exchange
Let A ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X)). Its modules can be placed in a fixed order along the transverse line.
Relative tensor product gives fusion:

                                        M ⋆A N := M ⊗L
                                                     A N.

Associativity follows from excision for three consecutive intervals. The configuration space of two
subintervals has two order chambers. A path between the chambers appears after the transverse line
is thickened to a plane, and its parallel transport gives a map M ⋆A N → N ⋆A M .

Separation theorem 22.1 (Planar enhancement criterion). Let A be an associative algebra con-
centrated in cohomological degree zero. An E2 refinement whose induced degree-zero product equals
the product of A forces that product to be commutative. Hence matrix algebras and free associative
algebras acquire exchange through an enlarged derived, categorical, or meromorphic structure rather
than through a discrete E2 refinement of the same multiplication.

Proof. The homology multiplication of an E2 algebra is graded commutative. Concentration in degree
zero turns graded commutativity into ordinary commutativity. The planar configuration space supplies
the geometric refinement: its exchange paths act on a category of modules or on a meromorphic local
system while preserving the original ordered fusion inside each chamber.

  Fusion belongs to the ordered chiral algebra. Planar transport, the Hochschild centre, or a
meromorphic local system supplies exchange.

22.2   The meromorphic line category
Let L be a category of line defects varying over the spectral curve X. For distinct spectral points,
fusion is defined and is compatible with factorization. An exchange structure consists of isomorphisms

                           RL,M (x, y) : Lx ⋆ My −→ My ⋆ Lx ,      x ̸= y,

which are horizontal for the D-module connection and satisfy the braid relation on Conf 3 (X).

Definition 22.2 (Meromorphic tensor category). A meromorphic tensor category on X consists of a
factorization category L on the ordered configuration spaces of X, an associative fusion product on
disjoint ordered blocks, and a braid-group descent datum on the locus of distinct points. The descent
datum is meromorphic along the collision divisors and satisfies the pentagon and hexagon identities.

   The associator compares the three asymptotic regions of four ordered insertions. The braiding
compares two chambers after a transverse exchange. The pole of the exchange operator is a chiral
singularity; its homotopy class is topological.

Theorem 22.3 (Line-category recognition). Let T be a renormalized quantum field theory on X × R2
that is holomorphic on X and topological on R2 . Assume that the fusion and exchange Ward identities
are anomaly-free on a chosen class of line defects, that their morphism complexes are perfect over
the meromorphic functions on X, that the line OPE extends meromorphically across the collision


                                                 102
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


divisors, and that the class is closed under fusion and duality. Then these line defects form a rigid
meromorphic tensor category in the sense of Theorem 22.2.

Proof. Fusion is factorization in one transverse direction. Associativity is isotopy of three parallel
lines. The exchange of two lines is a path in the configuration space of two points in the transverse
plane. Isotopy of three exchange paths gives the braid relation. Isotopy of decompositions of four
lines gives the pentagon; compatibility of exchange with fusion gives the hexagons. Holomorphic
dependence on spectral position and locality of counterterms make the structure meromorphic along
diagonals. Dual line orientations give categorical duals under the perfectness hypothesis.

22.3    The centre and categorical exchange
The internal Hochschild centre Zord,ch (A) is an E2 algebra in FactD(X). This means that its local
multiplication carries the homotopy-coherent exchange of little two-disks. Its ordinary module category
is naturally E1 -monoidal. The Drinfeld centre of that monoidal category, or modules over an E3
enhancement, carries the braided line category.

Corollary 22.4 (Exchange through a complete centre). Suppose the bulk-to-boundary map

                                       βT : Obsbulk −→ Zord,ch (A)

is an equivalence of local E2 algebras. Suppose in addition that a monoidal-affineness theorem identifies
the selected bulk line category with the Drinfeld centre Z(BimodA ), and identifies this categorical centre
with modules over an E3 topological enhancement of Zord,ch (A). Then the corresponding perfect line
categories are equivalent as braided monoidal categories.

Proof. The bulk-to-boundary equivalence identifies the local E2 observable algebra with the internal
centre. The monoidal-affineness equivalences identify, respectively, bulk lines with Z(BimodA ) and
that categorical centre with modules over the E3 enhancement. Composition of these braided monoidal
equivalences gives the asserted equivalence of perfect line categories.

   The hypotheses identify local bulk observables, extended line defects, and the categorical centre in
one comparison diagram. Together they yield the braided equivalence stated in the corollary.

22.4    Tannaka reconstruction and its exact output
Choose a faithful exact meromorphic fibre functor

                                   F : L −→ VectK ,        K = C(X).

The coend                                    Z L∈L
                                      HL =           F (L)∨ ⊗K F (L)

represents tensor automorphisms of F . The tensor product, associator, and braiding produce respec-
tively a coproduct, coassociator, and universal R-form.

Theorem 22.5 (Quantum symmetry of line defects). If F is strong monoidal, HL is a coquasitriangular
bialgebra over K, and rigidity supplies a Hopf antipode in the finite or continuous-dual class. If F is
only quasi-monoidal, HL is a coquasitriangular coquasi-bialgebra whose coassociator is the transported

                                                     103
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


tensor constraint. When the relevant dual exists, the dual objects are quasitriangular Hopf or quasi-
Hopf algebras, respectively. The construction is invariant under braided tensor equivalence compatible
with the chosen fibre functor.

Proof. The coend represents natural matrix coefficients of the fibre functor. A strong tensor con-
straint defines a coassociative coproduct and counit; rigidity defines the antipode in the finite or
continuous-dual class. The braiding defines the universal R-form, and the hexagon identities give
coquasitriangularity. A quasi-monoidal constraint transports the associator to a coassociator satisfying
the pentagon. Braided tensor equivalences compatible with fibre functors induce isomorphisms of the
representing coends and preserve all these maps.

    The theorem reconstructs quantum symmetry intrinsically. RTT, current, elliptic, or Hall relations
together with a PBW theorem recognize the resulting object as a Yangian, quantum loop algebra,
elliptic quantum group, or Hall algebra.

22.5    Rational gauge theory and the Yangian
In four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory on Σ × X, where the theory is topological on Σ and
holomorphic on X, parallel Wilson lines form a meromorphic tensor category. In the rational theory
X = C, perturbation theory produces

                                                      ΩV,W
                                   RV,W (z) = 1 + ℏ        + O(ℏ2 ).                              (144)
                                                       z
The one-loop anomaly of a line is cancelled by deforming the classical current algebra to the Yangian.
The resulting line OPE is the RTT or Drinfeld coproduct of Yℏ (g).
Theorem 22.6 (Rational Wilson-line algebra). For the perturbative Wilson-line categories constructed
in anomaly-free rational four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory by Costello–Witten–Yamazaki, the
algebra of line operators is the completed Yangian deformation of U (g[z]) on the stated class of
representations; exchange is given by the corresponding rational universal R-matrix. The spectral
parameter is the difference of the holomorphic positions. The assertion belongs to the completed formal
ℏ-adic category in which the line anomaly and its cancellation are defined [19, 20].
   The theorem is a field-theoretic recognition theorem. It uses the gauge-theory Ward identities and
anomaly calculation, together with the ordered bar construction and its boundary realization.

22.6    Trigonometric and elliptic geometries
Changing the holomorphic curve changes the meromorphic tensor category. For X = C× , the
trigonometric kernel and its periodicity lead to a quantum loop algebra. For an elliptic curve, quasi-
periodicity and a dynamical parameter lead to elliptic or dynamical quantum groups. These structures
are reconstructed from different meromorphic connections.
Principle 22.7 (The spectral curve selects the quantum group). For the standard rational, trigono-
metric, and elliptic meromorphic line categories, Tannaka reconstruction produces the corresponding
rational, quantum-loop, and elliptic or dynamical quantum symmetries after their presentation-
recognition theorems are applied. A degeneration of the spectral curve induces a degeneration of the
exchange kernel only when the line category, fibre functor, and meromorphic connection extend flatly
over the degenerating family.

                                                 104
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Proof. On the rational curve, the standard meromorphic connection and its representation category
are recognized by the rational RTT or Drinfeld presentation. Periodicity on C× replaces rational
currents by loop currents and yields the quantum-loop presentation. Quasi-periodicity on an elliptic
curve introduces the elliptic or dynamical parameter and yields the elliptic presentation. Tannaka
reconstruction converts each tensor category into its coordinate bialgebra, and the corresponding
presentation theorem identifies its continuous dual. Flat extension of the category, fibre functor, and
connection transports this identification through a degeneration.

22.7    Shifted Yangians and boundary conditions
A line defect may end on a boundary or meet an ’t Hooft defect. Magnetic charge changes the allowed
pole orders of the Drinfeld currents. Algebraically this produces shifted Yangians. Geometrically the
shift is a modification of the factorization module at a marked transverse boundary condition.
   Let µ be a coweight. A shifted object is represented by a pointed module bar

                                  Barord
                                     X (M− , A, M+ ) = M− ⊗A M+ ,
                                                           L


where the endpoint modules encode the two boundary charges. The unshifted algebra is recovered
when both modules are the augmentation boundary.

Criterion 22.8 (Shifted recognition). A line algebra is identified with a shifted Yangian by matching
the following data: the coweight filtration of currents, the shifted coproduct, the PBW associated graded,
the fundamental line modules, and the rational exchange kernel. The coweight filtration and shifted
coproduct determine the shift beyond the unshifted R-matrix.

Proof. The matched current filtration and coproduct define a homomorphism from the shifted Drinfeld
presentation to the line algebra. The fundamental line modules and rational exchange kernel identify
the images of the generators and the shift. The PBW comparison identifies the associated graded
map with an isomorphism; completeness then makes the filtered homomorphism an isomorphism. The
filtration and coproduct distinguish different coweight shifts that share the same unshifted binary
kernel.

22.8    KZ and gauge-theory exchange
For affine conformal blocks, the KZ connection gives braid monodromy with coupling (k + h∨ )−1 .
For four-dimensional Chern–Simons lines, the gauge coupling fixes the rational R-matrix. These two
constructions are related through the Drinfeld–Kohno theorem after the representation categories and
normalizations have been identified. The equality of kernels is established by the tensor equivalence
and normalization comparison: the affine theory is a two-dimensional chiral theory, whereas the gauge
theory is a four-dimensional holomorphic–topological theory.

Theorem 22.9 (Comparison through the line category). Let LKZ be a rigid braided tensor subcategory
of the Drinfeld–KZ category at a nonresonant shifted level, and let Lgauge be a rigid Wilson-line
subcategory of a rational gauge theory. A braided tensor equivalence matching tensor generators and
the first-order Casimir kernel identifies their braid monodromies. Under compatible Tannaka fibre
functors, both reconstruct the same quasitriangular Hopf or quasi-Hopf object up to the transported
associator twist.


                                                   105
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Proof. A tensor equivalence preserves the coend reconstruction. Matching the Casimir coefficient fixes
the formal deformation parameter. The braid operator on tensor generators then agrees, and rigidity
extends the agreement to the generated category. The associator records the difference between strict
and KZ tensor structures.

   The theorem isolates the actual comparison datum. The tensor equivalence upgrades similarity of
rational kernels to an identity of the corresponding line categories.

22.9    Koszul duality of line couplings
Let A be the algebra of local observables near a line and let ε : A → 1 describe the distinguished
trivial line. Formal deformations supported on that line are governed by the derived deformation
theory of the augmentation module. Its endomorphism algebra is the Koszul dual

                                         A! = RHomA (1, 1).

Theorem 22.10 (Koszul duality for the augmentation-generated line sector). Let A be a complete
augmented E1 algebra of local observables in the formal neighbourhood of a line defect, and put
A! = RHomA (1, 1). The adjunction of Theorem 7.5 identifies the thick, or presentably completed,
sector of A-modules generated by the augmentation with the corresponding sector of A! -modules. The
bar complex 1 ⊗L
               A 1 computes the derived matrix coefficients of the augmentation line.

Proof. The augmentation module is the distinguished line. Its derived endomorphisms form A! . The
endomorphism adjunction is an equivalence on the generator and therefore on its thick closure; compact
generation extends it to the stated presentable completion. The two-sided bar computes the derived
tensor product of the two augmentation modules.

   This theorem is the precise common content of Koszul duality in defect physics. Identifying A!
with a named Yangian is a further calculation.

22.10    Transfer matrices and scalar observables
Let V be an auxiliary object of a rigid line category and let M1 , . . . , Mn be quantum spaces. The
monodromy operator
                             TV (z) = RV,Mn (z − zn ) · · · RV,M1 (z − z1 )
obeys the RTT relation. Taking the categorical trace over V gives commuting transfer matrices:

                                         [tV (z), tW (w)] = 0.

The proof is the braid relation followed by cyclicity of the trace.
   This passage loses information. The transfer matrix is a scalar or endomorphism-valued trace
of ordered exchange. Bethe equations and Baxter relations describe its spectrum in selected rep-
resentations. They follow from the particular reconstructed quantum group and its representation
theory.




                                                 106
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


22.11    Knots and framing
Closing a braid requires duals and a ribbon twist. The Reshetikhin–Turaev invariant is the categorical
trace of the closed braid. In Chern–Simons theory the twist is the framing anomaly. Thus three
structures enter in order:

                            fusion    −→       braiding    −→   ribbon trace.

They are governed respectively by E1 , E2 , and cyclic or framed data. The distinction is the categorical
form of the distinction between an ordered word, an exchange path, and a closed world-line.

A holomorphic–topological theory on
                                                 Xhol × Rntop
assigns two independent kinds of local operation. Collision in the curve produces the chiral D-module
operation. Motion and little-disk insertion in the transverse space produce an En multiplication. Their
common algebraic carrier is an En -algebra internal to factorisable D-modules on X.

Proof. Apply the associative bar–cobar equivalence to A inside AlgEn−1 (CX ). The resulting bar is an

23.4    Configuration-space coefficients and the Poisson shadow
For d ≥ 2, the rational cohomology of Conf r (Rd ) is generated by classes
