R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The family index theorem identifies the curvature of the corresponding determinant line over moduli.
Its holomorphic component supplies the coefficient cλ/2 in the Atiyah class of the conformal-block
bundle [65].
   The stress tensor couples the Weyl response to the variation of complex structure. The family
index map carries this local response to the determinant line over moduli, while the transverse E1
multiplication continues to govern ordered fusion.

29.6    BRST anomaly
A BRST operator Q is required to satisfy Q2 = 0. In the bosonic string, the total BRST charge
squares to a multiple of the total central-charge defect and the intercept defect. Matter and ghosts
cancel the central extension when

                                   cmatter + cbc = 0,    cbc = −26.

This is additivity under tensor product of chiral theories.

Theorem 29.4 (BRST cancellation is tensorial). Let (V, QV ) and (G, QG ) be graded conformal
complexes whose odd BRST endomorphisms act on separate tensor factors. On homogeneous tensors
define
                           Q(v ⊗ g) = QV v ⊗ g + (−1)|v| v ⊗ QG g.
Then
                                       Q2 = Q2V ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ Q2G .
Consequently, central anomaly endomorphisms add under tensor product, and the tensor-product BRST
differential is square-zero precisely when their sum vanishes. A Koszul-duality comparison is a further
equivalence between the corresponding augmentation-generated sectors.

Proof. The two tensor-factor operators are odd and supercommute. Their two mixed compositions
therefore cancel. The remaining diagonal compositions are Q2V ⊗ 1 and 1 ⊗ Q2G , which proves the
formula and its cancellation statement.

29.7    BV anomaly
For a classical interaction I, define the renormalized quantum master defect
                                                 1
                                   A(Iℏ ) = QIℏ + {Iℏ , Iℏ } + ℏ∆Iℏ .
                                                 2
At the first unsolved order in ℏ, A is closed for the classical BV differential. Its cohomology class is
independent of counterterm representatives. A quantization exists to the next order precisely when
this class vanishes.

Proposition 29.5 (Locality of the BV anomaly). The BV anomaly is represented by a local functional.
In a configuration-space renormalisation scheme, graph weights extend to Fulton–MacPherson or
Axelrod–Singer compactifications. The codimension-one boundary factorisation formula expresses the
anomaly as the sum of local counterterm faces and the boundary terms that survive the quantum
master cancellation.


                                                  130
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Proof. The renormalised interaction is a local functional, and the BV bracket and BV Laplacian
preserve the local deformation complex. Hence the first nonzero quantum-master defect defines a
cocycle in local cohomology. In configuration-space renormalisation, Stokes’ theorem writes the defect
as a sum over compactification faces. Faces describing collapsed subgraphs give local counterterms; the
remaining faces give the factorisation terms of the quantum master equation. This is the compactified
form of the standard locality theorem for perturbative BV quantisation [9, 14].

  The quantum coefficient system consists of propagators and renormalised graph weights on the
compactified configuration spaces. Its boundary factorisation is the geometric realization of the BV
master equation.

29.8    Framing anomaly
A framing, or equivalently a compensating gravitational Chern–Simons term, lifts the Chern–Simons
partition function to an invariant of the framed three-manifold. The framing change multiplies the
partition function and Wilson-line observables by phases determined by the central charge of the
associated modular tensor category. This anomaly lives in three-dimensional geometry.
   The ribbon twist of the line category records the same phase on framed knots. Its relation to the
two-dimensional WZW central charge follows from the Chern–Simons/WZW correspondence. The
Chern–Simons/WZW comparison map relates this ribbon phase to the two-dimensional central charge
in the corresponding boundary theory.

29.9    Curved Koszul duality
A curved dg algebra or coalgebra has a curvature element m0 satisfying

                                           d2 (x) = [m0 , x]

in the associative case, with the corresponding A∞ identities in the homotopy case. For central scalar
m0 , the commutator is zero, while m0 remains as the constant term of the curved Maurer–Cartan
equation.
   Curvature arises naturally when an inhomogeneous relation has a constant term. For example,
quadratic-linear-constant Koszul duality places the constant term in m0 . A family comparison or
index map may send this algebraic curvature to a class such as c1 (E).

Separation theorem 29.6 (Native anomaly tuple). The anomaly data of an ordered chiral theory
form a tuple
                            k, c, [Ωdet ], [ABRST ], [Afr ], m0
                                                                

in the product of the current-extension line, Virasoro-extension line, determinant-line cohomology,
BRST local cohomology, framing-anomaly group, and curved Koszul algebra. A scalar relation among
components is the value of a specified comparison morphism or trace on this tuple.

Proof. Each component is defined by its native deformation problem: current extensions, stress-tensor
extensions, projective connections, ghost differentials, framed three-dimensional amplitudes, and
inhomogeneous quadratic relations. Tensor products and family-index maps act componentwise.
Therefore every equality between components is carried by an explicit morphism between their
deformation complexes.

                                                 131
R. Lorgat                                                          Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


29.10    Anomalies as invertible theories
For local anomalies admitting the standard anomaly-field-theory description, the anomaly is represented
by an invertible field theory one dimension higher. The anomalous theory is relative to its state lines,
and cancellation is a trivialisation of the tensor product anomaly.
   For chiral conformal blocks, the invertible theory is represented by a determinant or Hodge line.
For fermions it is the determinant line of the Dirac operator. For Chern–Simons theory it includes the
framing line. This viewpoint explains additivity: tensoring theories tensors anomaly lines and adds
their characteristic classes.

Principle 29.7 (Anomaly comparison). A morphism of invertible theories identifies two anomalies
by matching their state lines, connections, and gluing laws. Numerical equalities are traces of this
identification.

Proof. An invertible theory assigns a line to every closed codimension-one manifold and an isomorphism
of tensor products of lines to every bordism. A morphism is a compatible family of line isomorphisms,
so it identifies the corresponding connections and their curvature classes and intertwines every gluing
map. Evaluation on a closed bordism, or application of a character of the coefficient line, takes this
structured identification to the associated numerical equality.

29.11    The anomaly square
The complete theory contains four compatible arrows:

                                             Ward identity
                   local central extension                   Atiyah algebra of blocks

                     BV descent                                          c1                          (186)

                     local BV anomaly family index curvature of determinant line.

Each arrow is constructed in a specified field theory. For free chiral fields and WZW models, the
standard Ward-identity and family-index calculations construct the arrows of this square. For a
general ordered chiral algebra, the upper-left corner exists while the other corners require a stress
tensor, a family of states, and a BV realization.
   The square is the precise form of the unity sought by the subject. It gives one language through
the typed comparison maps joining its distinct objects.


Part V
Calculated noncommutative chiral algebras
30      A calculus of strictly noncommutative chiral algebras
The transverse multiplication is genuine structure. A factorisable D-module M may support several
associative products, and each product has its own interval bar. The calculations below begin with




                                                    132
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


generators and relations in the symmetric monoidal category

                                         CX := FactD(X), ⊗pt , 1 .
                                                                   


Every tensor product, kernel, cokernel, and dual in this section is formed in CX . Exactness statements
are read after a conservative family of component functors to complexes of vector spaces. This
convention gives concrete proofs while retaining the full chiral coefficient system on X.

30.1    The multiplication-dependence theorem
Let M ∈ CX be connective and tensor-flat. Two augmented associative algebras arise from the same
generator object:

                 TX (M ) = 1 ⊕ M ⊕ M ⊗2 ⊕ · · · ,          QX (M ) = 1 ⊕ M,      M · M = 0.

The first uses concatenation; the second uses the square-zero product.

Theorem 30.1 (Multiplication determines the ordered bar). There are natural equivalences
                         ord                               ord
                     BarX TX (M ) ≃ M [1],            BarX QX (M ) ≃             M [1]⊗n .
                                                                         M

                                                                           n≥1

The first reduced coproduct vanishes. The second is deconcatenation.

Proof. The augmentation module of TX (M ) has the two-term free resolution
                                                    conc
                               0 −→ TX (M ) ⊗ M −−−→ TX (M ) −→ 1 −→ 0.

Tensoring over TX (M ) with 1 produces M [1]. The simplicial bar gives the same equivalence through
its extra degeneracy: a bar word with an internal cut contracts, and a one-letter word survives.
    For QX (M ), every inner face of the normalized bar multiplies two elements of M and therefore
vanishes. The normalized complex is the tensor coalgebra on M [1] with its internal differential and
deconcatenation coproduct.

Corollary 30.2 (Presentation is part of the invariant). The ordered bar is the invariant of the pair
(M, m⊥ ), where M carries the chiral coefficient and m⊥ carries the transverse associative multiplication.
Distinct products on the same coefficient object produce distinct bar coalgebras.

Proof. The normalized bar differential is the coderivation induced by m⊥ . For the free tensor algebra
the extra-degeneracy contraction leaves one copy of M [1]; for the square-zero product the differential
vanishes and the whole tensor coalgebra survives. These two products on the same coefficient object
therefore give inequivalent bar coalgebras, and the general bar is functorial in the pair (M, m⊥ ).

   The corollary supplies the first exact test for every proposed numerical bar invariant: the multipli-
cation must appear in the chain differential that produces the number.

30.2    Tensor products and independent sectors
Theorem 30.3 (Eilenberg–Zilber for internal bars). Let A and B be augmented tensor-flat associative
algebras in CX . The shuffle and Alexander–Whitney maps give a natural equivalence of conilpotent

                                                     133
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


coalgebras
                               Barord
                                  X (A ⊗ B) ≃ BarX (A) ⊗ BarX (B).
                                                 ord        ord


If the Kunneth spectral sequence converges, then

                                              ∼ TorA (1, 1) ⊗ TorB (1, 1).
                               Tor∗A⊗B (1, 1) =    ∗             ∗

Proof. The bisimplicial object in bidegree (p, q) is

                                         1 ⊗ Ā⊗p ⊗ B̄ ⊗q ⊗ 1.

Its diagonal is the normalized bar of A ⊗ B. The Eilenberg–Zilber theorem identifies the realization of
the diagonal with the iterated realization. The shuffle map respects deconcatenation because a cut of
a shuffle is the sum of shuffles of the two induced cuts.

   This theorem constructs doubly structured examples directly. One factor may carry a complicated
chiral coefficient and a simple transverse product; the second may carry a highly noncommutative
transverse product. Their bar invariants multiply while their chiral factorization maps remain visible
in each tensor factor.

30.3    Quadratic algebras in a chiral coefficient category
Let K ∈ CX be tensor-flat and let V be a finite-dimensional vector space. Set M = K ⊗ V . A
subobject
                                   R = K ⊗2 ⊗ R0 ⊂ M ⊗2
for R0 ⊂ V ⊗2 defines
                                    AX (V, R0 ; K) := TX (M )/(R).
The chiral geometry lives in the powers of K; the transverse relation lives in R0 .

Proposition 30.4 (Base change of a quadratic resolution). Suppose the ordinary quadratic algebra
A0 = T (V )/(R0 ) has a linear free resolution whose matrices have scalar entries in the generators.
Tensoring every weight-n term with K ⊗n produces an internal free resolution of the augmentation of
AX (V, R0 ; K). Consequently the ordinary Koszul homology of A0 lifts coefficientwise to CX .

Proof. Every identity among the matrices of the ordinary resolution is an identity in the free tensor
category generated by V . The symmetric monoidal functor V ⊗n 7→ K ⊗n ⊗ V ⊗n preserves these
identities. Tensor-flatness preserves exactness after component evaluation, and conservativity returns
exactness in CX .

30.4    The quantum plane
Fix q ∈ C× . The quantum-plane chiral algebra is
                                                         .
                                Aq (K) = TX Kx ⊕ Ky                                                (187)
                                                                       
                                                              yx − qxy .

The relation orients the transverse product. The powers of K retain their chiral collision data.




                                                   134
R. Lorgat                                                                    Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Theorem 30.5 (Quantum-plane resolution and dual). The augmentation of Aq (K) has the length-two
free resolution
                                             d                                    d
            0 −→ Aq (K) ⊗ K ⊗2 [−2] −→
                                     2
                                       Aq (K) ⊗ (Kx ⊕ Ky)[−1] −→
                                                               1
                                                                 Aq (K) −→ 1 −→ 0,                                  (188)

where
                            d1 (ex ) = x,          d1 (ey ) = y,        d2 (1) = yex − qxey .
Hence
             A (K)                     A (K)                                          A (K)
        Tor0 q       (1, 1) = 1,    Tor1 q        (1, 1) = Kx[1] ⊕ Ky[1],        Tor2 q       (1, 1) = K ⊗2 ρ[2],

and the higher groups vanish.
   For dualizable K, the quadratic dual is
                                                                    . 2 2
                               Aq (K)! = TX K ∨ ξ ⊕ K ∨ η             ξ , η , ξη + qηξ .                            (189)
                                                                                      


Proof. The single reduction rule yx 7→ qxy gives the unique normal form xi y j , so these monomials
form a PBW basis. Formula (188) is a complex because

                                                 d1 d2 (1) = yx − qxy = 0.

Write a syzygy as aex + bey with ax + by = 0. Comparison of PBW normal forms shows that the terms
ending in x and y cancel precisely in pairs generated by yex − qxey . The Ore-extension realization of
Aq makes it a domain, and therefore d2 is injective. This proves exactness.
   Let ξ, η be dual to x, y. A tensor aξ 2 + bξη + cηξ + dη 2 is orthogonal to yx − qxy exactly when
c − qb = 0. The orthogonal relation space is generated by

                                                 ξ2,    η2,         ξη + qηξ,

which proves (189).

   The reduced coproduct of the degree-two bar class records the relation:

                                             ∆(ρ) = sy ⊗ sx − q sx ⊗ sy.

Thus the coalgebra remembers the ordered commutation parameter before any trace is taken.

30.5    The Jordan plane
The Jordan-plane chiral algebra is
                                                                    .
                                   AJ (K) = TX (Kx ⊕ Ky)                yx − xy − x2 .                              (190)
                                                                                      


Theorem 30.6 (Jordan-plane resolution and dual). The augmentation has the free resolution

            0 −→ AJ (K) ⊗ K ⊗2 [−2] −→ AJ (K) ⊗ (Kx ⊕ Ky)[−1] −→ AJ (K) −→ 1 −→ 0




                                                              135
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


with
                                         d2 (1) = (y − x)ex − xey .
The quadratic dual is
                                                       .
                          AJ (K)! = TX (K ∨ ξ ⊕ K ∨ η)      η 2 , ξη + ηξ, ξ 2 + ηξ .                  (191)
                                                                                   


Proof. The relation writes AJ as the Ore extension C[x][y; δ] with δ(x) = x2 . Hence xi y j is a PBW
basis and AJ is a domain. The displayed differential is a complex because

                                   (y − x)x − xy = yx − x2 − xy = 0.

PBW comparison gives the same one-generator syzygy argument as in the quantum plane. Orthogonal-
ity to yx − xy − x2 gives the linear condition c − b − a = 0 on the coefficients of aξ 2 + bξη + cηξ + dη 2 .
The three displayed relations form a basis of this orthogonal subspace.

    The quantum and Jordan planes have the same Hilbert series (1 − t)−2 and the same bar dimensions
(1, 2, 1). Their reduced coproducts and dual multiplication distinguish them. Dimension is therefore a
shadow of the ordered coalgebra rather than its complete invariant.

30.6    The Weyl deformation and curved duality
Let ℏ be central. The Rees Weyl algebra is
                                                              .
                               Wℏ (K) = TX (Kx ⊕ Ky)[ℏ]                                                (192)
                                                                              
                                                                   yx − xy − ℏ .

The filtration by total degree in x, y has associated graded the commutative polynomial algebra.

Theorem 30.7 (Curved dual of the Weyl relation). The nonhomogeneous quadratic dual of (192) is
the curved algebra
                            ΛX (K ∨ ξ ⊕ K ∨ η), d = 0, h = ℏ ξη ,
                                                               

in the suspension convention of Appendix A. Its curved cobar construction recovers Wℏ (K).

Proof. The quadratic part yx − xy has orthogonal relations ξ 2 , η 2 , and ξη + ηξ, which form the exterior
algebra. The constant component of the relation is the functional sending the quadratic relation to ℏ.
Under quadratic duality this functional is the curvature element ℏξη. The curved cobar differential
has a constant term dual to h; its defining relation is therefore yx − xy = ℏ.

   Theorem 30.7 places ℏξη inside the curved Koszul dual algebra. A family over moduli carries a
second geometric object: the curvature two-form of its determinant-line connection. The algebraic
element and the geometric two-form are related only through a constructed family index or anomaly
map.

Example 30.9 (The doubled A2 quiver). Let a : 1 → 2 and a∗ : 2 → 1. The preprojective algebra of
type A2 is
                             ΠA2 = C⟨e1 , e2 , a, a∗ ⟩/(aa∗ , a∗ a),

every n ≥ 1, the tensor power M ⊗S n has two basis paths, the alternating path starting at vertex 1
and the alternating path starting at vertex 2. Hence
                                              ΠA

and the completed dual is the completed path algebra of the doubled quiver. Decorating a and a∗ by
factorisation D-modules produces boundary-changing chiral fields with the same exact relative bar.

   The example exhibits infinite homological depth in a four-dimensional algebra. The depth comes
from transverse relations; the chiral coefficient may be varied independently.

30.8    Finite-group and orbifold extensions

30.10    The first structural conclusion
The examples produce three independent axes:

            chiral coefficient transverse multiplication     exchange enhancement
         factorisable D-module      E1 algebra law       E2 transport or flat connection

A change along the middle axis changes the interval bar. A change along the first axis changes collision
singularities and conformal blocks. A change along the third axis changes braid monodromy and
the Tannaka reconstruction. The full theory records all three axes and the comparison maps among
them.


31      Exact enumeration and the meaning of the legacy tables
The original calculations contain genuine combinatorics. Their mathematical meaning becomes precise
after the transverse bar and the chiral collision complex are placed in separate degrees. This section
performs that separation and identifies every sequence that survives as an exact invariant.

31.1    Three chain models
Let A ∈ Algaug
            E1 (FactD(X)). Three complexes occur naturally.
   The interval bar is
                               Bp⊥ (A) = Ā[1]⊗p , d = bA + dA .

    For a chiral Lie coefficient g, the collision Chevalley complex is

   The theorem gives a precise home to configuration-space numbers. A dimension from Cscr     ∗ is

chain-space dimension on the first page; later differentials encode the supplied interaction.

31.2    The Orlik–Solomon factor
For the configuration space of n ordered points on the affine line,

                                   Conf n (C) = {(z1 , . . . , zn ) : zi ̸= zj },

                         H ∗ (Conf n (C); C) = Λ(ωij )/ ωij ωjk + ωjk ωki + ωki ωij ,

with ωij = d log(zi − zj ).

                                                          n−1
                                               Pn (t) =         (1 + jt).
                                                          Y

                                                          j=1

In particular,
                                     dim H n−1 (Conf n (C)) = (n − 1)!.

                                                   dn (n − 1)!.                                              (195)
Formula (195) is an exact chain-space count. The interval bar of an associative multiplication on V
has chain dimension dn before normalization. A homology dimension requires the differential.

31.4    The corrected bar values for standard transverse products
Let the same three-dimensional generator space underlie several associative algebras. The exact
ordered-bar homologies are:

              transverse algebra Poincare series of Tor          mechanism
                    T (V )              1 + 3t            free-algebra contraction
                                           1
                1 ⊕ V, V 2 = 0                             zero bar multiplication
                                        1 − 3t
                    U (sl2 )             1 + t3             Chevalley homology
                      Aq              1 + 2t + t2       quadratic Koszul resolution

The final row is the rank-two quantum-plane calculation of Theorem 30.5.

Theorem 31.5 (Exact audit of the affine sequences). For the transverse multiplication U (g), the
corrected ordered bar is the Chevalley complex and has Poincare polynomial (193). Hence

                                            n dim Torn      (C, C)tn
                                          P           U (g)
                                     g

                                    G2        1 + t3 + t11 + t14

Proof. The first assertion is Theorem 30.12. Substituting the exponent sets {1}, {1, 2}, and {1, 5}
gives the table. The final sentence is the universal property of homology: an equality of dimensions
follows from a quasi-isomorphism or an independently proved equality of homology groups.

31.5    Virasoro coefficients and transverse presentations
The Virasoro OPE specifies a chiral coefficient. A transverse E1 product arises from additional data,
such as an endomorphism algebra of boundary conditions, a tensor algebra generated by the Virasoro
coefficient, or an enveloping algebra of a chosen mode Lie algebra.

Theorem 31.6 (Exact alternatives for a Virasoro coefficient). Let MVir denote a factorisation object
carrying the universal Virasoro chiral operation.

  (i) The free transverse algebra TX (MVir ) has reduced ordered-bar homology MVir [1].

 (ii) The square-zero transverse algebra 1 ⊕ MVir has ordered bar T c (MVir [1]).

                                                  142
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


 (iii) For a Lie subalgebra l of Virasoro modes and the transverse algebra U (l), the ordered bar is
       C∗CE (l).

A fourth transverse presentation realizes the Motzkin-difference series as a Virasoro bar invariant
when its bar complex is connected to the Motzkin complex by a proved quasi-isomorphism.

Proof. The three calculations are Theorem 30.1 and Theorem 30.12. The final assertion states the
data needed to define an invariant of an associative algebra.

   The theorem refines the theory: the Virasoro singularity and the transverse multiplication are
coordinates on different axes. A physical boundary condition selects a transverse endomorphism
algebra, and its bar can then be calculated.

31.6    A reproducible finite-window test
Let A = T (V )/(R) be a homogeneous algebra with finite-dimensional graded pieces. For weights at
most N and bar length at most L, form the finite complex

                                   B≤L,≤N (A) =             (Ā[1]⊗p )w .
                                                   M M

                                                  p≤L w≤N

Proposition 31.7 (Finite-window certification). A matrix computation of the bar differential on
B≤L,≤N (A) certifies the homology in every bidegree (p, w) satisfying

                     p ≤ L,      w ≤ N,       p + 1 ≤ L for incoming boundaries.

For a quadratic Koszul algebra generated in weight one, the length-two Koszul resolution certifies every
weight for the quantum and Jordan planes.

Proof. The differential preserves total weight and lowers bar length by one. Homology at length
p uses only the maps from lengths p + 1 and p. The stated window contains both. For a length-
two free resolution, the resolution itself computes all derived tensors, so every weight is included
simultaneously.

    This finite-window principle supplies arithmetic checks while preserving the mathematical definition
of the complex. The matrix is a verification of a specified differential; the theorem identifies the range
certified by the matrix.


32     Modules, boundary conditions, and orbifold operations
A noncommutative chiral algebra acts on boundary conditions. The correct derived object for two
boundaries is the two-sided interval amplitude.

32.1    The pointed ordered bar
Let A ∈ Algaug
           E1 (FactD(X)), let MR be a right A-module, and let ML be a left A-module. Define


                              BA (MR , ML ) = [n] 7−→ MR ⊗ Ā⊗n ⊗ ML .                              (197)


                                                   143
R. Lorgat                                                               Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Theorem 32.1 (Boundary-to-boundary amplitude). There is a natural equivalence

                                      BA (MR , ML ) ≃ MR ⊗L
                                                          A ML .

If the modules arise from boundary conditions in a rectangular holomorphic–topological factorisation
algebra on X × R, the same object is the factorisation homology of an interval labelled by MR and ML
at its endpoints.

Proof. The simplicial object (197) is the standard derived coequalizer of the right and left actions.
Geometric realization computes the relative tensor product. Excision for the interval identifies the
same realization with the labelled factorisation homology.

  A marked chiral insertion produces a comodule over Barord
                                                         X (A) by cutting the interval between the
marked point and either endpoint. The two cuts are the left and right coactions.

32.2   Quiver boundary conditions
For a finite quiver Q, let AQ = TS (M ) be the internal path algebra of Section 30. The vertex
idempotent ei defines a boundary condition and a simple relative module Si .

Proposition 32.2 (Derived boundary-changing fields). For the free path algebra AQ , the relative
derived morphisms satisfy
                                      
                      A
                                        1,    i = j,             A
                   Tor0 Q (Sj , Si ) ∼                         Tor1 Q (Sj , Si ) ∼           Ma [1],
                                                                                     M
                                     =                                           =
                                         0,   i ̸= j,                               a:i→j

and the higher groups vanish.

Proof. The relative bar of a tensor algebra contracts to S ⊕ M [1]. Multiplication by ej on the left
and ei on the right selects the summands with target j and source i.

   For the radical-square-zero doubled A2 algebra of Example 30.9, the corresponding derived morphism
has one alternating path in every admissible length. The complete bar therefore records an infinite
tower of boundary-changing composites even though the algebra itself has radical square zero.

32.3   Koszul modules for the quantum plane
Let Aq = Aq (1) and let C be the augmentation module. The resolution (188) gives

                           Ext∗Aq (C, C) ∼
                                         = A!q = C⟨ξ, η⟩/(ξ 2 , η 2 , ξη + qηξ).

The Yoneda product is the multiplication of the quadratic dual. The two degree-one extension classes
compose to the degree-two relation class, and their ordered composition carries the parameter q.
  Let Lx = Aq /(Aq x) and Ly = Aq /(Aq y). Their defining one-step resolutions give
                             A                                    A
                          Tor1 q (C, Lx ) ∼
                                          = Cx[1],             Tor1 q (C, Ly ) ∼
                                                                               = Cy[1].

The modules distinguish the two ordered generators even when the ungraded Poincare series of Aq
agrees with that of the Jordan plane.

                                                         144
R. Lorgat                                                                Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


32.4    Equivariant modules and crossed products
Let a finite group G act on A by augmented automorphisms. The crossed product A ⋊ G contains
both the algebra and the symmetry operators.

Theorem 32.3 (Equivariantization). There is an equivalence of module categories

                                          ModA⋊G ≃ ModG
                                                      A,

where the right side is the category of G-equivariant A-modules. Under this equivalence, the two-sided
bar of crossed-product modules is the total complex of the group bar and the A-bar.

Proof. An A ⋊ G action restricts to an A action and a G action satisfying g(am) = g(a)g(m).
Conversely, this compatibility defines the crossed-product action. The standard bar resolution of the
semidirect product is the diagonal of the resulting bisimplicial resolution.

   In characteristic zero, group averaging contracts the positive group-homology rows. The invariant
formula of Theorem 30.10 follows.

32.5    Invariant algebras and crossed products
The fixed algebra AG and the crossed product A ⋊ G encode complementary operations. The fixed
algebra contains invariant local operators. The crossed product contains equivariant boundary
conditions and symmetry defects. The canonical functor

                                         ModA⋊G −→ ModAG

forgets the equivariant structure and restricts scalars. A Morita equivalence between these algebras is
supplied by a Galois or freeness theorem for the action.
   This distinction becomes geometric for orbifolds. Untwisted invariant fields belong to AG ; twisted
sectors arise from defect boundary conditions in the equivariant category. Sewing the twisted sectors
produces the orbifold conformal blocks.

32.6    Symmetric powers and plethystic exponentials
Let V =     n≥0 Vn be a graded vector space with finite-dimensional pieces and character
          L


                                         f (q) =        (dim Vn )q n .
                                                   X

                                                   n≥0

The symmetric algebra has the second-quantized character
                                                                                 
                                                                   X pr
                                    pN ch SymN V = exp                    f (q r ) .                     (198)
                             X                      

                             N ≥0                                r≥1
                                                                     r

Proof. Choose homogeneous bases. Each even basis element of weight n contributes (1 − pq n )−1 .
Multiplication over the basis and logarithmic expansion of the Euler factors gives (198). The super
version replaces dimensions by superdimensions and includes the fermionic Euler factors.



                                                    145
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


  The formula is a trace identity. The orbifold operator algebra additionally contains twisted sectors
and their OPEs.

32.7    The DMVV formula
Let Y be a compact complex manifold for which the elliptic genus

                                   Ell(Y ; q, y) =
                                                       X
                                                               c(m, ℓ)q m y ℓ
                                                     m≥0,ℓ∈Z

is defined. The symmetric-product orbifold elliptic genera satisfy the Dijkgraaf–Moore–Verlinde–
Verlinde product formula [26]:
                                                      Y Y Y                           −c(nm,ℓ)
                         pN Ellorb SymN Y ; q, y =                    1 − pn q m y ℓ                    (199)
                   X                           
                                                                                                   .
                  N ≥0                                n≥1 m≥0 ℓ∈Z

Theorem 32.4 (Cycle decomposition behind DMVV). Formula (199) is the exponential of connected
cycle contributions. A length-n cycle contributes the seed coefficient with energy multiplied by n and
with the standard 1/n automorphism factor. Exponentiation assembles disjoint cycles.

Proof. Conjugacy classes in the symmetric group are indexed by partitions, equivalently by multiplici-
ties of cycles of each length. The orbifold trace factorizes over cycles. Summing the multiplicity of
length-n cycles exponentiates its connected contribution. Expanding the logarithm of the product in
(199) reproduces the cycle sum.

   The same combinatorics appears in a cyclic bar: connected annular contributions exponentiate
to disconnected traces. The coefficient object and the orbifold twisted sectors determine the actual
amplitudes.

32.8    Boundary categories as the physical carrier
A physical boundary condition b in a holomorphic–topological theory supplies

                                 Ab = RHom(b, b) ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X)).

Two boundaries b0 , b1 supply the bimodule

                                         M01 = RHom(b0 , b1 ).

Composition of boundary-changing operators gives the module structure; collision on X gives the
chiral coefficient. The interval amplitude

                                             M10 ⊗L
                                                  Ab M010


counts derived excursions from b0 to b1 and back. A quiver of boundary conditions is therefore a
literal source of the quiver chiral algebras above.
    This construction realizes strict noncommutative chirality in field theory. The associative order is
composition in the topological direction, and the chiral singularity is the operator product on the
curve.

33      Rational exchange, RTT algebras, and Yangian recognition
The interval supplies associative order. A transverse plane supplies paths that exchange ordered
lines. This section calculates the resulting rational exchange algebra and states the exact recognition
theorem that produces a Yangian.

33.4    Drinfeld–Kohno recognition
The KZ connection produces a braided tensor category of suitable affine conformal blocks. The
Drinfeld–Kohno theorem identifies this braided tensor category with the corresponding quantum-group
category, with
                                                      πi

                                            q = exp
                                                      κ
under the chosen normalization [51, 50].
Theorem 33.5 (Recognition from exchange data). Let L be a rigid meromorphic tensor category
generated by evaluation objects V (a), and let F : L → VectC((ℏ)) be a faithful exact tensor functor.
Assume:
  (i) the exchange on F (V (a)) ⊗ F (V (b)) is the rational matrix R(a − b) of (200);

 (ii) fusion is compatible with the RTT coproduct (206);

 (iii) tensor products of evaluation objects generate L under finite colimits, subquotients, and duals;

 (iv) their matrix coefficients separate the spectral completion of Yℏ (glN ).
Then the Tannaka coend                        Z L∈L

is the spectral finite-dual bialgebra of the RTT Yangian. When the spectral graded pieces are finite-
dimensional, the continuous dual satisfies

                                              OF∨ ∼  ℏ (glN ).
                                                  = Y\

Proof. The coend is generated by matrix coefficients of the evaluation objects. Naturality of exchange
gives the RTT relation on matrix coefficients, while tensor compatibility gives the dual of the coproduct
(206). The generation hypothesis makes the resulting morphism from the spectral finite dual onto OF
surjective. Separation makes it injective. Finite-dimensionality of the spectral graded pieces identifies
the continuous dual of the finite dual with the completed RTT algebra.

  This theorem locates the Yangian precisely. The interval E1 product supplies fusion. The
meromorphic exchange supplies R(u). The fibre functor reconstructs the bialgebra.

33.5    Four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory
Costello–Witten–Yamazaki study a gauge theory on Σ × C, topological along the real surface Σ and
holomorphic along the complex curve C [19, 20]. Wilson lines supported on curves in Σ carry a
spectral parameter in C. Perturbative exchange of two lines gives the classical rational kernel

                                                          ℏΩ
                                               r(z) =        ,
                                                           z
and isotopy of three lines gives the Yang–Baxter equation. Their all-orders analysis produces the
RTT presentations of the rational, trigonometric, and elliptic quantum groups in the corresponding
geometries.

Proposition 33.6 (Ordered chiral structure of Wilson lines). Fix an oriented line in the topological
surface Σ. The fusion of Wilson lines along that line is an E1 multiplication. Holomorphic dependence
on their positions in C is a factorisable coefficient. Allowing the lines to move in the full surface
supplies exchange paths and promotes the line category to a braided meromorphic tensor category.

Proof. Disjoint intervals on the oriented line compose by topological gluing, which is the little-interval
operation. Holomorphic translation in C preserves the protected sector and produces meromorphic
collision kernels. In the full surface, the configuration space of two lines has a nontrivial exchange
path; topological invariance identifies isotopic paths and yields braid relations.

  The proposition is the physical realization of an E1 algebra in a chiral coefficient category. The
Yangian is the Tannaka reconstruction of its planar exchange enhancement.

34     Prime forms, sewing, and exact higher-genus amplitudes
The complex curve contributes a geometric genus through plumbing and conformal blocks. A cyclic
transverse algebra contributes a ribbon genus through Hochschild and open topological sewing. The
constructions below calculate the chiral-curve side and display its exact relation to the local collision
operation.

34.1    The prime form and the normalized bidifferential
Let C be a compact Riemann surface of genus g, choose a symplectic basis of H1 (C, Z), and let
ω = (ω1 , . . . , ωg )T be the normalized holomorphic one-forms with period matrix Ω. Choose an odd
theta characteristic δ. The prime form is
                                                           R          

Proposition 34.1 (Local form of the prime form). The section E(p, q) is antisymmetric, has a simple

                                     z(p) − z(q)                      

gives zero A-periods and the stated B-periods.

   The normal symbols of Section 15 are the algebraic local coefficients of (212). The prime form
supplies their global analytic transport.

Let V = n≥0 Vn carry a nondegenerate invariant pairing, and choose dual homogeneous bases {uα }

   For strongly rational vertex operator algebras, conformal blocks form vector bundles and the sewing

Proof. The Heisenberg Fock representation is Gaussian. The oscillator trace contracts one positive

   The theorem gives a precise genus threshold. Degree-four Siegel modular data distinguishes the

34.8    Separating and nonseparating plumbing
A separating plumbing identifies local coordinates xy = q at punctures of two curves. In the state-space
realization, the amplitude is

                            ZC1 #q C2 =         q wt(uα ) ZC1 (. . . , uα )ZC2 (uα , . . .),                  (230)
                                          X

                                            α

with the anomaly line carrying the vacuum-energy and coordinate factors. A nonseparating plumbing
contracts two insertions on one curve with the same tensor (217).

Proposition 34.8 (Graph expansion of sewing). Iterated plumbing expands a conformal block as
a sum over stable graphs. Vertices carry lower-genus blocks, edges carry (217), and automorphism


                                                           155
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


groups supply the standard symmetry factors. Absolute convergence follows in every region where the
q L0 sewing operators are trace class.

Proof. Apply (230) at every node. Choices of intermediate states label the half-edges. Completeness
contracts the labels along each edge. Quotienting labelled gluings by graph automorphisms gives the
symmetry factor. Trace-class bounds permit rearrangement of the sums.

34.9    The two genus variables
Let Θ carry a chiral modular sewing structure and a transverse cyclic E1 structure. Denote the
corresponding edge contractions by
                                        ∆X ,     ∆⊥ .
The first forms nodes in the algebraic curve. The second closes open topological boundaries into
ribbon handles.

Theorem 34.9 (Bigraded sewing equation). Assume the two cyclic structures act on commuting
colors of a two-colored modular operad and the determinant orientation lines are ordered as orX ⊗ or⊥ .
Then
                           ∆2X = 0,     ∆2⊥ = 0,     ∆X ∆⊥ + ∆⊥ ∆X = 0,
and the combined master equation is
                            1         1
                        dΘ + [Θ, Θ]X + [Θ, Θ]⊥ + ℏX ∆X Θ + ℏ⊥ ∆⊥ Θ = 0.
                            2         2
Proof. Each square is the oriented codimension-two boundary identity for two nodes of the same
color. A mixed pair of nodes has product normal line NX ⊕ N⊥ . Interchanging the two odd normal
generators reverses the determinant orientation, so the mixed contractions anticommute. The master
equation is the image of the total boundary differential under the supplied coefficient operations.

   The two formal variables retain the physical distinction between complex-curve sewing and open-
string ribbon topology. A duality relating them is represented by a map between their two modular
operads.


Part VI
Exact mathematics and exact physics
35     Exact examples
35.1    Heisenberg
Let Hk be generated by a current J with

                                                           k
                                        J(z)J(w) ∼              .                                     (231)
                                                       (z − w)2




                                                 156
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The chiral operation is concentrated in the second normal symbol of the diagonal. The corresponding
ordinary Lie conformal bracket is
                                              [Jλ J] = kλ.
The Jacobi identity is immediate because the bracket is central.
   The chiral Chevalley coalgebra is cofree on the abelian chiral Lie algebra with the central extension
built into the operation. Ordered monodromy is scalar. For vacuum and Fock-module insertions on a
compact curve, the conformal blocks admit Fock-space descriptions and their partition functions are
determinant expressions. The rank-one conformal vector has central charge one, independent of the
nonzero normalization k after rescaling the current. This already separates level from central charge.

35.2    Affine Kac–Moody
For Vk (g) the current OPE is (140). The simple pole is the Lie bracket; the double pole is the invariant
bilinear form. The chiral Jacobi identity splits into the ordinary Jacobi identity and invariance of the
form:
                                         ([a, b], c) = (a, [b, c]).
The Chevalley differential formed from the simple-pole bracket squares to zero by itself. The level
term supplies a central extension and enters conformal and representation-theoretic constructions.
   At noncritical level the Sugawara vector has central charge

                                                       k dim g
                                              caff =           .                                      (232)
                                                       k + h∨
The KZ connection and its quantum-group monodromy are described in Section 21. At positive
integral level, the integrable modules of the simple affine VOA Lk (g) form a modular tensor category,
and their conformal blocks satisfy the Verlinde formula.
   At level one for simply laced g, the Frenkel–Kac lattice realization expresses the affine currents
inside a lattice VOA [35]. The Frenkel–Kac realization preserves the nonabelian simple-pole bracket
inside the lattice VOA.

35.3    Virasoro
The Virasoro vertex algebra is generated by T with

                                               c/2      2T (w)     ∂T (w)
                             T (z)T (w) ∼            +           +        .                           (233)
                                            (z − w)4   (z − w) 2   z−w

The three singular terms lie in the fourth, second, and first normal symbols. Their relation is fixed
by conformal covariance. The central charge c is the coefficient of the fourth-order symbol and the
central extension of the vector-field algebra.
   The vacuum Verma module has a level-four quasi-primary
                                                           3 2
                                            Λ =: T T : −      ∂ T
                                                           10
whose norm is proportional to c(5c + 22). The zeros at c = 0 and c = −22/5 signal null vectors. This
Gram determinant is representation-theoretic data controlling the level-four null-vector locus; any


                                                   157
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


quartic deformation invariant requires a separately defined deformation complex and projector.
    Conformal blocks carry a projective connection with Hodge coefficient c/2. At c = 26, coupling
to the (b, c) ghost system of weights (2, −1) cancels the total central charge. The value c = 13 is
the fixed point of the numerical reflection c 7→ 26 − c used in bosonic string bookkeeping; a Virasoro
self-duality theorem requires an OPE-compatible equivalence at that point.

35.4    Free fermions
A complex free fermion has fields ψ, ψ ∗ with
                                                          1
                                         ψ(z)ψ ∗ (w) ∼       .
                                                         z−w
The chiral operation lies in the first normal symbol. Wick’s theorem determines all higher correlators
as determinants. The representation category, spin structures, and modular transformation law depend
on whether one uses a real, complex, or lattice extension.
   Boson–fermion correspondence identifies the charged free fermion VOA with a rank-one lattice
VOA. This is an isomorphism of vertex algebras after the lattice extension. A linear Koszul comparison
between the Heisenberg and fermion chiral Lie algebras is a different construction.

35.5    The βγ and bc systems
A first-order bosonic system has
                                                          1
                                         β(z)γ(w) ∼          ,
                                                         z−w
while a fermionic bc system has the same singular kernel with odd parity. Their oscillator algebras are
Weyl and Clifford algebras. For fields of weights (λ, 1 − λ), the central charges are

                         cβγ = 2(6λ2 − 6λ + 1),         cbc = −2(6λ2 − 6λ + 1).                    (234)

The opposite signs reflect statistics. The determinant lines cancel when the systems are paired with
identical weights.
   The full homotopical structure also contains the operations generated by composite fields and
modules. Pole order and deformation cohomology are independent.

35.6    Lattice vertex algebras
Let L be an even positive-definite lattice. The lattice VOA VL has central charge rk L. Its fields
include Heisenberg currents and exponentials eα . Their operator products contain powers

                                             (z − w)(α,β)

and a cocycle ϵ(α, β). Locality requires the commutator of the cocycle to match the parity determined
by the lattice form.
   If L is even unimodular, VL is holomorphic. The character is (184). Inverting the cocycle gives the
contragredient braiding; a symmetric cocycle may make the theory self-contragredient. This statement
belongs to the lattice extension and its cocycle-reversal involution.



                                                  158
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


35.7    Leech and Moonshine
The Leech lattice VOA VΛ and the Moonshine VOA V ♮ both have central charge 24. Their weight-one
spaces differ:
                                  (VΛ )1 ∼
                                         = C24 , (V ♮ )1 = 0.
Their characters also differ in the constant term after the vacuum normalization. Thus the weight-one
algebra, graded character, OPE, and factorization coalgebra refine central charge in the classification
of holomorphic VOAs.
   The Monster action on V ♮ produces McKay–Thompson series. These are traces of automorphisms
on the state space. They are arithmetic traces of Monster automorphisms on the VOA and become
coefficients of any modular invariant only through the corresponding twining trace.

35.8    Principal W -algebras
The principal W -algebra W k (g) is obtained by BRST reduction. At generic level its standard strong
generators have conformal weights equal to the exponents of g plus one; special levels may impose
decoupling relations or collapse generators. Their OPEs are nonlinear and contain normally ordered
composites. The full chiral operation nevertheless satisfies the same Maurer–Cartan equation (116).
   For sl2 , the principal W -algebra is Virasoro. For sl3 , it is generated by T and a weight-three field
W ; the W W OPE contains T , its derivatives, and the quasi-primary Λ. These terms are fixed by
Jacobi and conformal covariance. The W W OPE is determined by the complete family of structure
coefficients and Jacobi identities.
   Feigin–Frenkel duality uses (158). The central charges of dual pairs agree because the vertex
algebras are isomorphic. This duality is distinct from KZ inverse braiding.

35.9    Critical affine algebras
At k = −h∨ , the affine OPE remains (140), but the Sugawara conformal vector ceases to exist. The
Feigin–Frenkel center becomes large and is identified with opers. KZ equations in their usual WZW
normalization become singular. The critical level is therefore the structural locus where Sugawara
conformal data degenerates and the oper centre emerges.




                                                   159
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields

   The table lists the structures and the comparison maps that distinguish the examples. Each row
realizes the diagonal principle in a different representation category.


36       Strictly noncommutative chiral algebras
36.1     Free ordered chiral algebras
Let M ∈ FactD(X). The free augmented ordered chiral algebra is

                                 TX (M ) = 1 ⊕ M ⊕ M ⊗2 ⊕ M ⊗3 ⊕ · · · ,                           (235)

with concatenation. The tensor products are taken in FactD(X), so each word retains the full chiral
coefficient system of its letters.

Proposition 36.1 (Bar homology of the free algebra). Assume the tensor products in (235) are exact.
The reduced bar of TX (M ) contracts onto M [1]. Equivalently,

                                             TX (M ) 1 ≃ 1 ⊕ M [1]
                                          1 ⊗L

as a conilpotent coalgebra, with zero reduced coproduct on M [1].

Proof. The word-length decomposition gives a fibre sequence of left TX (M )-modules

                                                   mult
                                    TX (M ) ⊗ M −−−→ TX (M ) −→ 1.

On the summand of words of positive length, multiplication is the canonical identification obtained by
separating the last letter; the cofiber is the empty word. Applying 1 ⊗LTX (M ) − leaves the two-term
complex M → 1 with zero reduced differential, hence the class M [1]. Equivalently, the standard extra
degeneracy of the normalized bar contracts every bar word having an internal cut and leaves the
one-letter cogenerator.




                                                    160
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Thus the Koszul dual algebra of a free associative object is the square-zero extension

                                        TX (M )! ≃ 1 ⊕ M ∨ [−1]

when M is dualizable. Noncommutative freedom on one side becomes a first-order infinitesimal object
on the other.

36.2    Square-zero chiral algebras
Conversely, let
                                       A = 1 ⊕ M,            M 2 = 0.
The bar multiplication differential vanishes, and

                                        Barord
                                           X (A) =           M [1]⊗r
                                                       M

                                                       r≥0

with deconcatenation. Its continuous dual is the completed tensor algebra

                                           A! ≃ TbX (M ∨ [−1]).

This pair is the local model of an infinitesimal boundary coupling and its algebra of iterated defect
insertions.

36.3    Quiver chiral algebras
Let Q be a finite quiver, let
                                              S=
                                                     M
                                                           1ei ,
                                                    i∈Q0

and assign an object Ma ∈ FactD(X) to every arrow a : i → j. Put

                                         M=
                                               M
                                                     et(a) Ma es(a) .
                                              a∈Q1

The relative tensor algebra
                                              AQ = TS (M )                                            (236)
is an E1 algebra in factorization D-modules. Multiplication concatenates composable arrows and is
zero otherwise. The augmentation AQ → S forgets nontrivial paths.
    The relative bar
                                       BS (AQ ) = S ⊗LAQ S

retains the ordered path structure. If Q is free over S, it contracts to S ⊕M [1]. If relations R ⊂ M ⊗S M
are imposed, the quadratic coalgebra begins

                                        S ⊕ M [1] ⊕ R[2] ⊕ · · · .

Example 36.2 (Two boundary conditions). For the quiver 1 ⇄vu 2, the objects Mu and Mv are
boundary-changing fields. The composites uv and vu are endomorphisms of different boundaries and
belong to different vertex endomorphism spaces and are independently specified. Their chiral insertion


                                                    161
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


points may collide on X, while the order of composition is fixed by the transverse interval. This is
strict noncommutative chirality in its smallest form.

   A cyclic potential W determines a cyclic functional on the completed path algebra. After choosing
the standard Ginzburg or cyclic A∞ construction, its cyclic derivatives enter the differential and are
the classical equations of motion of the corresponding open-string field theory. The ribbon genus of
Section 26 is then the genus of the resulting open-string Feynman surface.

36.4    Matrix and Chan–Paton extensions
Let V be a commutative algebra object in FactD(X) and let E be a finite-dimensional vector space.
The matrix extension
                                       A = End(E) ⊗ V                                       (237)
is an E1 algebra in FactD(X). Its multiplication is matrix multiplication times the chiral factorization
product. The OPE coefficients are End(E)-valued and preserve their order.
    The algebra (237) is Morita equivalent to V . Hence

                                        Zord,ch (A) ≃ Zord,ch (V )

and their perfect module categories have equivalent centres. The open amplitudes differ by Chan–Paton
traces, producing powers of dim E on ribbon graphs.

Proposition 36.3 (Chan–Paton weight). For a connected ribbon graph Γ contributing to a cyclic
amplitude of (237), contraction of matrix indices contributes

                                              (dim E)F (Γ) ,

where F (Γ) is the number of index faces. For a closed ribbon surface of genus h with b boundary
components, this is (dim E)2−2h−b after the standard normalization of vertices and propagators.

Proof. Write every propagator and vertex in matrix indices. Along each ribbon edge the two Kronecker
symbols identify adjacent indices. Following an index strand around a closed face leaves one freely
summed index and contributes dim E. Independent faces give the product (dim E)F (Γ) . With the
conventional factor (dim E)−1 for each normalized propagator and the matching vertex normalization,
Euler’s identity V − E + F = 2 − 2h − b gives (dim E)2−2h−b .

   This is the exact algebraic origin of the ’t Hooft expansion. It belongs to the transverse ribbon
genus and is independent of the genus of the chiral curve.

36.5    Universal enveloping ordered chiral algebras
Let g be a Lie algebra object in FactD(X). Its universal enveloping algebra

                                      UX (g) ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X))

is defined by the usual coequalizer of the tensor algebra by

                                    x ⊗ y − (−1)|x||y| y ⊗ x − [x, y].


                                                   162
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The Poincare–Birkhoff–Witt filtration has

                                          gr UX (g) ≃ SymX (g)

under the standard flatness hypotheses.

Theorem 36.4 (Associative bar and internal Chevalley chains). For a flat Lie object g in FactD(X),
the canonical twisting morphism induces a quasi-isomorphism
                                                 ∼
                                     ChevX (g) −
                                               → Barord
                                                    X UX (g) .
                                                                  


Proof. Filter both sides by symmetric degree. The associated graded map is the ordinary Koszul
resolution of SymX (g) by the exterior coalgebra on g[1]. PBW identifies the associated graded
of the enveloping algebra. Completeness and exactness of the filtration lift the associated-graded
quasi-isomorphism.

    This theorem is the internal Lie–associative Koszul comparison in the pointwise coefficient category.
It is distinct from the Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral Chevalley construction on the Ran space. The internal
Lie bracket maps to the commutator in its enveloping algebra, and internal Lie chains compute the
ordered bar of that enveloping algebra.

36.6    Affine currents as chiral coefficients
The affine current algebra is a Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral Lie algebra: its simple pole is the Lie bracket
and its double pole is the invariant cocycle. Its chiral enveloping construction produces the universal
affine chiral coefficient. This construction takes place in the chiral pseudo-tensor category and is
distinct from the internal enveloping algebra of Theorem 36.4, which begins with a Lie object for the
pointwise tensor product.
    A strictly noncommutative ordered chiral algebra from affine data consists of a transverse associative
object: for example an endomorphism algebra of affine boundary conditions, a quiver of affine modules,
or a free tensor algebra generated by the affine factorisation coefficient. The resulting internal bar
resolves that transverse multiplication. The chiral Chevalley complex resolves the affine chiral Lie
operation. A comparison between them is an enveloping or Morita theorem and is provided by an
enveloping or Morita theorem. In either construction the simple-pole bracket satisfies Jacobi and
invariance of the level cocycle makes the central extension chiral-Lie.

36.7    Weyl and Clifford chiral algebras
Let M carry a perfect pairing ω : M ⊗ M → 1[d]. The tensor algebra modulo

                                      xy − (−1)|x||y| yx = ℏ ω(x, y)                                (238)

produces a Weyl or Clifford object according to parity. In the chiral category, M may be a factorization
D-module and ω may contain a higher normal symbol along the diagonal.
   The relation is quadratic-linear-constant. Its Koszul dual is curved: the constant term becomes the
curvature m0 of the dual coalgebra. The curvature belongs to the algebraic presentation. A Hodge
class on moduli enters through a separate family-index or sewing construction.


                                                     163
R. Lorgat                                                          Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Example 36.5 (The βγ system). The chiral Weyl algebra generated by β and γ has singular relation

                                                           1
                                          β(z)γ(w) ∼          .
                                                          z−w
With matrix Chan–Paton factors, the ordered product is matrix multiplication while the singular
coefficient is the Weyl pairing. The two operations are visibly independent.

Example 36.6 (The bc system). The odd analogue has Clifford relation. Its determinant line
contributes the ghost conformal anomaly. The Weyl–Clifford Koszul exchange is a statement about
quadratic relations; the cancellation of their conformal anomalies is a separate determinant-line
calculation.

36.8    Chiral differential operators
For a smooth variety Y , a sheaf of chiral differential operators exists after its obstruction gerbe
has been trivialized. It supplies a chiral factorisation coefficient whose local fields include functions
and vector fields on Y . The vertex or chiral operation of that coefficient becomes the transverse E1
multiplication after a factorization-compatible associative product has been specified.
   Strict ordered examples are obtained from associative endomorphism objects in a category of
chiral-differential-operator modules, from quiver endomorphisms of boundary conditions, or from
matrix and tensor-algebra extensions of a dualizable coefficient. When Y carries a group action, BRST
or quantum Hamiltonian reduction acts on such an ordered endomorphism algebra provided the BRST
operator is a derivation of the transverse product. The resulting comparison is governed by Section 24.

36.9    Boundary endomorphism algebras
Let B be a factorization category of boundary conditions on X. For objects bi , set

                                         Aij = RHomB (bi , bj ).

Composition gives
                                          Ajk ⊗ Aij −→ Aik .
The direct sum
                                             AB =
                                                    M
                                                          Aij
                                                    i,j

is an algebra over the semisimple idempotent object S = ⊕i 1ei . Its relative bar computes chains of
boundary changes. This is the categorical form of the quiver example and the native algebra of open
strings.

Theorem 36.7 (Morita reconstruction from a boundary generator). Let B be a stable presentable
FactD(X)-module category whose action preserves colimits separately, and let b = ⊕i bi be a compact
generator. Then
                                      Ab = RHomB (b, b)
is an E1 algebra in FactD(X) and

                                        B ≃ ModAb (FactD(X))


                                                  164
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


by the enriched Hom functor.

Proof. Composition makes Ab an E1 algebra in the enriched coefficient category. The adjunction

                                       − ⊗Ab b ⊣ RHomB (b, −)

has equivalence unit on the free module Ab and equivalence counit on the generator b. Both functors
are exact and preserve the colimits used to form the presentable categories. Since b and Ab compactly
generate their respective sides, the unit and counit are equivalences on every object.

   The theorem explains why noncommutative chiral algebras are primary in boundary physics. The
category of boundary conditions comes first; its endomorphism algebra is a coordinate chart on that
category.

36.10    Factorization quantum groups
A factorization quantum group combines an algebra object, a compatible coalgebra or tensor-category
structure, and meromorphic exchange. In the ordered language these pieces occupy different levels:

                      E1 multiplication       :   fusion,
                   factorization coproduct    :   splitting of lines or representations,
                       E2 enhancement         :   exchange,
                         ribbon trace         :   closure and link invariants.

A Yangian possesses all these structures in a compatible completed sense. A generic ordered chiral
algebra possesses only the first.

36.11    A noncommutative Koszul pair
Let V be dualizable and let R ⊂ V ⊗ V be a factorization subobject. Define

                         A = TX (V )/(R),         A!,quad = TX (V ∨ [−1])/(R⊥ ).

The canonical twisting morphism A¡ → A gives the Koszul complex

                                             K(A) = A ⊗ A¡ .

Theorem 36.8 (Quadratic recognition in the ordered chiral category). Assume positive weight,
degreewise dualizability, and strong convergence of the weight filtration. The following are equivalent:

  (i) A¡ → Barord
              X (A) is a quasi-isomorphism;

 (ii) CobarX (A¡ ) → A is a quasi-isomorphism;

(iii) the left and right quadratic Koszul complexes are acyclic off weight zero;

 (iv) TorA (1, 1) is concentrated on the quadratic diagonal.

Proof. The quadratic twisting morphism defines the left and right twisted tensor products and the
comparison maps in (i) and (ii). Component evaluation in FactD(X) preserves tensor products, the


                                                    165
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


quadratic subcoalgebra, and the weight filtration. In each finite weight the fundamental theorem of
Koszul twisting morphisms identifies (i), (ii), and acyclicity of both twisted tensor products. The bar
spectral sequence identifies their homology with TorA (1, 1), so diagonal concentration is equivalent
to the same acyclicity. Strong convergence and conservativity of component evaluation lift these
equivalences to the ordered chiral category.

   When these conditions hold and finite duals exist, dualizing the quadratic coalgebra identifies
A!,quad with the boundary endomorphism algebra RHomA (1, 1).

36.12    What these examples establish
The examples exhibit three independent kinds of noncommutativity.
   Quiver and matrix algebras have noncommutative transverse multiplication even when their chiral
coefficient is free or commutative.
   Internal enveloping algebras have noncommutative transverse multiplication generated by an
internal Lie object. Affine current algebras supply chiral coefficients; a strictly ordered affine example
requires the additional boundary, quiver, tensor, or endomorphism multiplication described above.
   Yangians and line categories add meromorphic braid exchange to noncommutative fusion.
   The ordered chiral language accommodates all three through their typed comparison maps. That
separation is the condition under which their Koszul dualities can be stated correctly.


37      Arithmetic traces and automorphic forms
37.1    Arithmetic enters through periods and traces
The chiral operation is defined over an algebraic or analytic coefficient field. Arithmetic appears
when one takes periods of configuration spaces, traces of monodromy, dimensions of conformal blocks,
theta sums over lattices, or regularized determinants. These operations are functorial shadows. Their
arithmetic is controlled by the geometry of the chosen trace.
   There are two basic sources. Ordered iterated integrals produce multiple zeta values and their elliptic
analogues. Symmetric and modular traces produce modular forms, theta series, and characteristic
numbers. The two sources can interact, and interact through explicit period and trace maps.

37.2    The KZ associator
The regularized holonomy of the KZ connection on P1 \ {0, 1, ∞} is the Drinfeld associator. Its
coefficients are multiple zeta values. The weight filtration is the length of iterated integration. The
pentagon equation expresses compatibility around the five degenerations forming the boundary of
a real associahedral pentagon inside M0,5 ; the complex Deligne–Mumford boundary itself has ten
irreducible divisors.
    This is a direct arithmetic consequence of ordered configuration geometry. Passing to the
Grothendieck ring of the representation category discards the associator but retains fusion mul-
tiplicities. Thus ordered and symmetric traces have visibly different arithmetic content.




                                                   166
R. Lorgat                                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields

of the genus-g conformal-block space is

The cosecant power sums are polynomials in k + 2 with Bernoulli-number coefficients. This arithmetic
follows from the modular tensor category and the geometry of flat connections. A bar-cohomology
dimension formula requires a separate identification of the relevant bar complex with this conformal-
block trace.

37.5    Theta series
For an even lattice L, the degree-g Siegel theta series is
                                                                                           
                                (g)
