It is a Siegel modular form when L is even unimodular. The genus-g partition function of a lattice
theory contains (243) multiplied by an oscillator determinant.
    Theta series are traces over zero modes. They forget the cocycle and many OPE coefficients.
Nonisometric lattices may have equal theta series in low genus. The Schottky form illustrates the first
place at which the two rank-sixteen even-unimodular lattice theta series differ on the full Siegel space,
while their restrictions to the genus-four Jacobian locus agree [59, 66].



                                                            167
R. Lorgat                                                                Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


37.6    Symmetric products and the DMVV formula
Let ϕM (τ, z) be the elliptic genus of a compact Calabi–Yau manifold M . Dijkgraaf–Moore–Verlinde–
Verlinde prove the product formula
                                                                                     −c(nm,ℓ)
                             pN ϕSymN M (τ, z) =                     1 − pn q m y ℓ                        (244)
                       X                               Y
                                                                                                  ,
                      N ≥0                         n>0,m≥0,ℓ∈Z

where c(m, ℓ) are the Fourier coefficients of ϕM [26]. The logarithm of the product is a sum of Hecke
transforms. This is the plethystic exponential of connected single-particle data.
   The formula has the same combinatorial exponential structure as a cofree commutative coalgebra and
as the connected-graph identity (181). The equality of combinatorial forms becomes an identification
of the DMVV state space with a chiral Chevalley coalgebra. after constructing a functor from the
sigma-model or BPS category to chiral factorization data.

37.7    Borcherds products
A weak Jacobi form with integral Fourier coefficients can produce an automorphic product whose
exponents are those coefficients. Gritsenko and Nikulin use such products to construct automorphic
corrections of Lorentzian Kac–Moody algebras [44]. The Igusa cusp form Φ10 is the multiplicative lift
relevant to the K3 elliptic genus, and the DMVV formula is naturally expressed using 1/Φ10 .
   The weight of a Borcherds product is half the constant coefficient of its input in the standard
normalization. This weight is an automorphic invariant. The central charge, lattice rank, and Koszul
data retain their own definitions and can be compared to the automorphic weight through explicit
index formulas.

37.8    K3 and geometric quantum groups
Maulik and Okounkov construct Yangians from the geometry of Nakajima quiver varieties using stable
envelopes and R-matrices [56]. The cohomology of Hilbert schemes of points on a surface carries
Heisenberg and, in suitable settings, larger quantum-group actions. Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima
construct Coulomb-branch algebras that include shifted Yangians [10].
   These are exact geometric constructions of quantum groups. A K3 surface also supplies a lattice
of signature (4, 20), elliptic genera, and Hilbert-scheme Fock spaces. An identification of a K3 Hall
algebra with the ordered Koszul dual of a particular chiral algebra. is established by a functor
preserving products, coproducts, pairings, completions, and R-matrices.

37.9    Moonshine
Moonshine associates modular functions to traces of Monster elements on V ♮ . Borcherds’ proof uses a
generalized Kac–Moody algebra and an automorphic denominator identity. The chiral algebra supplies
the graded representation and its vertex operators; the denominator identity supplies an automorphic
trace.
   Chiral Koszul duality applies to the local chiral operation of V ♮ . Moonshine is additional equivariant
and arithmetic structure. A trace remembers the conjugacy class and graded multiplicities while the
complete OPE is recovered from all vertex-operator products.



                                                      168
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


37.10    Genus one and genus two
Genus one detects characters, modular transformations, and one-variable theta series. Genus two
detects interactions between two cycles and introduces Siegel modular forms. Some distinctions first
appear only on the full Siegel space and disappear on the Jacobian locus. Therefore the phrase “genus
two detects” has mathematical content only after the function, the ambient moduli space, and the
restriction locus have been named.
   The same precision applies to L-functions. A modular form determines an L-function; a graph
amplitude equals a special value after an integral representation or Rankin–Selberg unfolding has
identified the graph amplitude with that special value. An operator-product coefficient becomes an
L-value through this explicit period map.

37.11    Arithmetic duality
Dualizing a local system sends its period matrix to the contragredient one. Poincare duality pairs
homology and cohomology periods. Functional equations of L-functions reflect duality of motives.
These are distinct dualities, connected only when the relevant realization functors commute.
  The common language is a diagram of realizations:

 algebraic coefficient object −→ de Rham local system −→ Betti monodromy −→ period or trace.

Chiral Koszul duality acts at the first stage. Arithmetic enters at the final stages.


38      Quantum field theory in the same language
38.1    Observables as a factorization algebra
A perturbative quantum field theory assigns a cochain complex of observables to each open set.
Observables with disjoint supports multiply. Locality and descent make these complexes a factorization
algebra [14, 15]. The differential combines the equations of motion, gauge symmetry, and quantum
corrections.
    A holomorphic theory on a complex curve produces a holomorphic factorization algebra. Under
suitable regularity, its local observables determine a vertex algebra or chiral algebra. A topological–
holomorphic theory on M × X may produce a chiral algebra on a boundary parallel to X. The OPE
is the short-distance expansion of the factorization product.

38.2    Renormalization and compactified configurations
A Feynman graph with vertices at points of a manifold defines a differential form or distribution on the
configuration space of distinct vertices. Divergences occur at collision diagonals. Compactifying the
configuration space resolves these diagonals into boundary faces indexed by forests. Renormalization
extends the weight to the boundary by adding local counterterms.
   The forest formula and the Fulton–MacPherson forest resolution have the same incidence category.
The coefficient systems differ: a Feynman weight contains propagators, gauge-fixing data, and densities;
the chiral Chevalley complex contains the diagonal operation. A comparison theorem identifies them
when the boundary asymptotics of the propagator reproduce the chiral operation.



                                                  169
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


38.3    Classical and quantum master equations
Let E be the BV complex of fields with odd Poisson bracket {−, −}. A classical action satisfies

                                             1
                                         QS + {S, S} = 0.
                                             2
A quantization satisfies
                                            1
                                      QS + {S, S} + ℏ∆S = 0
                                            2
after renormalization. The anomaly is the cohomology class of the quantum-master defect.
   The modular equation (178) has the same form because both are generated by graph contractions.
The identification is exact when the Feynman rules define an algebra over the same modular cooperad.
The boundary strata of compactified configurations then prove the master equation by Stokes’ theorem.

38.4    Chern–Simons theory and affine currents
Chern–Simons theory on a three-manifold with the standard chiral boundary condition induces a
chiral Wess–Zumino–Witten boundary theory. The boundary currents form an affine Kac–Moody
algebra. In the abelian theory, the boundary current OPE is proportional to the level and is computed
directly from the Green kernel. In the nonabelian theory, one-loop effects produce the familiar shifted
coupling in KZ and surgery formulas.
   Gwilliam and Williams construct a one-loop exact quantization of a mixed holomorphic–topological
presentation of Chern–Simons theory and analyze its boundary behavior [45]. The factorization
algebra of boundary observables is the analytic counterpart of the affine chiral algebra.
   Wilson lines form a braided tensor category. Their exchange is described by quantum-group
R-matrices; their closure produces knot invariants. This is the physical realization of Theorems 20.2
and 21.2.

38.5    Four-dimensional Chern–Simons and integrability
In four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory, line operators are topological along a surface and holo-
morphic in a spectral parameter. Their OPE produces rational, trigonometric, or elliptic R-matrices
depending on the curve. The Yang–Baxter equation follows from moving three lines through an
isotopy in the regular configuration space. Transfer matrices commute because separated line networks
are isotopic.
   The Gaudin model arises from the semiclassical rational connection. Spin chains arise from choosing
representations on parallel lines. Baxter relations are exact sequences or functional relations in the
representation category. These structures are ordered because the sequence and positions of lines
matter.

38.6    BRST, ghosts, and strings
Gauge fixing introduces ghosts and a BRST differential. The total central charge of matter and ghosts
controls the conformal anomaly of the worldsheet theory. For the bosonic string, the (b, c) ghost
system has central charge −26; BRST nilpotence requires matter central charge 26 together with the
usual normal-ordering or intercept condition in the state-space formulation.



                                                 170
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Polyakov’s path integral produces determinant lines over moduli. The Weyl anomaly is the
curvature of these determinant lines. The Belavin–Knizhnik theorem and the theory of Quillen metrics
make this relation geometric. The cancellation of matter and ghost determinant lines is the global
form of the central-charge condition.
   This anomaly cancellation is a tensor-product cancellation in the conformal-block and determinant-
line theory.

38.7    Brown–Henneaux and Cardy
Three-dimensional gravity with negative cosmological constant has asymptotic Virasoro symmetry
with
                                                   3ℓ
                                              c=                                            (245)
                                                  2G
by the Brown–Henneaux theorem [12]. In a unitary modular-invariant two-dimensional CFT with a
sparse light spectrum and vacuum dominance, the Cardy formula gives the high-energy density of
states. Substituting (245) reproduces the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy of the BTZ black hole in the
semiclassical regime.
   The rigorous algebraic input is the boundary Virasoro algebra and modular covariance of its
partition function. A nonperturbative theory of three-dimensional gravity additionally requires a
spectrum, integration cycle, modular completion, and a bulk–boundary comparison with the Virasoro
centre.

38.8    Entanglement in two-dimensional conformal field theory
For the vacuum of a unitary CFT on the line, the entanglement entropy of an interval of length L
with ultraviolet cutoff ϵ is
                                             c + c̄    L
                                       SA =         log + const.                                  (246)
                                               6       ϵ
The replica derivation uses twist fields and conformal maps. The coefficient is fixed by the left- and
right-moving stress-tensor anomalies; in a parity-invariant theory with c = c̄ it reduces to c/3.
   Equation (246) is a trace statement. The OPE, braid monodromy, and representation category
provide the complementary operator-algebraic data. Two CFTs with the same c may have different
entanglement spectra and operator algebras.

38.9    Bulk–boundary maps
A boundary condition for a factorization algebra of bulk observables defines an action on boundary
observables. By the universal property of the derived center, there is a map

                                       Obsbulk |∂ −→ Zch
                                                      der
                                                          (A).

Costello and Gaiotto use this structure in twisted holography, where protected bulk sectors and
boundary vertex algebras can sometimes be compared exactly [16]. The comparison is a theorem of
the particular twisted theory, a theorem of the specified twisted bulk–boundary system.
   A decoupled bulk factor lies in the homotopy fibre of boundary restriction. A fixed boundary
algebra may therefore admit several bulk completions with different global observables. The centre
classifies their central boundary actions, while the homotopy fibre records the additional bulk sectors.


                                                 171
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


38.10    Celestial chiral algebras
In celestial and twistor constructions, scattering amplitudes can be encoded by correlators of chiral
algebras. Costello–Paquette and collaborators construct examples in which gauge-theory amplitudes
arise from current-algebra correlators and their deformations [17]. Associativity of the celestial OPE
is then a nontrivial constraint on loop corrections.
    The precise statement depends on the background geometry, asymptotic states, and the map from
scattering observables to chiral fields. Soft theorems provide currents in the chiral algebra, and a
theory-specific comparison map identifies the resulting current insertions with the gravitational soft
expansion.

38.11    Calabi–Yau categories and topological strings
A Calabi–Yau A∞ category has a cyclic pairing. Costello’s construction associates to suitable Calabi–
Yau categories a two-dimensional topological conformal field theory whose closed states are Hochschild
chains. The Connes operator and the cyclic pairing supply the BV structure.
   A holomorphic field theory on a Calabi–Yau variety may produce factorization algebras and BPS
operator algebras. Cohomological Hall algebras, shifted Yangians, and Borcherds products arise in
specific geometries. Passing from such a category to a chiral algebra on a curve requires an explicit
compactification, reduction, or boundary functor. The dimension shift and cyclic pairing supply inputs
to the functor once the compactification, reduction, and boundary morphisms are specified.

38.12    The physical comparison diagram
The exact relation between the mathematical and physical objects is a commutative diagram of maps,
a commutative diagram in the relevant categories:

                       coChevch (g)                    algebraic factorization coalgebra
                                                                                                (247)

              renormalized collision complex                        Obs∂

The bulk-to-boundary action has the separately typed form

                                      Obsbulk |∂ −→ Zch
                                                     der
                                                         (Obs∂ ).                               (248)

The vertical maps are Riemann–Hilbert, analytification, or explicit field-theoretic comparison maps.
When they are quasi-isomorphisms, the mathematical and physical master equations are the same
equation in two models.


39      The physical derivation of ordered chirality
39.1    A local experiment
Consider a quantum field theory on
                                               X × Rt ,



                                                 172
R. Lorgat                                                                    Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


holomorphic along the complex curve X and topological along the oriented coordinate t. Let Oi (zi , ti )
be local observables with
                                       t1 < t2 < · · · < tn .
Topological invariance permits the ti to move inside a fixed order chamber while preserving the
correlator. Holomorphy permits the zi to move on the complement of collision diagonals. Hence a
correlator is locally constant in the ordered chamber of the ti and meromorphic or distributional in
the zi .
   The short-distance product has the form

                         O1 (z1 , t1 ) · · · On (zn , tn ) ∼       Cα (z1 , . . . , zn )Oα (z, t),             (249)
                                                               X

                                                               α

where the coefficient Cα is a chiral distribution and the multiplication order is the order of the ti .
Formula (249) is the physical content of an E1 algebra in factorization D-modules.

Principle 39.1 (Ordered OPE principle). Holomorphic collision determines the coefficient distribution.
Topological ordering determines the associative word. The full product is their interchange law.

Proof. Choose pairwise disjoint product disks Ui × Ii ⊂ X × R with I1 < · · · < In . The factorisation
map first multiplies observables according to the linear order of the intervals and then transports the
result along the holomorphic configuration of the Ui . Shrinking the Ui toward a diagonal produces the
chiral coefficient distribution, while isotopy of the ordered intervals preserves the word. Associativity
of factorisation and compatibility with products of embeddings give the interchange identity.

   This principle is more primitive than a vertex-algebra expansion. A local coordinate on X converts
the coefficient distribution into modes. Choosing an ordering prescription on R converts the topological
factor into a time-ordered product.

39.2    Topological descent and the E1 product
Let Q be the cohomological supercharge of the twist. Topological invariance in the real direction
means that the translation generator is Q-exact:

                                                    Pt = [Q, Gt ].

For a Q-closed observable O,
                                                 ∂t O = [Q, Gt O].
The difference between insertions at two values of t is therefore Q-exact. Ordered products are
independent of the positions on cohomology.
    The identity Pt = [Q, Gt ] is the first descent equation. A chain-level E1 structure requires the
coherent extension of this identity to families of interval embeddings: the descendant operators must
satisfy the Cartan homotopy relations and their higher compatibility on compactified configuration
cells. Integrating the coherent descendants gives the A∞ operations. The boundary of an associahedron
is the sum of its parenthesized degenerations, so Stokes’ theorem gives the Stasheff identities.

Theorem 39.2 (Descent produces ordered multiplication). Let a factorization algebra of observables
on X × R be holomorphic in X and equipped with coherent topological descent in the real direction, so


                                                          173
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


that its restriction to intervals is a locally constant factorization algebra. Then restriction to product
disks determines an E1 algebra in holomorphic factorization observables on X. A local operator Gt
with Pt = [Q, Gt ] exhibits the first coherence; when it extends to the stated descent datum, the product
on Q-cohomology is independent of the transverse positions.

Proof. Locally constant factorisation algebras on an oriented line are algebras over the little-interval
operad. For each disk U ⊂ X, restriction to U × R therefore gives an E1 algebra. Holomorphic
factorisation in U makes these algebras a factorisation object on X, and factorisation on product
embeddings makes the E1 operations morphisms of that object. The descent identity integrates to
                                                           Z t2
                                   O(t2 ) − O(t1 ) = [Q,          Gt O dt],
                                                            t1

so ordered insertions at different points of one chamber define the same cohomology class. Higher
Cartan homotopies extend this equality over compactified interval configurations; Stokes’ formula on
their cells is the collection of Stasheff identities.

   The theorem is the physical proof of Theorem 10.2 for a cohomological field theory.

39.3    Why the OPE is a D-module
The coefficient in (249) is a distribution with a supported singular part at the collision locus. Ward
identities imply differential equations in the insertion points. Contact terms are distributions supported
on diagonals. A right D-module records both the differential equations away from collisions and the
allowed supported terms at collisions.
    For a conserved holomorphic current,
                                                   ¯ =0
                                                   ∂J
away from other insertions. In a correlator,
                                               1
                                        ∂¯z       = πδ (2) (z − w)
                                              z−w
in the distributional normalization. Thus the singular OPE and the contact Ward identity are the
same distribution seen before and after applying ∂.  ¯ The D-module language retains this equality
intrinsically, before any choice of Laurent contour.

39.4    Boundary conditions and open strings
Let the theory occupy X × R≥0 and let b be a boundary condition at t = 0. Boundary operators form
the endomorphism algebra of b. If several boundary conditions bi are present, a boundary-changing
field lies in
                                       Aij = RHom(bi , bj ).
Composition has the order
                                              Ajk ⊗ Aij −→ Aik .
This is the open-string product. The insertion point on X supplies its chiral dependence.
   The strip with boundary conditions bi and bj has state complex Aij . Gluing strips is composition.
Closing a strip into an annulus gives Hochschild homology when a cyclic trace and the required

                                                     174
R. Lorgat                                                               Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


dualizability are present. Thickening the Feynman graphs of a cyclic open-string interaction then
gives ribbon surfaces. These hypotheses are exactly the transverse modular data of Section 26.

39.5    The interval bar as a propagator-independent amplitude
Place the augmentation boundary at the two ends of an interval. Factorization homology gives
                                             Z
                                                         A ≃ 1 ⊗L
                                                                A 1.
                                             ([0,1],∂)

A cellular decomposition of the interval yields the bar complex. Its differential composes adjacent
operator insertions. This construction is the exact topological interval gluing of the E1 algebra.
   A Feynman expansion is another model of the same derived tensor product when the field theory
admits an action and a gauge fixing. The comparison map sends a bar word to the corresponding
time-ordered amplitude. Its proof is a homological perturbation or factorization-excision theorem, an
explicit comparison between time-ordered amplitudes and simplicial interval gluing.

39.6    A second topological direction and exchange
Now place the theory on X × R2 . Two parallel lines can move around one another. The exchange
path defines
                            R12 (z1 , z2 ) : Lz1 ⊗ Lz2 −→ Lz2 ⊗ Lz1 .
Three-line isotopy gives the Yang–Baxter equation. The exchange depends meromorphically on z1 − z2
because a singularity occurs when the spectral points collide.
   This derivation explains the codimension hierarchy:

                                 R1 : E1 fusion,
                                 R2 : E2 braid exchange,
                                 R3 : E3 higher linking operations.

The chiral curve is a coefficient direction and contributes an additional little-disks direction when its
holomorphic translations become topological.

39.7    Four-dimensional Chern–Simons theory
The action
                                    1                        1          1
                                         Z                                         
                          S[A] =                 ω∧            (A, dA) + (A, [A, A])
                                   2πℏ   Σ×X                 2          6
is topological on the oriented surface Σ and holomorphic on the curve X. A Wilson line lies on a
curve in Σ and at a point of X. The point of X is its spectral parameter.
    At tree level, exchange of two Wilson lines gives the Casimir kernel

                                                               Ω
                                                  r(z) =         .
                                                               z
The configuration of three lines has two isotopic resolutions, which gives the classical Yang–Baxter
equation. Quantization replaces r by an R-matrix. The one-loop anomaly of line gauge invariance
forces the Yangian deformation in the rational theory. In the formal perturbative rational theory it is


                                                         175
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


selected by the gauge-theory anomaly and Ward identities [19, 20].
   For X = C× and for elliptic X, the propagator has trigonometric and elliptic periodicity. The
quantum symmetry changes accordingly. The spectral curve is part of the physics.

39.8    Three-dimensional gauge theory and boundary chiral algebra
A holomorphic twist of a three-dimensional supersymmetric theory on X × R≥0 may admit a boundary
condition preserving the holomorphic supercharge. Boundary local operators form a chiral algebra on
X. Boundary line junctions carry an ordered product inherited from the normal direction. Bulk lines
act by modules.
   In a Lagrangian theory, the boundary algebra is computed from boundary fields and BRST
reduction. Gauge anomalies must cancel for the boundary condition to exist quantum mechanically.
Different boundary conditions can give different E1 chiral algebras with equivalent central bulk actions.
This is the physical origin of Morita and Koszul dual boundary descriptions.

39.9    Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction as gauge fixing
Start from affine currents and couple them to ghosts imposing a moment-map constraint. The BRST
charge is                                                              

                                  X

                                 
                                    a

The equation Q2 = 0 follows from the current algebra, invariance of the level cocycle, and cancellation
of the gauge anomaly. The reduced chiral algebra is HQ (A).
   Because Q is a derivation of the transverse product, it acts on the ordered bar. The total differential
is
                                            D = bA + Qbar .
The spectral sequence obtained by first taking BRST cohomology compares the bar of the reduced
algebra with the reduction of the bar. Degeneration is a theorem of the particular good grading and
filtration. The higher W -operations are the homotopy-transferred remnants of affine multiplication
and ghost contraction.

39.10    Polyakov’s determinant and the chiral anomaly
For a free scalar on a Riemann surface,

                                           Z[g] = (det ′ ∆g )−1/2

up to zero-mode volume. Under g 7→ e2σ g, zeta regularization gives the Polyakov action

                                     Z[e2σ g]    1
                                                      Z              
                                 log          =             |∇σ|2 + Rσ dvol.
                                      Z[g]      24π

For a theory of central charge c, the right side is multiplied by c. The same coefficient appears in the
fourth-order pole of the stress tensor because both measure the central extension of local conformal
transformations.



                                                      176
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


    On a family of curves, the determinant forms a line bundle with Quillen connection. Its curvature
is computed by the family index theorem. This is the exact bridge from the local Virasoro cocycle
to the Hodge class. The affine level, ribbon genus, and Koszul curvature retain their independent
definitions.

39.11    Worldsheet sewing
Choose local coordinates zw = q around two punctures. Sewing inserts

                                              q L0 −c/24

between dual states. The factor q −c/24 is the vacuum energy on the cylinder. The sum over a
homogeneous basis is the inverse two-point pairing. Convergence for |q| < 1 is the analytic sewing
theorem.
   A nonseparating sewing increases the genus of the chiral curve. An annular trace of the transverse
E1 algebra increases ribbon genus. The two operations can occur in the same amplitude, giving the
bidegree of Section 26.

39.12    The Chern–Simons/WZW comparison theorem and its hypotheses
For compact gauge group at integral level, and after the standard choice of polarization and framing
data, the Chern–Simons/WZW and Reshetikhin–Turaev constructions give the fundamental example
in which the quantized surface state spaces, boundary current algebra, Wilson-line labels, conformal
blocks, and ribbon-category invariants are connected by comparison theorems.
   The boundary current algebra is affine Kac–Moody. Canonical quantization on a surface gives
the WZW conformal-block space. Wilson lines are labelled by admissible representations. The
Chern–Simons/WZW and Drinfeld–Kohno comparison theorems identify their exchange with the
corresponding quantum-group braiding. Closing lines with the ribbon trace gives link invariants.
Under these comparison theorems, the framing dependence of the three-dimensional theory matches
the ribbon twist and the projective anomaly data of the WZW conformal blocks.
   Each statement has a different mathematical carrier:

                           current OPE          :   chiral D-module,
                       boundary composition     :   E1 algebra,
                         Wilson exchange        :   E2 line category,
                           surface states       :   conformal blocks,
                          framing phase         :   invertible anomaly theory.

The strength of the example lies in the comparison maps between these carriers, among these distinct
carriers.

39.13    Large N and the double genus
For N coincident branes, boundary operators acquire matrix indices. A connected ribbon graph
contributes N 2−2h−b . Independently, a closed-string worldsheet of genus g contributes gs2g−2 . Open–
closed duality can reorganize the first expansion into the second after summing boundaries and
applying a dynamical transition.


                                                 177
R. Lorgat                                                            Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Before that transition, the amplitude is naturally
                                       X
                                               gs2g−2 N 2−2h−b Fg,h,b .
                                       g,h,b

The bimodular formalism is the algebraic form of this double expansion. The relation between gs and
N is a physical duality parameter, a parameter of the physical duality relating the two expansions.

39.14    Holographic reconstruction
Let A be the ordered chiral algebra of a boundary condition. Bulk observables act centrally on A,
giving
                                    β : Obsbulk −→ Zord,ch (A).
If the boundary condition is complete, β is a quasi-isomorphism. Then the protected bulk local algebra
is reconstructed from the boundary. Two boundary descriptions have the same centre when an explicit
Morita context identifies their bimodule categories; a Koszul equivalence of augmentation-generated
sectors upgrades to this stronger statement when it extends to an explicit Morita context on the full
bimodule categories.
    This is the precise algebraic statement behind twisted holography. It reconstructs a protected local
sector. A metric, Hilbert space, nonperturbative spectrum, and integration cycle require additional
physical structure.

39.15    The field-theoretic meaning of the dual
The augmentation A → 1 is a distinguished boundary or defect. Its derived endomorphism algebra

                                          A! = RHomA (1, 1)

acts on deformations of that defect. The bar complex

                                                    1 ⊗L
                                                       A1

is the complex of interval amplitudes between two copies of the defect. This is why Koszul duality
repeatedly appears in line-operator physics: it exchanges local bulk couplings near the defect with
operators living on the defect.
    The dual is a completion near the chosen augmentation. Sectors connected to the chosen boundary
condition form the completed local theory recovered by the double dual. The physical meaning of
double duality is therefore completion of the local theory around the chosen vacuum.

39.16    The physical theorem in one sentence
A holomorphic–topological quantum field theory whose protected observables are product-generated,
algebraizable as factorisable D-modules, locally constant in the ordered direction, and anomaly-free
produces an ordered chiral algebra: collision on the complex curve gives the chiral coefficient and
ordered fusion gives the E1 product. A second topological direction supplies exchange, a cyclic trace
supplies ribbon loops, variation of the curve supplies conformal sewing, and each anomaly is the
invertible theory obstructing the corresponding gluing.


                                                      178
R. Lorgat                                                               Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


40     Replica defects, cyclic bars, and entanglement
Entanglement is a property of a state and a factorization of its observable algebra. In two dimensions,
the replica construction represents the required traces by branched covers and cyclic permutation
defects. The chiral algebra supplies local twist operators and conformal blocks; the full state and the
antiholomorphic sector supply the density matrix.

40.1    Twisted Hochschild chains
Let A be a unital E1 algebra and let σ ∈ Aut(A). The σ-twisted bimodule σ A has left action
a · m = σ(a)m and its usual right action m · a = ma. The twisted Hochschild chain complex is

40.4    Conformal-block decomposition
For several intervals, the replica surface has positive genus and the twist-field correlation function
decomposes into conformal blocks. For two intervals, the four twist insertions depend on the cross-ratio

40.5    Topological entanglement and categorical trace
For a unitary modular tensor category with simple objects a, quantum dimensions da , and total
quantum dimension                            s
                                             D=
                                                     X
                                                          d2a ,
                                                      a

the vacuum entanglement entropy of a simply connected region in the associated (2 + 1)-dimensional
topological phase has the universal constant term

                                             Stop = − log D.                                        (262)

The same category supplies the Reshetikhin–Turaev trace, and the categorical dimension is the trace
of the identity line. Equation (262) is therefore the logarithm of a categorical normalization of the
line sector.

40.6    Bulk–boundary interpretation
A complete boundary condition gives an open–closed map

                                          Obsbulk −→ ZE1 (A).

The entangling defect is a module for the replica crossed product (254). A bulk replica path integral
and a boundary twisted Hochschild trace agree when the open–closed comparison extends to the
replica defect category. This statement gives the exact bridge between bulk entanglement and the
cyclic bar of the ordered boundary algebra.
   The replica construction therefore adds a concrete physical realization to the chiral Koszul language:

  ordered interval product −→ cyclic bar −→ permutation defect,
        permutation defect −→ branched-cover amplitude −→ Renyi and entanglement entropies.

Every arrow is a gluing operation, and every scalar entropy is the final logarithmic trace of a structured
defect amplitude.


41     Higher-dimensional sources and reduction to a curve
41.1    Pushforward is the source of chirality
Let Y be a compact framed d-manifold and let p : Y × X × R → X × R. Suppose F is a factorization
algebra that is locally constant in the Y and R directions and holomorphic in X. Define
                                                          Z
                                     (pfact
                                       ∗ F)(U × I) :=                 F.                            (263)
                                                            Y ×U ×I

Excision gives factorization maps on the target.

Theorem 41.1 (Topological pushforward to ordered chirality). Assume the local constancy, dualis-
ability, and excision hypotheses required for factorisation homology, and assume that the pushforward
is product-generated and its holomorphic coefficient admits the factorisable D-module realisation of


                                                   181
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Theorem 10.1. Then (263) is a rectangular holomorphic–topological factorisation algebra on X × R
and determines an ordered chiral algebra in AlgE1 (FactD(X)).

Proof. Fubini for factorization homology identifies integration over disjoint product opens with
iterated integration over Y and over the target open. Local constancy in Y and R is preserved by the
pushforward. Holomorphic differential equations in X commute with integration in the topological
directions. Weiss descent follows from excision. Apply Theorem 10.2.

   The theorem gives the invariant shape of the higher-dimensional constructions treated here under
these hypotheses. The source is a factorization algebra; the reduction is factorization homology; the
output is an E1 algebra in chiral coefficients.

41.2    Dimensional bookkeeping
If a theory is locally constant in m transverse directions, its local operators carry Em structure.
Integrating over a framed r-manifold with r < m leaves Em−r structure. A holomorphic curve remains
a coefficient direction. Thus a theory on

                                            Y r × X × Rm−r

can reduce to an Em−r algebra in FactD(X).
   This formula replaces a universal “chirality ladder.” The output depends on the actual number of
topological directions and on the tangential structure used in the pushforward.

41.3    Calabi–Yau categories
A smooth proper Calabi–Yau A∞ category C has a nondegenerate cyclic trace. Its open sector is
the family of morphism complexes RHom(E, F ) with A∞ composition. Its closed state complex is
Hochschild homology HH• (C). The Connes operator and the cyclic pairing supply the circle and
surface operations of the associated topological conformal field theory.
   These data are topological. To obtain a chiral algebra on X, one needs a family Cx varying
holomorphically with x ∈ X, or a holomorphic field theory whose boundary category is C. The ordered
chiral algebra is then the endomorphism object of a chosen boundary generator, or the pushforward
(263).
Theorem 41.2 (Categorical source theorem). Let C be a stable presentable FactD(X)-module category
whose action preserves colimits separately, let b be a compact generator, and let the ordered topological
direction act by composition of boundary morphisms. Then

                                           Ab = RHomC (b, b)

is an ordered chiral algebra and the enriched Morita functor gives an equivalence
                                                  ≃
                               RHomC (b, −) : C −−→ ModAb (FactD(X)).

A Calabi–Yau trace on C induces the transverse cyclic structure of Theorem 26.1 on Ab .

Proof. Composition of endomorphisms gives Ab its E1 multiplication. Factorisation of the enriched
Hom objects over disjoint configurations gives the chiral coefficient, and functoriality of composition

                                                  182
R. Lorgat                                                             Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


makes multiplication a morphism of factorisation objects. The adjunction

                                         − ⊗Ab b ⊣ RHomC (b, −)

has unit and counit equivalences on the generators Ab and b. Exactness, preservation of colimits,
and compact generation extend those equivalences to the presentable categories. A Calabi–Yau trace
pairs RHom(b0 , b1 ) with RHom(b1 , b0 ) cyclically; specializing to b0 = b1 = b gives a cyclic trace on Ab ,
compatible with the factorisation maps by naturality of the categorical trace.

   This theorem is the correct passage from a Calabi–Yau category to noncommutative chirality. It
requires a factorization family over the curve; the Calabi–Yau trace supplies cyclic contractions once
the factorization family over the curve has been constructed.

41.4    Naturality of obstruction classes
Let g and h be complete filtered L∞ algebras controlling two deformation problems, and let

                                                F : g −→ h

be a filtered L∞ morphism. It sends Maurer–Cartan elements to Maurer–Cartan elements and gauge
equivalences to gauge equivalences.

Proposition 41.3 (Transgression of obstructions). Let α≤r be a Maurer–Cartan solution modulo
filtration F r+1 g, with obstruction

                                        or+1 (α) ∈ H 2 (grr+1 g, dα ).

Let F1α denote the linear term of the L∞ morphism twisted by α≤r . The obstruction to extending
F∗ (α≤r ) is
                              [grr+1 F1α ] or+1 (α) ∈ H 2 (grr+1 h, dF∗ (α) ).

Proof. Write the Maurer–Cartan curvature as
                                                      X 1
                                         Rg (α) =            ℓgn (α⊗n ).
                                                      n≥1
                                                          n!

The L∞ identities give the curvature identity
                                                X 1
                                Rh (F∗ (α)) =              Fm+1 Rg (α), α⊗m .
                                                                             

                                                m≥0
                                                      m!

Modulo F r+2 , the right-hand side is precisely F1α applied to the filtration-(r + 1) curvature. Twisted
L∞ functoriality states that F1α is a cochain map from (g, dα ) to (h, dF∗ (α) ). Passing to the associated
graded and then to cohomology proves the formula.

   The proposition gives the rigorous form of a Calabi–Yau, Hall, or gauge-theoretic obstruction
becoming a chiral obstruction: the comparison is the twisted linear term of a constructed filtered L∞
morphism.

41.5    Cohomological Hall algebras
Let M be a moduli stack of objects in a suitable abelian or derived category. Correspondences of
short exact sequences define a convolution product on an appropriate homology theory of M. The
resulting cohomological Hall algebra is an E1 algebra. Its multiplication is noncommutative because
extension flags are ordered.
   A coproduct requires additional geometry, typically localization, a stability condition, or a choice
of chamber. A Drinfeld double requires a nondegenerate Hopf pairing. An R-matrix requires a
stable-envelope or line-exchange construction. These are separate structures.

Criterion 41.4 (Hall-to-quantum-group construction). A completed Hall algebra together with the
following data determines a quasitriangular completed Hopf algebra on the chosen representation
category:

  (i) a compatible completed coproduct;

 (ii) a nondegenerate pairing and a double;

(iii) a PBW theorem and a presentation of the relation ideal;

 (iv) a universal or categorical R-matrix;

 (v) convergence of the completion on the chosen representation category.

Proof. The completed coproduct and counit give the bialgebra structure. The nondegenerate pairing
identifies the negative half with the continuous dual of the positive half and constructs the Drinfeld
double; the PBW theorem identifies the completed vector space and proves that the displayed relation
ideal presents it. The universal R-matrix furnished in (iv) intertwines the coproduct with its opposite
and satisfies the two hexagon identities, hence the quantum Yang–Baxter equation. Rigidity of
the representation category supplies the antipode, while (v) makes every structure map continuous
and every matrix coefficient convergent. These operations are exactly the data of a quasitriangular
completed Hopf algebra.

    The ordered chiral pushforward of a Hall theory carries the E1 multiplication. Transport of items
(i)–(v) through the pushforward identifies its line category with the reconstructed quantum group.

41.6    Coulomb branches and shifted Yangians
For three-dimensional N = 4 gauge theories, the Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima construction
defines Coulomb-branch algebras by convolution on affine-Grassmannian spaces. In type A quiver
examples, quantized Coulomb branches are truncated shifted Yangians. The coweight shift records
magnetic or boundary data.
   In the holomorphic–topological twist, these algebras act on line defects. A boundary reduction to
X gives a factorization family of modules with spectral parameter. The ordered chiral algebra records
fusion of these lines. The BFN identification supplies the named shifted Yangian after the geometric
convolution algebra and the Drinfeld presentation have been compared.




                                                 184
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


41.7    K3 geometry
Let S be a K3 surface. Its Mukai lattice

                              H(S,
                              e    Z) = H 0 (S, Z) ⊕ H 2 (S, Z) ⊕ H 4 (S, Z)

has signature (4, 20). Nakajima correspondences give a Heisenberg action on

                                                    H ∗ (S [n] ).
                                              M

                                              n≥0

The elliptic genera of the symmetric products satisfy the Dijkgraaf–Moore–Verlinde–Verlinde product
formula. The reciprocal Igusa cusp form encodes the second-quantized K3 elliptic genus.
   These are exact statements about cohomology, modular forms, and second quantization. A
specialization functor carrying their products, coproducts, and traces defines the corresponding
ordered chiral algebra and Koszul dual.
Principle 41.5 (K3 comparison datum). A common ordered chiral realization of K3 geometry consists
of explicit functors from the Mukai-lattice, Nakajima, Hall, elliptic-genus, Siegel-modular, and line-
defect constructions to one factorisation category, together with natural transformations identifying
their products, coproducts, pairings, and traces.

Proof. The six inputs occupy different native categories: an integral quadratic lattice, a representation
on Hilbert-scheme cohomology, a convolution algebra, a Jacobi-form index, a section over Siegel
moduli, and a braided tensor category. Each structure reaches an ordered chiral algebra through a
definite functor: lattice vertex construction, factorisation pushforward, Hall convolution, trace, sewing,
or Tannaka reconstruction. A common realization is therefore precisely a cone over this diagram whose
comparison transformations preserve the named operations. Such a cone supplies one chiral object;
conversely, any chiral object realizing all six structures furnishes these functors and transformations
by restriction to the corresponding operations.

    A valid construction may begin with a K3-associated field theory on S × X × R, integrate over S
or a cycle in S, and apply Theorem 41.1. Its comparison with a Hall algebra or a Borcherds product
is then a theorem about that pushforward.

41.8    Borcherds products and scalar traces
A Borcherds lift takes a suitable vector-valued modular or Jacobi form to an automorphic product.
The weight is determined by the constant term of the input. The product can be interpreted as a
denominator identity of a generalized Kac–Moody algebra in favorable cases.
    The automorphic product is a scalar or section of a line bundle. It may arise as a trace, index, or
partition function of a categorical or chiral object. The trace has a fibre consisting of the categories
with that index. The ordered multiplication, braiding, and extension data distinguish points in this
fibre.

41.9    Omega backgrounds and BPS algebras
An Omega background makes rotations equivariantly exact and can reduce a higher-dimensional field
theory to a noncommutative algebra of protected operators. In M-theory and gauge-theory examples,

                                                     185
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


the resulting algebras include Yangians and their deformations. The deformation parameters are
equivariant weights; line-defect spectral parameters are positions in a residual holomorphic curve.
   The algebraic structure is obtained from the factorization OPE of protected operators. Koszul
duality appears when local bulk observables are coupled to a defect and one computes derived
endomorphisms of the trivial defect. The named BPS algebra follows from a separate comparison
with geometric correspondences or representation-theoretic generators.

41.10    Shifted symplectic geometry
Derived moduli stacks of fields or objects often carry shifted symplectic forms. A boundary condition
is represented by a Lagrangian morphism. The derived intersection of two Lagrangians has a shifted
Poisson structure and may quantize to the algebra of boundary-changing operators.
    Let L0 , L1 → M be Lagrangians in an n-shifted symplectic stack. Their derived intersection is
(n − 1)-shifted symplectic. Its (n − 1)-shifted symplectic form determines a Pn -structure. Whenever
an En -quantisation of that Pn -structure has been constructed and varies factorisably over a chiral
curve, the resulting observables form an En algebra in factorisation D-modules.
    This gives a geometric source of ordered chiral algebras when the ambient symplectic shift is n = 1,
so that the Lagrangian intersection is 0-shifted symplectic and its quantisation is associative. The
quantization and the factorization family are part of the theorem; the shifted symplectic form alone is
classical data.

41.11    A source-to-chiral comparison
Suppose the higher-dimensional factorisation pushforward constructs

                                      Ageom ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X)),

and a Hall, BPS, or localization construction independently produces

                                          Asrc ∈ AlgE1 (FactD(X)).

The comparison datum is an E1 -algebra morphism

                                             ϕ : Asrc −→ Ageom

compatible with the chiral coefficient. Applying the ordered bar gives the commutative square

                                                    ϕ
                                     Asrc                      Ageom

                                 Barord
                                    X                             Barord
                                                                     X
                                                                                                   (264)

                                Barord
                                   X (Asrc )               Barord
                                                              X (Ageom ).
                                                  Bar(ϕ)


The upper objects are associative and the lower objects are coassociative; the functorial bar map is
what relates the two types. The construction of Asrc contains the Hall or BPS correspondences, while
the construction of Ageom contains factorisation pushforward. Their equality is the theorem that ϕ is
an equivalence.


                                                    186
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   If ϕ is an equivalence on a chosen complete sector, then (264) identifies the corresponding Hall
or BPS resolution with the ordered chiral bar. A quantum-group identification still requires the
coproduct, pairing, R-matrix, and PBW data of Criterion 41.4.

41.12     The higher-dimensional conclusion
Higher-dimensional geometry enters ordered chiral Koszul duality through factorization pushforward,
boundary endomorphisms, and morphisms of deformation complexes. Cyclic traces produce surfaces;
Hall correspondences produce associative multiplication; Omega backgrounds produce deformation
parameters; shifted symplectic forms produce Poisson brackets. The chiral curve carries the spectral
dependence of the resulting operators.
   The language remains unchanged: an E1 algebra in factorization D-modules is the target through
which these sources can be compared while retaining their geometric origin.

42.1     The theorem of the construction
Theorem 42.1 (Noncommutative chiral Koszul geometry). Let X be a smooth complex algebraic
curve and let
                               A ∈ Algaug
                                       E1 FactD(X) .

Then the following statements hold in the indicated ambients.

  (i) The operadic Grothendieck construction for E1 ⊗ ChX presents A by ordered sequences of entire
      finite chiral configurations. Chiral collision occurs inside a block; the associative operadic maps
      compose adjacent blocks.

 (ii) The pointwise tensor and chiral convolution on the Ran category satisfy the duoidal interchange
      law. The E1 multiplication of A is a morphism of chiral factorisation coalgebras. Consequently
      the ordered bar is a coassociative coalgebra object in chiral factorisation coalgebras.

 (iii) The internal ordered bar is the derived interval tensor product

                                              Barord
                                                 X (A) ≃ 1 ⊗A 1.
                                                            L


       Its differential contracts adjacent transverse factors, while the complete chiral singularity remains
       in the coefficient D-module.

 (iv) On connected positive-weight objects and their complete Mittag–Leffler limits, internal associative
      bar and cobar reconstruct one another. The boundary Koszul dual A! = RHomA (1, 1) is the
      endomorphism algebra of the augmentation boundary, and A!! is the derived augmentation
      completion.

  (v) When A is admissibly positive, the universal twisting morphism identifies augmentation-complete
      A-modules with conilpotent comodules over C = BarordX (A). Its two-sided form sends the diagonal
      bimodule to C∆ , carries relative tensor products to derived cotensor products, and identifies
      Hochschild homology and cohomology with the corresponding coHochschild theories.


                                                    187
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


 (vi) The cardinality pro-nilpotence used by Francis–Gaitsgory is formulated in D(Ran X) with the
      chiral convolution product; the pointwise coefficient tensor of FactD(X) carries the independent
      transverse E1 structure. A conservative strong monoidal realization

                                  J : (FactD(X), ⊗pt ) −→ (D(Ran X), ⊗ch )

      whose image is closed under enhanced bar and cobar transports the internal constructions to
      the Ran constructions. In the chiral monoidal category, the enhanced theorem gives the reduced
      associative bar–cobar equivalence and, in the corresponding Lie case, the chiral Lie–commutative-
      coalgebra equivalence.

(vii) A cofibrant resolution of E1 ⊗ ChX is described by two-coloured levelled forests. Transverse edges
      encode associative homotopy, chiral edges encode collision homotopy, and the two edge-contraction
      differentials anticommute.

(viii) For a chiral coefficient admitting a meromorphic pole filtration along the collision divisors, the
       associated graded records every normal symbol of the singular product. On a formal disc, a
       translation-equivariant chiral coefficient becomes a vertex or nonlocal-vertex operation. In the
       standard chiral operadic model, its Maurer–Cartan equation is the integral Jacobi identity and
       hence the Borcherds identity.

 (ix) An E2 enhancement or a flat configuration-space connection supplies exchange. A rigid meromor-
      phic line category with a faithful strong monoidal fibre functor reconstructs a coquasitriangular
      bialgebra, and a Hopf algebra when the antipode exists. A faithful quasi-monoidal fibre functor
      reconstructs a coquasi-bialgebra, or a quasi-Hopf algebra under the corresponding rigidity hypothe-
      ses. A Yangian or quantum loop algebra is obtained after its presentation has been recognized by
      a separate theorem.

 (x) A square-zero BRST derivation extends to the ordered bar and forms a bicomplex with its
     differential. The comparison with the bar of BRST cohomology is the corresponding convergent
     spectral sequence; degree-zero Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction is recovered when BRST cohomology is
     concentrated.

 (xi) A transverse Calabi–Yau trace and a chiral modular sewing structure define two edge colours in
      a completed graph transform. Their structure element satisfies the two-edge master equation
      (174). Ribbon genus and chiral genus are independent gradings.

(xii) A perturbatively renormalized holomorphic–topological BV theory whose observables satisfy the
      hypotheses of Theorem 10.5 produces such an A. Its renormalized graph weights give a morphism
      from the mixed forest operad when they extend to compactified configurations and satisfy the
      quantum master equation.

Proof. Statement (i) is the block-ordered recognition theorem, Theorem 4.2. Statements (ii) and
(iii) follow from the duoidal Ran theorem and the interval-bar identification, Theorems 3.1, 3.3, 6.2
and 6.3. Statement (iv) is Theorems 7.2, 7.3, 7.5 and 7.6. Statement (v) is the Koszul–Morita
square of Theorems 8.1 to 8.3, 8.5 and 8.6. Statement (vi) combines the declared strong monoidal
realization with Theorems 7.4 and 17.2. Statement (vii) is the mixed Boardman–Vogt resolution,
Theorems 5.3 and 5.4. Statement (viii) follows from the normal-cone collision operad, the chiral-to-
classical symbol map, and the formal-disc selection theorem, Theorems 15.1, 15.2 and 16.1. Statement

                                                  188
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


(ix) is Theorem 20.2 together with the relevant representation-theoretic recognition theorem. Statement
(x) is Theorems 24.1 and 24.2. Statement (xi) is Theorems 26.4, 26.6 and 26.7. Statement (xii) is
Theorems 5.5 and 10.5. Every implication therefore factors through a construction proved in its native
monoidal category.

  The theorem obtains each construction from one object by a functor whose source, target, and
hypotheses are visible.

that is holomorphic in X and locally constant in R.
   This is the minimal language because it remembers exactly the two primitive experiments:

              collision on the complex curve,       ordered composition on the real line.

Removing the first loses the operator product. Removing the second loses noncommutative fusion.
Adding a second real direction produces exchange rather than altering either primitive operation.

42.4    The clarity obtained
The full OPE is the diagonal D-module with its complete pole filtration. Arnold’s relation is a relation
among configuration-space forms that may be paired with a coefficient identity. A Fulton–MacPherson
screen carries its intrinsic configuration-space cohomology, while the terminal corolla contracts the
separate incidence resolution. The internal E1 bar uses the pointwise coefficient tensor, and the
Francis–Gaitsgory chiral bar uses chiral convolution. Planar exchange or a flat configuration-space
connection braids the ordered line. A quantum group is a reconstruction of a tensor-compatible
exchange category. A constructed sewing action satisfies the modular master equation through its
codimension-two boundary identities. Curved Koszul data and projective conformal curvature occupy
their respective algebraic and moduli-theoretic deformation complexes. The derived centre is the
universal target of central bulk actions.
   Each correction strengthens the original intuition. The subject becomes unified by preserving the
outputs in their native categories and by deriving them from the same local object through exact
functors.

42.5    Mathematics and physics
Mathematically, the primitive datum (265) is the algebraic form of one holomorphic and one topological
direction. The chiral coefficient is controlled by diagonals and the Ran space. The ordered product
is controlled by intervals. Their common cofibrant resolution is controlled by two-coloured forests.
Koszul duality is the passage from an augmented algebra to the coalgebra of interval states and to the
endomorphisms of its augmentation boundary.
    Physically, the same datum is the protected local algebra of a holomorphic–topological system.
Chiral collisions are short-distance singularities. The E1 product is time- or normal-ordered composition.
The bar is interval evolution between boundary states. The E2 centre is the universal algebra of
central bulk operations. Braiding is exchange of lines in a transverse plane. Ribbon contraction is an
open loop. Deligne–Mumford contraction is worldsheet plumbing. The BV master equation asserts
compatibility of renormalized amplitudes with these boundary degenerations.
    The two descriptions coincide when a factorization-observable comparison has been constructed.
Before that comparison, they are parallel realizations of the same operadic geometry.

42.6    Structured invariants and their scalar traces
Corollary 42.2 (Structured completeness precedes scalarization). The complete invariant considered
here is the tuple consisting of the E1 multiplication, complete chiral coefficient, interval bar coalgebra,
exchange local system, and the two sewing theories. Ordinary and supertraces, Euler characteristics,
conformal-block ranks, Grothendieck classes, determinants, and numerical indices are functorial images
of this tuple. An equality between two scalar expressions is the image of a specified comparison or
trace.

Proof. The commutator records the antisymmetric part of multiplication. A trace records the cyclic
central contribution. A Grothendieck ring records decategorified fusion. A conformal-block rank


                                                   190
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


records the dimension of the state space. A central charge records the Virasoro extension. The
complete ordered chiral object is recovered from the full structured tuple rather than from any one of
these projections.

   The universal object is the noncommutative chiral algebra (265), together with the functorial bars,
centres, exchange structures, and sewing operations that exist for the structures actually supplied.




                                                 191
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Coda: the geometry remembers
A quantum field carries its collision law. On a curve, the law is a D-module operation supported on a
diagonal.
   An ordered product carries the orientation of an interval. Its algebraic form is an E1 multiplication.
   An exchange carries a path in a transverse configuration space. Its algebraic form is monodromy,
and braided Tannaka reconstruction gathers all matrix coefficients into a quantum symmetry algebra.
   A trace closes an interval into a circle. Its algebraic forms are Hochschild homology, cyclic homology,
and the self-cotensor of the diagonal bar state.
   Ribbon genus closes open topological boundaries. Chiral genus plumbs complex curves. The
bicoloured graph transform records both gradings and their commuting sewing operations.
   An anomaly is a class in the deformation theory of an invertible field theory. A determinant line,
connection, surface, and normalization evaluate that class numerically.
   Koszul duality resolves an operation into coalgebraic boundary states and identifies their endomor-
phisms at the augmentation boundary. On the Koszul–Morita locus, the boundary category and its
universal centre pass through the duality unchanged.
   The common syntax is factorisation. Disjoint regions multiply. Colliding regions produce singular
coefficients. Cutting an interval produces a derived tensor product. Gluing coalgebraic states produces
a derived cotensor product. Moving a defect produces monodromy. Closing a boundary produces a
trace. Degenerating a surface produces a master equation.
   The equations inherit the locality of their geometry. Associativity is the boundary identity of an
interval compactification. Chiral Jacobi is the boundary identity among partial diagonals. Yang–
Baxter is flatness on three-point configurations. The Batalin–Vilkovisky equation is Stokes’ theorem
for renormalised graph weights. The modular equation is the oriented cancellation of codimension-two
clutching strata.
   The subject has one invariant sentence and many exact realizations:

                                        A ∈ Algaug
                                               E1 FactD(X) .                                        (266)
                                                               


The diagonal supplies chirality. The interval supplies noncommutativity. The plane supplies exchange.
The circle supplies trace. The stable curve supplies sewing. The bar supplies resolution. The centre
supplies universal bulk action. The determinant line supplies anomaly. The BV complex supplies
quantum consistency.
   Each construction preserves the category of its input. Their comparison maps form the inner music
of chiral Koszul duality.


A     Orientation lines and the collision differential
The square-zero identities used throughout the paper are identities of oriented correspondences. This
appendix fixes one convention and proves the two cancellations on which the collision complex and the
corolla-normalized forest resolution rest. A coherent change of convention conjugates every differential
by diagonal signs and preserves the resulting complex.




                                                  192
R. Lorgat                                                                  Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


A.1      Cohomological suspension and unordered tensor products
Let (K, dK ) be a cochain complex. We write sK = K[1] in the cohomological convention

                      (sK)n = K n+1 ,           |sx| = |x| − 1,           dsK (sx) = −s(dK x).

For a finite set S, the unordered tensor product is

where a permutation acts by the Koszul symmetry. An ordering of S identifies (267) with an ordinary

   Let µ : g ⊗ g → g be a degree-zero graded Lie bracket. Its suspended form

                                                 µ̄ : sg ⊗ sg −→ sg

has cohomological degree +1 and is defined by

                                            µ̄(sx, sy) = (−1)|x| s[x, y].                                    (268)

With this convention, graded antisymmetry of [−, −] becomes graded symmetry of µ̄ with respect to
the shifted degrees:
                                    µ̄(u, v) = (−1)|u||v| µ̄(v, u).

             µ̄(µ̄(u, v), w) + (−1)|u|(|v|+|w|) µ̄(µ̄(v, w), u) + (−1)|w|(|u|+|v|) µ̄(µ̄(w, u), v) = 0.      (269)

The same formulas hold for a chiral Lie bracket after the tensor products are replaced by chiral
products and the outputs are pushed forward to the common small diagonal.

A.3    Rank-two intervals in the partition lattice
                        (1)
Every coefficient of (dcoll )2 is supported on a rank-two interval [π, σ] of Π(I). Such an interval has
exactly one of two forms: two disjoint pairs of blocks are merged, or three blocks are merged into one.

Proposition A.2 (Disjoint-pair cancellation). Let B, C, D, E be four distinct blocks of π. The two
composites that merge B with C and D with E in the two possible orders sum to zero.

Proof. The two coefficient operations act on disjoint factors. The transposition of the two odd collision
directions can be recorded either as the interchange sign of the two degree-one maps or as the reversal

                                          eBC ∧ eDE = −eDE ∧ eBC

of the determinant line. These are two presentations of the same transposition, two presentations of
one orientation transposition. In the total oriented convention the two paths induce opposite maps to
the common codimension-two component.

Proposition A.3 (Triple-collision coefficient). Let B, C, D be three blocks of π, and let σ be obtained
                                                                               (1)
by replacing them with B ∪ C ∪ D. The component Cπ (g) → Cσ (g) of (dcoll )2 is one half of the
suspended chiral Jacobiator.

Proof. The three intermediate partitions are obtained by first merging B, C, first merging C, D, or
first merging D, B. For a temporary ordering u, v, w of the suspended factors, the oriented sum is

               µ̄(µ̄(u, v), w) + (−1)|u|(|v|+|w|) µ̄(µ̄(v, w), u) + (−1)|w|(|u|+|v|) µ̄(µ̄(w, u), v),

which is (269). The factor 1/2 is the conventional conversion between the square of an odd coderivation
and one half of its graded commutator with itself.


                                                        194
R. Lorgat                                                               Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Theorem A.4 (Square of the collision differential). For a graded chiral antisymmetric operation µ
compatible with dg ,
                                                1
                                        d2coll = d[µ,µ] .
                                                2
Consequently d2coll = 0 if and only if µ satisfies the chiral Jacobi identity.

Proof. The internal square is zero and the mixed terms vanish by Theorem A.1. Disjoint mergers
cancel by Theorem A.2; triple mergers give the Jacobiator by Theorem A.3. Conversely, the component
from the discrete three-block partition to the one-block partition is precisely the Jacobiator.

A.4    Signs in the corolla-normalized forest resolution
For a nonempty block B, let K(B) = N∗ (N Tree(B)) be the normalized nerve complex from Section 14.
Its vertical differential ∂B is the alternating simplicial boundary and satisfies ∂B
                                                                                   2 = 0. The augmentation

and terminal-corolla section
                                                   ϵB      ιB
                                            K(B) −→     C −→  K(B)
are chain maps. For disjoint blocks B, C, set

                        mB,C = ιB∪C (ϵB ⊗ ϵC ) : K(B) ⊗ K(C) −→ K(B ∪ C).                                 (271)

The maps mB,C are symmetric and strictly associative, and commute with the vertical differentials.
  The total complex
                                                                    unord
                                  WIFM (g) =            Cπ (g) ⊗
                                                M                    O
                                                                            K(B)
                                               π∈Π(I)               B∈π

has vertical differential ∂ = B ∂B and horizontal differential obtained by tensoring dπ,ρ with mB,C .
                              P

The mixed commutator vanishes because (271) is a chain map. Strict associativity of the mB,C reduces
the horizontal square to the partition-lattice calculation above. Hence the total square is zero exactly
when the chiral Jacobi identity holds.
   This construction is intentionally different from an assertion that a single internal-edge contraction
merges disconnected roots. Vertical contractions resolve a rooted tree inside a fixed block. Horizontal
collision maps merge blocks through the partition differential and insert the terminal corolla of the
new block.

A.5    Residues and coorientations
Let Y be smooth and D1 , . . . , Dr a normal-crossing divisor. A local logarithmic form can be written
                                                                   
                                                            ^ dtj
                                     α=
                                               X
                                                                    ∧ αJ .
                                          J⊆{1,...,r}
                                                                t
                                                            j∈J j

The iterated residue along an ordered tuple is the corresponding coefficient. Therefore

                                      ResDi ResDj = − ResDj ResDi                                         (272)

when the residue maps include their conormal orientation lines. This concerns the logarithmic conormal
factors. Higher OPE poles are first paired with normal jets, as in Section 15, and then subjected to

                                                        195
R. Lorgat                                                              Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


the same residue orientation law.

A.6    Stable graphs
For a stable graph Γ, set or(Γ) = det RE(Γ) . A separating clutching inserts an edge joining two vertices;
a nonseparating clutching inserts a loop. Ordering the newly inserted edges gives the graph differential.
The determinant-line calculation proves that two successive node-forming operations anticommute.
The automorphism group of Γ acts on or(Γ), and the modular convolution complex takes coinvariants
with this twist.
   In a BV presentation, joining different vertices gives the bracket and joining two flags of one
vertex gives the loop operator ∆. With the standard modular-operad determinant convention, the
codimension-two relations give

                                d2 = 0,       d∆ + ∆d = 0,             ∆2 = 0,

together with the second-order identity expressing the bracket as the deviation of ∆ from being a
derivation. These equations are orientation identities of two node-forming parameters; their proof is
entirely the orientation calculus of the two node-forming parameters.


B     Low-arity calculations
Low arity is where every abstract object can be seen in its complete elementary form. This appendix
computes the collision complex in arities two, three, and four; identifies the Arnold and infinitesimal-
braid cancellations; and records the first examples of pole symbols, BRST descent, and modular
sewing.

B.1    Two labels
Let I = {1, 2}. The partition lattice has two elements,

                                                   1|2 < 12.

For a chiral Lie algebra g, the labelled collision complex is
                                                           µ̄
                                      ∆1|2,! (sg ⊠ sg) −→ ∆12,! (sg).                                    (273)

Since ∆1|2 is the identity of X 2 and ∆12 is the diagonal, the map is exactly the chiral bracket

                                           j∗ j ∗ (g ⊠ g) −→ ∆! g

written after suspension. The first quadratic compatibility condition appears in arity three. Antisym-
metry is the compatibility with the transposition of labels.
   On a formal disc, a translation-invariant binary chiral operation is represented by
                                                                 X λn
                                {aλ b} + : ab :,      {aλ b} =              a(n) b.
                                                                 n≥0
                                                                       n!



                                                     196
R. Lorgat                                                                    Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


The summand a(n) b is the coefficient of the normal principal part of order n + 1. Thus the arity-two
chain already contains the full singular OPE and the regular product.

Example B.1 (Heisenberg). For the rank-one Heisenberg chiral algebra with field J and

                                                                    k
                                              J(z)J(w) ∼                 ,
                                                                (z − w)2

the binary operation is concentrated in the second normal symbol. Its singular part is the second
                    ⊗2

the chiral operation.

Example B.2 (Affine currents). For a finite-dimensional Lie algebra g with invariant form (, ),

                                                         k(a, b)   J [a,b] (w)

                                                        (z − w)2    z−w

The first normal symbol is the Lie bracket and the second is the invariant form multiplied by the level.
They have different tensorial weights and satisfy different identities: Jacobi for the first, invariance for
the second.

B.2    Three labels and the Jacobiator
For I = {1, 2, 3}, the partition lattice is

                                        1|2|3 < {12|3, 13|2, 23|1} < 123.

Suppressing the diagonal pushforwards, the collision complex has the form
                                                    d             ⊕3 d2

                                                                                      
