   The columns record independent structures linked by the comparison maps constructed for each
model. The local pole controls the singular operation. Ordered transport requires modules and a
connection. Modular data requires sewing. A physical realization requires an action and boundary
condition. The exact models show that the same chiral operation can feed all four constructions while
preserving their distinct categories and operations.

G.8        What the examples establish
The examples test the common language by independent constructions whose comparison maps are
known.
   For Heisenberg theory, the boundary quantization map identifies the current OPE with the abelian
Chern–Simons boundary bracket, while Gaussian sewing identifies the oscillator trace with the
determinant formula.
   For affine theory, the Ward identities produce the KZ connection; Drinfeld–Kohno identifies its braid
category with the corresponding quantum-group category, and the Chern–Simons/WZW comparison
identifies the same braid data with Wilson-line exchange.
   For Virasoro theory, the fourth normal symbol gives the central cocycle, the Ward identity gives
the Schwarzian transformation, and the family index theorem identifies its moduli-space image with
determinant-line curvature and the Weyl anomaly.
   For ghost systems, the Clifford or Weyl quadratic relations determine the parity signs, the
corresponding determinant ratio gives the central charge, and the BRST square measures the same
total anomaly after the BRST comparison is made.
   For lattice theories, the group-algebra cocycle determines ordered phases, Poisson summation gives
the modular transformation of the theta series, and the compactified-boson path integral yields the
same theta factor after zero-mode and oscillator separation.

                                                      228
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


   Each agreement is mediated by an explicit construction. This is the standard of evidence appropriate
to chiral Koszul duality: one operation, calculated in several faithful realizations, with every passage
preserving the category of the object.


H     Object types, historical lineage, and the common language
A unifying language earns its force by preserving the native category of every object. This appendix
records the precise domains of the constructions used in the paper and the comparison maps that
connect them.

supplies the Francis–Gaitsgory Chevalley branch. The transverse associative multiplication is an
additional operation on the coefficient object. Together they form the ordered chiral algebra of (321).

   A comparison morphism between two rows states their relation. A quasi-isomorphism, monoidal
equivalence, or index map specifies the strength of that relation.

H.2    Bar, boundary dual, quadratic dual, Verdier dual, and centre
Five neighboring constructions occur.

Bar. For an augmented associative object A, the bar construction is a coalgebraic resolution governed
by twisting morphisms. Its definition uses augmentation, associative multiplication, and the chosen
completion.

Boundary Koszul dual. The universal dual algebra is

                                        A! = RHomA (1, 1).

Its double centralizer is the derived augmentation completion under the compactness and proxy-
smallness hypotheses stated in the text.




                                                230
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Quadratic dual. A presentation A = T (V )/(R) defines a quadratic coalgebra A¡ . The canonical
comparison
                                     A¡ −→ Bar(A)
is a quasi-isomorphism on the quadratic Koszul locus. PBW and curved versions replace the homoge-
neous presentation by filtered and quadratic-linear-constant data.

Verdier dual. Verdier duality is contravariant on sheaves and D-modules. It sends a factorisation
coalgebra to a factorisation algebra when duality commutes with the tensor products, diagonals, and
limits appearing in the factorisation structure.

Derived centre.     For an associative factorisation algebra A,

                                         der
                                        Zch  (A) = HH•Fact (A, A)

is the derived endomorphism algebra of the diagonal bimodule. For a chiral Lie algebra g, the typed
construction applies to U ch (g). The higher Deligne theorem gives the centre an E2 structure.
    The Koszul–Morita square relates these constructions on the finite, complete, and perfect locus.
The comparison maps carry the mathematical content.

H.3    Four squares and four curvatures
Four operators carry square identities in this subject.

  (i) A differential on a dg object satisfies d2 = 0.

 (ii) A curved A∞ algebra satisfies the curved Stasheff identities with curvature element m0 .

 (iii) A connection satisfies ∇2 = F∇ , where F∇ is a two-form on the base with values in endomor-
       phisms.

 (iv) A BV quantum differential squares to the anomaly operator, and the quantum master equation
      sets that anomaly class to zero.

   For a central scalar curvature element, the commutator [m0 , −] is zero while m0 remains in the
curved Maurer–Cartan equation. An exact BV anomaly is removed by a local counterterm. A
projective curvature is cancelled by tensoring with a line of opposite Atiyah class when that line
has been constructed. These mechanisms occupy distinct deformation complexes and meet through
explicit comparison maps.

H.4    Residue, principal part, and propagator
Residue. Poincaré residue extracts the coefficient of the logarithmic normal form dt/t.

                                                                      ⊗m
Principal part. The coefficient of a pole of order m is a section of ND/Y . The pole filtration and
its associated graded carry all singular modes of a chiral operation.




                                                  231
R. Lorgat                                                        Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Propagator. A propagator is a Green kernel or parametrix selected by a kinetic operator and a
gauge-fixing condition. Its singular part represents local principal-part data; its regular and harmonic
parts encode global geometry.
   The logarithmic form d log(z − w) generates the first cohomology of a two-point affine configuration
screen. The normal-symbol tower carries the full OPE. The propagator provides an analytic realization
of these local symbols in a chosen field theory.

H.5    Ordering, descent, and braiding
Labelled configurations carry pure-braid monodromy. Unlabelled configurations arise by descent along
the symmetric-group cover. Homotopy invariants and coinvariants agree for finite symmetric groups
over characteristic zero after averaging, while the labelled local system retains its braid representation.
   A Drinfeld associator is a group-like series satisfying pentagon and hexagon equations. It com-
pares parenthesized monodromies. The Grothendieck–Teichmüller group acts on the torsor of such
associators.
   Verdier duality sends a local system with monodromy ρ to the dual local system with monodromy
ρ−T . A chosen nondegenerate bilinear form may identify this with inverse monodromy. A Tannakian
or presentation-theoretic recognition theorem then identifies the resulting symmetry with a named
quantum group.

H.6    Modular sewing and the master equation
The compactification Mg,n has boundary divisors indexed by one-edge stable graphs and codimension-
two strata indexed by two-edge stable graphs. Their oriented incidence relation is the differential of
the Feynman transform. A cyclic object equipped with clutching-compatible contractions produces a
structure element satisfying
                                            1
                                      dΘ + [Θ, Θ] + ℏ∆Θ = 0.
                                            2
The clutching maps provide the coefficients; the boundary incidence provides the signs.
   For conformal blocks, the modular-functor hypotheses provide sewing and a projectively flat
connection. The first Chern class contains a Hodge term from the central charge, cotangent-line terms
from conformal weights, and boundary terms from factorisation channels. The complete modular
datum is therefore the bundle with connection, fusion decomposition, and sewing maps.

H.7    The mathematical history
The language assembled here arose through a sequence of exact advances.
Historical note H.1 (Distributions). Dirac’s delta function made an operation supported on a subman-
ifold calculable. Schwartz’s distribution theory made support and differentiation intrinsic. A chiral
bracket is the holomorphic D-module-valued descendant: the product is defined on the complement of
the diagonal and its singular commutator is supported on the diagonal.
Historical note H.2 (Operator products). Wilson separated short-distance singularity from long-
distance state dependence. The operator product expansion is a local asymptotic expansion whose
coefficients are local fields. Vertex algebra theory expresses this expansion by formal identities. Chiral
algebra theory replaces the chosen coordinate by a geometric correspondence.


                                                   232
R. Lorgat                                                      Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


Historical note H.3 (Borcherds and Beilinson–Drinfeld). Borcherds recognized locality, commutator
formulas, and associativity as coefficient forms of one formal-distribution identity. Beilinson and
Drinfeld placed that identity on a curve: operations are defined on configuration space and land on
diagonals. The formal variable becomes the normal coordinate of a collision.
Historical note H.4 (Fulton–MacPherson and Kontsevich). Fulton and MacPherson resolved simulta-
neous collisions by adding screens that record relative configurations. Kontsevich used compactified
configuration spaces to organize deformation quantization. Perturbative field theory uses the same
spaces to separate ultraviolet regions and encode the forest structure of counterterms.
Historical note H.5 (Ran space and chiral Koszul duality). The Ran space treats a finite subset of a
curve as one variable. Factorisation multiplies over disjoint unions. Francis and Gaitsgory proved the
Chevalley–primitive equivalence between chiral Lie algebras and conilpotent commutative factorisation
coalgebras in the pro-nilpotent chiral monoidal category.
Historical note H.6 (Braids and quantum groups). Knizhnik and Zamolodchikov constructed a flat
connection from current algebra. Kohno and Drinfeld identified its braid monodromy with the braided
tensor structure of quantum groups. Witten’s Chern–Simons theory produced the same representations
from Wilson-line amplitudes. Costello–Witten–Yamazaki obtained rational, trigonometric, and elliptic
integrable systems from four-dimensional gauge theory.
Historical note H.7 (Polyakov, Segal, and modular geometry). Polyakov’s path integral expressed the
conformal anomaly as curvature on the space of metrics. Segal formulated conformal field theory
through sewing of Riemann surfaces. Tsuchiya–Ueno–Yamada constructed conformal blocks in families.
Determinant and Hodge lines record the projective anomaly, while modular operads encode the
topology of sewing.
Historical note H.8 (Feynman and BV). Feynman’s graphs expand an action perturbatively. The
Batalin–Vilkovisky formalism expresses gauge invariance through classical and quantum master equa-
tions. Costello’s renormalisation theory constructs effective interactions on compactified configuration
spaces and places anomalies in local cohomology. A comparison of fields, pairings, and vertices
identifies a cyclic chiral graph equation with a BV theory.

H.8    The physical dictionary
Let Obs be a factorisation algebra of quantum observables whose protected boundary geometry is
X an × R. A quasi-isomorphism
                                                 ≃
                                         Φ : Aan −
                                                 → Obs∂                                  (322)
of E1 ⊗ ChX factorisation objects identifies the following structures.

measures the remaining physical data.

H.9    Exact data requirements
Separation theorem H.9 (Quantum-group recognition data). A quantum-group reconstruction
consists of a rigid braided or meromorphic tensor category, a tensor fibre functor, and matrix coefficients
satisfying a recognition theorem. The dimension of ker(V ⊗n → Symn V ) records a vector-space loss
under symmetrisation; the categorical data supply multiplication, coproduct, monodromy, and coherence.

Proof. The coend of a rigid braided category with a tensor fibre functor carries multiplication from
tensor product, coproduct from matrix coefficients, and the coquasitriangular form from braiding.
These maps depend on categorical composition and coherence. The displayed kernel is the fibre of a
linear projection and carries only its inherited vector-space structure. Thus the recognition data are
exactly the structures that construct the quantum group.

Separation theorem H.10 (Coefficient data for modular sewing). A modular master element
consists of cyclic contraction maps assigned to separating and nonseparating clutching strata, together
with their compatibility on codimension-two boundaries. The incidence relation ∂ 2 = 0 then proves the
master equation.

Proof. A cyclic contraction labels every internal edge of a stable graph. Pullback to a codimension-two
boundary composes two such contractions in the two possible orders. Equality of these pullbacks
identifies the two orientations of each codimension-two stratum. Their signed sum is zero, so the
induced convolution differential satisfies the modular master equation.

Separation theorem H.11 (Open–closed reconstruction data). The derived centre is terminal among
algebraic central actions on the boundary. A physical bulk is identified with it by a bulk-to-centre
quasi-isomorphism or by a generation theorem that produces such a quasi-isomorphism. The homotopy
fibre records bulk sectors with unit boundary action.

Proof. The Swiss-cheese action of bulk observables on boundary observables factors through the
universal central action, producing the bulk-to-centre map. A quasi-isomorphism identifies the two

                                                   234
R. Lorgat                                                          Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


local observable algebras. For a product bulk whose second factor acts through the unit, the reduced
observables of that factor map to zero and therefore lie in the homotopy fibre. This proves both
reconstruction and the stated residual datum.

Separation theorem H.12 (Classification data). Central charge determines the Virasoro central
extension and its Hodge coefficient. The complete chiral operation, representation category, braid
monodromy, and higher-genus blocks provide additional coordinates. Holomorphic theories at fixed
central charge display these independent coordinates concretely.

Proof. The central charge is the coefficient of the Virasoro central extension. The remaining structures
depend on the other OPE coefficients, module intertwiners, flat transport, and sewing maps. Even
lattices of a fixed rank give holomorphic chiral theories with the same central charge and different
lattice products and higher-genus theta series. Hence the listed coordinates refine the central-charge
fibre.

Separation theorem H.13 (Anomaly comparison data). Current level, Virasoro central charge,
determinant-line curvature, BRST anomaly, framing anomaly, and Borcherds-product weight live in
their respective deformation theories. A family theorem relates selected components by a morphism of
invertible theories, an index map, or a trace.

Proof. Each entry is a class in the deformation complex native to its geometric carrier: current
extensions, Virasoro extensions, determinant lines, BRST local functionals, framed invertible theories,
and automorphic line bundles. A morphism of invertible theories, a family index, or a trace induces the
corresponding map on these complexes and hence the stated relation among selected components.

H.11    The final principle
The mathematical phenomenon is the coexistence of holomorphic collision and transverse associative
composition. The physical phenomenon is the short-distance product of observables together with

                                                    235
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


their ordered fusion along a topological direction. The geometric carrier is the operadic product
of chiral collision spaces and oriented intervals. The algebraic laws are the chiral identities of the
coefficient, associativity of the E1 multiplication, and their interchange. Chiral Koszul duality produces
factorised coalgebraic states in the Ran direction; ordered bar–cobar duality produces interval states
in the transverse direction. Ordering records composition, planar transport records exchange, cyclicity
permits loops, moduli record sewing, and the derived centre records universal central action.
   Every noun has a category. Every verb is a functor, correspondence, contraction, or trace. This
typed syntax is the common language of the subject.


I     Historical lineage of the language
I.1   Distributions and local fields
Dirac’s delta distribution made support a mathematical datum rather than a limiting metaphor [27].
Quantum field theory inherited this lesson: products of fields are defined away from coincidence and
acquire contact terms on diagonals. The chiral D-module operation is the algebraic form of that
distributional fact. It records the singular product and its support before a coordinate expansion is
chosen.
   Wilson’s operator product expansion separated short-distance coefficients from local operators
[75]. Borcherds and the vertex-algebra tradition encoded the consistency of those coefficients in
formal distributions. Beilinson and Drinfeld replaced the formal coordinate by a curve and the formal
delta function by a morphism of D-modules supported on the diagonal. The present paper uses the
Beilinson–Drinfeld object as the chiral coefficient of a genuinely associative ordered product.

I.2   Time ordering and the associative direction
Feynman’s time-ordered product is an ordered associative multiplication [39]. It depends on an ordering
of insertion times, and its perturbative expansion is indexed by graphs. Stasheff’s associahedra later
supplied the homotopy geometry of all parenthesizations [68, 69]. Factorization homology identifies
the same geometry with the bar construction on an interval.
    The ordered chiral algebra combines these two historical lines. Dirac and Beilinson govern support
on the complex diagonal. Feynman and Stasheff govern composition along the ordered real direction.

I.3   Configuration spaces and renormalization
Arnold computed the cohomology of configuration spaces and isolated the three-term relation that un-
derlies the flatness of logarithmic connections [4]. Fulton and MacPherson compactified configurations
by replacing collisions with screens [40]. Axelrod–Singer and Kontsevich used related compactifications
to make perturbative gauge-theory and deformation-quantization integrals finite and compatible with
boundary strata [9, 53].
   The compactification has two roles that must remain distinct. Its face category resolves collision
incidence. Its screen spaces carry nontrivial cohomology and propagator integrals. The first is
combinatorial; the second contains the analytic weight of Feynman graphs.




                                                   236
R. Lorgat                                                     Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


I.4   Chiral algebras and Koszul duality
Koszul, Priddy, Quillen, and the operadic tradition developed the passage between algebraic operations
and coalgebraic resolutions. Beilinson–Drinfeld placed local Lie operations on the Ran space. Francis–
Gaitsgory proved the corresponding chiral Koszul duality in a pro-nilpotent monoidal category.
   The associative extension considered here uses the same Ran geometry with one additional ordered
operadic direction. Its bar is the interval bar internal to factorization D-modules. Its dual is the
derived endomorphism algebra of the augmentation boundary. This is the form of Koszul duality that
appears in defect physics.

I.5   Anomalies and geometry
Polyakov identified the Weyl anomaly with a local action for the conformal factor [65]. Quillen and the
family index theorem identified the same anomaly with curvature of a determinant line [62]. Belavin
and Knizhnik related determinant geometry to string measures on moduli [11]. These are different
realizations of a central extension of conformal transformations.
   The language of the present paper preserves the chain of maps: local stress-tensor cocycle, Ward
identity, Atiyah algebra of conformal blocks, determinant-line curvature. Every scalar coefficient
appears as the final trace of this chain.

I.6   Gauge theory, knots, and quantum groups
Witten’s Chern–Simons theory connected three-dimensional gauge fields, two-dimensional current
algebras, conformal blocks, braid groups, and knot invariants [74]. Drinfeld and Jimbo supplied the
quantum groups [29, 48]; Kohno identified their braid representations with KZ monodromy [51]. The
decisive structure is the category of lines and its exchange, and the Tannakian reconstruction from
that exchange category.
   Costello, Witten, and Yamazaki added a holomorphic spectral curve to topological gauge theory
[19, 20]. Wilson-line OPEs then produced rational, trigonometric, and elliptic quantum groups.
Gauge-theoretic constructions of Gaiotto, Moore, and Neitzke and of Nekrasov placed line defects,
framed BPS states, Omega backgrounds, and BPS/CFT Ward identities in the same protected sector
[41, 57]; Coulomb-branch convolution gives a complementary algebraic realization [10]. The spectral
curve carries chiral dependence; transverse topology carries fusion and exchange.

I.7   Factorization algebras and BV theory
Costello and Gwilliam formulated perturbative quantum field theory as a factorization algebra of
observables [14, 15]. The BV classical and quantum master equations became local cohomological
identities. Boundary and defect observables became factorization modules. This made the passage
from an action functional to a chiral or topological operator algebra a functor with a defined source
and target.
   The present construction is compatible with that formalism. An ordered chiral algebra is the
algebraic local observable structure on a holomorphic–topological product. A BV theory is one
realization of it, together with fields, pairing, integration, and renormalization.




                                                 237
R. Lorgat                                                       Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


I.8   Open–closed theories and centres
Hochschild cochains have long governed deformations of associative algebras. Deligne’s conjecture and
the Swiss-cheese operad identified their E2 structure and their universal action on an E1 algebra. In
open–closed field theory, the centre is the universal closed algebra acting on the open sector.
   This universal property is exact but limited. It reconstructs the protected bulk from a boundary
only when the bulk-to-centre map is an equivalence. Witten’s open-string field theory and Costello’s
Calabi–Yau categories supply settings in which Hochschild chains and cyclic operations are physical
closed states [76, 18]. Twisted holography supplies settings in which a boundary algebra determines a
protected bulk sector.

I.9   The resulting synthesis
The historical constructions meet in one typed sentence. A noncommutative chiral algebra is an
E1 algebra in factorization D-modules; its chiral coefficient is a diagonal-supported distribution, its
multiplication is ordered fusion, its bar is interval factorization homology, its centre is the universal
exchange algebra, its quantum group is Tannaka reconstruction of a braided line category, its ribbon
loops come from a cyclic trace, its worldsheet sewing comes from conformal blocks, and its anomalies
are invertible theories identified by transgression.
   Every clause has an independent historical origin. Their unity is the geometry of support, order,
exchange, and sewing.


J     Concordance with the extended source corpus
The extended source corpus contains calculations and constructions developed in several earlier
languages. The present paper assigns each construction a geometric carrier and records the map that
places it in the ordered chiral theory. The concordance below is part of the mathematics: it specifies
the object whose invariant is being calculated.

Proposition J.1 (Typed promotion of a calculation). A chain-level calculation from the source corpus
defines an invariant of the present theory when it is accompanied by a quasi-isomorphism from its
source complex to one of the carriers in (324), compatible with the relevant product, coproduct, module
action, or sewing maps. A numerical calculation defines a scalar invariant after composition with the
displayed trace τ .

Proof. A quasi-isomorphism transports cohomology together with every operation preserved by the
map. Compatibility with the structural maps identifies the transported cohomology as an invariant of
the target carrier. Composition with a trace produces a functorial scalar. These are the standard
invariance properties of derived functors and traces.

J.12    Arithmetic shadows and scalar projections
The arithmetic source material contributes established modular forms, theta series, Hecke operations,
determinant products, and period integrals. Section 37 attaches each scalar to its trace map. The
structured objects appear first; their arithmetic values appear after character, determinant, theta,
period, or index functors. The relation among two scalar values is therefore the image of a displayed
comparison morphism.
